--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page marks the beginning of the commentary on the "Chamatkara Chintamani." It follows the introductory flyleaf and establishes the author, the commentator, and the devotional framework of the astrological text. The numbering continues from the internal folio count of 51.</meta>

# Folio 51

->**Om! Salutations to the illustrious Ganesha.**<-

Having bowed to Ganesha, Shiva-Bhaskara <note>Original: śivabhāskaraṁ; likely referring to the Sun God in the form of Shiva, or Shiva and the Sun together as sources of light and wisdom</note>, and Ramachandra, and having offered prostrations, I now begin this **easy-to-understand commentary**. For the clear enlightenment of the wise, **Dharmeshwara** speaks on the *Chamatkara Chintamani* <term>Chamatkara Chintamani: Literally "The Wish-Fulfilling Gem of Wonders," a classic 14th-century Sanskrit text on predictive astrology</term>. || 1 ||

Now, the author **Narayana Acharya**, desiring to explain the *Chamatkara Chintamani* for the knowledge of the nature and results of the birth chart <term>Lagna-kundali: The natal chart or horoscope used in Hindu astrology to determine one's destiny</term>, performs an auspicious invocation in the form of remembering **Lord Krishna**. He does this to ensure the successful completion of the work without obstacles, while also instructing his students. This is the meaning.

At the beginning of this work, I, Narayana Acharya, bow with speech, mind, and body to the dark-complexioned **Shri Krishna**. He is adorned with glittering yellow garments and is embraced by **Radha**, appearing like a dark rain cloud illuminated by a flash of lightning. I shall now compose the work named *Chamatkara Chintamani* in the proper manner. || 1 ||

Again, he speaks of the aforementioned **Mukunda** <note>A name for Krishna meaning "The Giver of Liberation"</note> while performing the worship of Govinda.

<margin>51</margin>

->**Om! Salutations to the illustrious Ganapati.**<-

> Prostrating deeply to the dark-complexioned Lord Krishna, who wears glittering yellow silk garments and is joyfully embraced by Radha—like a dark cloud streaked with lightning—I, **Narayana**, shall now speak the *Chamatkara Chintamani*. || 1 ||

> I worship **Mukunda**, whose dark skin is the color of a rain cloud. He is filled with the tinkling sound of a multitude of tiny golden bells, wearing glittering yellow clothes as he moves about. Though he is unattainable even to the great meditators <term>Yogis: Ascetics who seek union with the divine through intense meditation</term>, he crawls in the courtyard of **Yashoda** <note>Krishna’s foster mother; the verse highlights the contrast between his divine infinity and his accessible, human childhood form</note>. || 2 ||

> Having churned the great ocean of the four hundred thousand verses of the **science of light** <term>Jyotish: The traditional Indian system of astrology and astronomy</term>, for the delight of the learned, I compose this work. It is grounded in supreme logic, elegantly concise in its wording, and written in the **snake-glide** meter <term>Bhujangaprayata: A poetic meter in Sanskrit characterized by a rhythmic, undulating flow resembling the movement of a cobra</term>. || 3 ||

The phrase "filled with the tinkling sound" refers to the noise made by the clusters of small bells <note>Original: kinkini-jala; small bells often worn by children on waistbands or anklets</note>. Similarly, "wearing yellow garments" describes the beautiful yellow cloth he wears. Though he is "unattainable to yogis"—meaning he is difficult to reach even through intense meditation—he is seen "moving" or walking in **Yashoda's courtyard**. I remember in my heart Shri Krishna, who wanders there. This is a poetic expression of his divine nature. || 2 ||

Now, the author describes the purpose of the book. "Great knowledge" is compared to an ocean. Having thoroughly reflected upon and "churned" the **four hundred thousand** verses of the science of astrology <note>This refers to the vast legendary body of Vedic astrological literature</note>, for the sake of the delight and benefit of the practitioners of astrology <term>Daivajnas: "Knowers of fate," the traditional term for astrologers</term>, he creates this excellent work. It is beautiful due to its logical arguments and the grace of its words, and it is concise rather than being overly long...

<summary>This page contains the opening invocations and the first three verses of Narayana Acharya's Chamatkara Chintamani, along with Dharmeshwara's commentary. The text establishes the work as a concise synthesis of a vast astrological tradition, framed by devotional praise of Lord Krishna.</summary>

<keywords>Narayana Acharya, Dharmeshwara, Chamatkara Chintamani, astrology, Jyotish, Krishna, Radha, Bhujangaprayata meter, birth chart, Hindu manuscript</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory commentary by Dharmeshwara on Narayana Acharya's "Chamatkara Chintamani." The text transitions from a discussion on poetic meter (Prosody) to the core astrological purpose of the book: the effects of planets in various houses. The left margin remains damaged, obscuring the start of several lines.</meta>

... <note>original: prajātairbhujangaprayātaṁ; text lost due to damage</note> produced, it is the **Snake’s Gait** meter <term>Bhujangaprayata: A poetic meter in Sanskrit consisting of four 'ya' ganas (short-long-long feet), resembling the slithering motion of a snake</term>, characterized by four units.
... <note>original: prayātaṁ ravyu chando-bhiḥ</note> with the meters dedicated to the **Sun**, I compose this work—the *Jewel of Wonders*—which is like a gemstone of amazing thoughts. 
... <note>original: pingaloktatvāt</note> because it was stated by **Pingala** <note>The ancient sage credited with the foundation of Sanskrit prosody</note>, there is a break in the meter in the verse...
... <note>original: jyotiṣa-bhūri-bhedam</note> the many divisions of **astrology**... or thus the first verse should be read.
... || 3 || Now, the author promises to declare the **results of the planetary houses**.

->**If the planets were not established in the zodiac wheel, what would be the use?**<-
->**And if they were not precisely calculated, what use would the kings of planets be?**<-
->**If the clarified houses are not established, what is the cause?**<-
->**I tell them all for the sake of the results for all living beings.**<-
->**Therefore, these [are explained]. || 4 ||**<-

The meaning is: What is to be considered of the results if the **planets** <term>Khecara: Literally "sky-goers," referring to the planets</term> are not established within the **zodiac wheel** <term>Rashimandala: The 360-degree circle of the zodiac</term>? Furthermore, even if the planets are established, if they are not **precisely calculated** <term>Spashta: Mathematically corrected planetary positions</term> through observational mathematics, what is the point of the "lords of the planets"? Without clarifying the **house positions** <term>Bhava: The twelve houses of a horoscope, representing different life areas</term>, even precise planetary positions are ineffective. Therefore, the precise positions of the planets and the various houses must be calculated. Now, the purpose for calculating the planetary houses is stated. Since all [destinies] of living beings are known through these results, I shall therefore speak and declare the **results of the planets and houses**. || 4 || <note>The sequence "raḥ raṁ raṁ..." appears to be a mnemonic or scribe's notation</note>

### || The Root Text ||

> || The **Sun** in the **first house** makes one's stature tall like a staff. The mind suffers because of the wife and the circle of kinsmen. The body is constantly afflicted by **wind** and **bile**. Such a person wanders and experiences the rise and fall [of fortune]. || 1 || 

Here, first, the author declares the results of the planets starting from the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna: The first house, also known as the 'Tanu-bhava' or house of the body</term> and the other houses. For one whom the **Sun** <note>original: Ravi</note> is in the first house, he makes the physical form tall. From the group of the wife and heirs (sons, etc.), the mind attains suffering. The body is pained by **bile combined with wind** <term>Vata-Pitta: Two of the three 'doshas' or humors in Ayurvedic medicine; their imbalance causes disease</term>. Furthermore, that person travels to foreign lands and constantly experiences **increase and decrease**—meaning the fluctuation of wealth. || 1 ||

Now, the result of the **Sun in the second house**: For a person whose birth chart shows the **Sun** in the house of wealth <term>Dhana-bhava: The second house, governing assets and speech</term>, that man will be exceptionally fortunate. He will obtain the happiness of **four-legged animals** <note>original: catuṣpāt; traditionally referring to wealth in the form of livestock like horses and elephants</note>. Furthermore, his own wealth is quickly spent; the meaning is that he may spend it on religious or virtuous causes. Due to his wife... <note>text ends mid-sentence</note>

<summary>This page concludes the introductory discussion on poetic meter and transitions into the primary astrological content, detailing the effects of the Sun in the first and second houses of a birth chart.</summary>
<keywords>Bhujangaprayata, Pingala, Sun (Ravi), First House (Lagna), Second House (Dhana-bhava), Vata-Pitta, Chamatkara Chintamani, Narayana Acharya</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary by Dharmeshwara on the effects of the Sun (Surya) as it moves through the twelve astrological houses. Having previously discussed the first house, the text now details the outcomes for the second, third, fourth, and fifth houses.</meta>

In the family, and especially concerning one's own relatives, a state of strife—meaning a quarrel—arises. Likewise, for the sake of profit, even skillful actions result in failure. This means that if any religious duty or work is performed with pride or ego, it becomes fruitless. || 2 ||

### The Results of the Sun in the Third House

If at the time of birth the <term>Jewel of the Day</term> <note>original: aharmant; a poetic name for the Sun, literally the "sky-gem"</note> is in the third house, the person possesses great majesty and demonstrates immense valor or courage. Furthermore, he may suffer distress or friction when meeting with his siblings. He will be a traveler of holy places, one who is inclined to go on pilgrimages. In the midst of battle, there is always the destruction of enemies, and he shall receive "well-being"—meaning prosperity or favor—from the king. || 3 ||

### The Results of the Fourth House

When the <term>Lord of the Day</term> <note>original: dinesho; another name for the Sun</note> is present in the fourth house (the house of happiness), the person is highly glorious and holds much authority. Due to this authority, wealth, and the recognition he receives, he faces some trouble or conflict from his relatives and kinsmen.

->|| He in whose second house the Sun resides is rich in fortune, but <-
->finds no thirst or joy in accumulating wealth. In the <-
->family, strife is born; actions performed for profit go <-
->to waste. || 2 || When the Jewel of the Day is in the third <-
->at the time of birth, one manifests great majesty and <-
->valor. Then, there is distress with siblings; <-
->he wanders to holy sites, destroys enemies in battle, and gains favor from the king. || 3 || <-
->In the fourth, the Lord of the Day makes a person glorious and <-
->authoritative, yet he faces conflict from relatives. A wanderer, <-
->facing a loss of honor in the battle against his rivals, his <-
->mind may never find peace. || 4 || Located in the house of the son, <-
->the Sun causes suffering regarding the first-born; he is skilled <-
->in mantras and sciences. He is negligent in speech, a hoarder, <-
->and his mind is prone to thoughts of abdominal disease. || 5 ||<-

From these relatives, he finds conflict or a state of being broken. Likewise, in a battle with rivals, he faces a loss of honor—meaning a great blow to his reputation. He becomes a wanderer, inclined to travel to foreign lands. Furthermore, his heart or mind may never find peace; the meaning is that he will always be anxious or unsettled. || 4 ||

### The Results of the House of the Son

When the <term>Maker of Light</term> <note>original: bhāskara; the Sun</note> is in the fifth house (the house of the son/children), the person suffers on account of his first-born child. This "suffering" refers to the death or loss of the first-born, which results in the sorrow of grief. He possesses "knowledge of mantras," meaning he is an expert in the sacred scriptures or a scholar of political science. He is negligent or careless, and a "hoarder"—meaning one who accumulates things. Furthermore, his intellect is as sharp as the <term>tip of Kusha grass</term> <note>kushāmagra; a common Sanskrit metaphor for "razor-sharp" intelligence, as the tip of the sacred Kusha grass is very pointed</note>, capable of discerning very subtle matters. He finds pleasure in deceiving others with his speech. His thoughts are often occupied by feelings arising from diseases of the belly or abdomen. || 5 ||

<summary>This page describes the astrological implications of the Sun's placement in the 2nd through 5th houses, detailing effects on family harmony, sibling relationships, travel, and the health of children.</summary>
<keywords>Astrology, Sun (Surya), Second House (Wealth/Family), Third House (Valor/Siblings), Fourth House (Happiness/Mother), Fifth House (Children/Intellect), Mantravidya, Pilgrimage</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the placement of the Sun in the astrological houses. The previous page concluded with the effects on the fifth house, mentioning illness and mental distress. This page moves into the descriptions for the sixth, seventh, eighth, and ninth houses.</meta>

...the distress arising from diseases and such is to be expected; this applies everywhere. || 5 || 

### The Results of the Sun in the Sixth House

Now, the results of the sixth house. He in whose sixth house—the <term>House of Enemies</term> <note>original: ripu-bhava</note>—the <term>Illuminator</term> <note>original: Bhaskara; a common name for the Sun</note> is placed, becomes a destroyer of his foes, one who slays his enemies. Likewise, there is an expenditure of wealth due to the King—meaning on account of state-imposed fines—or due to friends, for the sake of assisting them in their affairs. He causes the loss of riches. Furthermore, during travels or journeys, due to <term>forest-dwellers</term> <note>original: nishada; historically referring to indigenous forest tribes or hunters</note>... 

<margin>2</margin> ...and through despair, they cause sorrow. This means he may experience suffering on the road due to looting committed by thieves. In the maternal family—the mother’s lineage—as well as from four-legged animals such as dogs and others, there is a risk of misfortune. Alternatively, the meaning is that sorrow may arise from the maternal family, from fire, from a fall, or from the slaughter of cattle. In this particular commentary, the reading is "from the family." || 6 ||

### The Results of the Sun in the Seventh House

When the <term>Lord of the Journey</term> <note>original: ayanatha; a reference to the Sun's movement</note>, the Sun, is located in the seventh house at birth, that man’s beloved wife suffers from distress <note>original: tapana; literally "heating" or "burning," implying suffering or fever</note>. There is also <term>bodily pain</term> <note>original: pinda-pida</note> and anxiety. His mind becomes agitated regarding the acquisition of wealth and profit.

<gloss>(physical distress 5)</gloss>
->|| He whose Sun is in the sixth house becomes a destroyer of enemies, <-
->whether through the King or through friends. In the family, on the <-
->maternal side, or from four-legged creatures, or during travel, <-
->the forest-dwellers cause despair. || 6 || Now, when the Lord is in the seventh house <-
->at birth, there is distress for the man's beloved and bodily pain and anxiety. <-
->In the acquisition and sale of goods, the intellect is unsettled; even in <-
->competition, he never finds rest. || 7 || He who is <-
->intent on his deeds in the eighth house suffers hardship; when abroad, <-
->the King’s secrets or worthless objects might lead to <-
->robbery or loss of wealth... the Sun grants such a form. || 8 || <-

Regarding the collection of goods or in sales—the exchange of items for money—there is <gloss>unsettled</gloss> intellect, meaning the profit will be very small. Likewise, in competition or rivalry, an opponent causes obstacles, and the person "never finds sleep"—meaning they do not find peace. || 7 ||

### The Results of the Sun in the Eighth House

When the Sun is in the eighth house, it makes one <term>intent on deeds</term> <note>original: kriya-lampata; often implies being overly busy or even shrewd/deceitful in one's dealings</note>.

<margin>shrewd</margin>
Being extremely cunning, the Sun causes him to become a sufferer, one who experiences hardship. Likewise, he follows foreign women, or perhaps he becomes attached even to worthless things. Through <term>dacoits</term> <note>original: dasyu; bandits or robbers</note> or thieves, or through a lapse in judgment, he loses wealth. This leads to poverty and distress arising from <term>private ailments</term> <note>original: guhya-ja; literally "born of the secret parts," usually referring to hemorrhoids or reproductive health issues</note> or troubles involving women and the senses, creating a state of suffering. This is the connection. || 8 ||

### The Results of the Sun in the Ninth House

When the <term>Lord of the Day</term> <note>original: divana-natha</note> is in the ninth house—the house of fortune—a man is unrestrained and free from mental burdens. He reaches the "height of penance"—meaning his spiritual practices are of the highest order—and reaches "height through his actions," meaning he attains prominence and respect in his dealings. Even while being religious, he becomes highly respected by the common people. Likewise, with his siblings...

<vocab>षष्ठ भाव (sixth house), सप्तम भाव (seventh house), अष्टम भाव (eighth house), नवम भाव (ninth house), फलम् (result), भास्कर (Sun/Bhaskara), रिपु (enemy)</vocab>

<meta>This page continues the astrological commentary (Jyotisha) on the placement of the Sun in the various houses of a birth chart. While the previous page ended with the seventh house, this page begins by concluding the analysis of the ninth house before moving into the final three houses: the tenth, eleventh, and twelfth.</meta>

<note>The manuscript page contains yellow pigment highlights over certain words, likely added by a later scholar to emphasize key astrological predictions or corrections.</note>

The reason here is that he will be scorched by the third <note>likely referring to the 3rd house or a specific planetary aspect</note>. Because of the blow to the <term>trine</term> <note>original: kona; referring to the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, the cessation of worry—peace—does not reach his mind. Here, by the word "trine," one should understand the "triple-trine" is intended. || 9 ||

### Now, the Results of the Tenth House

He in whose <term>tenth house</term> <note>original: dashama-bhava; the house of career and public life</note> the <term>Radiant One</term> <note>original: Anshuman; a name for the Sun</note> is placed at birth, that man causes motherly suffering and distress to his mother through the "flow of radiance" <note>likely referring to heat or fever</note>; he expands upon this. Likewise, his <term>valor</term> <note>original: prabhava</note> is equal to that of a king. He is a settler of all matters, like a noble king, meaning he achieves all his goals. However, there is separation from his beloveds—his friends, children, wife, and others. From this, <term>exhaustion</term> <note>original: klama</note> and weariness may overcome him; this is the intended meaning. || 10 ||

### Now, the Results of the Eleventh House

When the Sun is in the <term>House of Gains</term> <note>original: labha-bhava; the 11th house</note>, that man receives wealth from the "King's Door" <note>the royal court</note>. Through the authority of the royal seal, he attains various kinds of prosperity, such as elephants and horses. Furthermore, his enemies fall into the "fire of his valor." As it is said in the *Vision of Authority*: "They shall be scorched." Likewise, he shall experience sorrow regarding his cattle and his offspring; this is how the connection should be made. || 11 ||

> The Lord of Day <note>original: Divanatha; the Sun</note> causes the mind to never find an end to worry due to the blow to the wicked trine. He is scorched by his brother through the actions of the "destroyer of the day" <note>an epithet for the Sun in a difficult position</note> which blocks his penance. || 9 || For him who has the Radiant One in the house of "the struggle" <note>the 10th house</note>, his labor brings success and is equal to a king's; yet he brings suffering to his mother, and exhaustion comes through separation from beloved ones. || 10 || When the Sun is in the House of Gain, one receives wealth from the King’s gate through the authority of the royal seal. Enemies fall into the fire of his valor, but women experience many kinds of sorrow from these husbands. || 11 || The Sun, in this vision, causes eye defects, and in times of disaster, the goddess of victory and fortune is born. One’s status is easily lost, and there is bodily suffering; illnesses of the bile and loss occur in the lower regions <note>original: adha-pradeshe; glossed below as "on the road"</note>. || 12 || Prosperity!

### Now, the Results of the Twelfth House

This Sun, having gone to the <term>twelfth house</term> <note>original: dvadasha-bhava; the house of loss and liberation</note>, causes eye defects, such as dim-sightedness, and "bile-accidents"—distress arising from a bilious constitution. Likewise, in the "shouting of disaster" <note>meaning the heat of battle</note>, the Goddess of Victory <note>original: Jaya-shri</note> appears. One's status or residence in a single place is "easily dissolved," meaning he wanders by his own will. In the "lower region"—meaning on the path or road—loss and damage occur. Also, bodily suffering "dissolves," meaning it is destroyed <note>or experienced</note> upon arriving on the path; this should be explained according to experience. In the phrase "bile-accidents in the lower regions," it means that loss occurs due to the aggravation of bile-related illnesses. || 12 ||

->Thus ends the results of the Sun in the houses, beginning with the Ascendant.<-

The previous page concluded the Sun's placement in the eleventh and twelfth houses. This page begins a new chapter focusing on the Moon’s placement across the twelve houses of the birth chart.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>

# Now, the Results of the Moon in the Twelve Houses Beginning with the Ascendant are Told.

The **Moon** <note>original: Vidhu; a poetic name for the Moon meaning "the solitary one"</note>, when situated in the signs of Leo, Taurus, Cancer, or Aries at the time of birth, and placed in the **Ascendant** <note>original: Vapu; literally "the body" or 1st house</note>, makes one the "superintendent of wealth"—a high financial official—full of joy and beauty, and endowed with merit in his delights. However, if the Moon is in the remaining signs <note>the other eight signs of the zodiac</note> in the first house, it makes the man devoid of righteousness, weak-bodied, lacking in vigor, impoverished, dull-witted, mute, or lacking in intelligence; it may even make him deaf. || 1 || When the **Moon** <note>original: Himanshu; "the cool-rayed one"</note> is in the **Second House** <note>original: Vasu-sthana; the house of wealth</note>, there is great physical comfort. There is the play and pleasure of women, and joy among one's family and kinsmen. He becomes extremely devoted to the King. Moreover, just by looking at such a man, even a celestial nymph would come under his influence, let alone an ordinary woman. The meaning is that he will be exceptionally handsome. || 2 || When the Moon is in the **Third House** <note>original: Vikrama; the house of prowess or valor</note>—

---

> || The Moon in Leo, Taurus, Cancer, or Aries <note>original: go-kulira-4-a-1-gah</note> while in the 1st house makes one a lord of wealth, full of beauty and joy. But in other signs, the Moon makes one unrighteous, weak-bodied, poor, dull, and deaf. || 1 ||
> When the Moon is in the House of Wealth, there is gain of grain, great physical comfort, and the play of women. One finds delight with family, and even a celestial nymph comes under his influence upon seeing him. || 2 ||
> When the Moon is in the House of Prowess, he gains great wealth; he becomes an ascetic, yet is delighted by beautiful women. Whatever he thinks of regarding his brothers, he obtains that happiness; he is brilliant in his majesty, a banner of righteousness. || 3 ||
> When it goes to the **Fourth House** <note>original: Bandhu-ga; the house of kin</note>, he is always delighted by his relatives and holds all authority at the King’s Gate. In his early age, he does not find such happiness, but later he is fully satisfied by his groups of children and women. || 4 ||

---

—in this third house of prowess, he attains great wealth. Even if he is delighted and enticed by a beautiful woman, he remains a "disciplined one" <note>original: tapasvi</note>. He becomes a "banner of righteousness" <note>original: dharma-dhvajitvena</note>, shining with the glory of his majesty. Whatever happiness he desires regarding his **siblings** <note>original: sahajam</note>, he obtains it. || 3 || When the Moon—the "one born of the sage Atri" <note>original: Atrijanma</note>—is in the **Fourth House**, the man finds happiness through his relatives. He finds complete satisfaction from his "groups of children and women" <note>meaning a large, happy family</note>. He is always an official in the **King's Gate** <note>original: nripa-dvara; the royal court</note>, managing all affairs. However, in his first age <note>youth</note>, he does not attain such authority; it is in his later years that he becomes highly prosperous and powerful. || 4 || For him in whose birth the "Lord of the Stars" <note>the Moon</note> is in the **Fifth House**, the Moon grants satisfaction through children, a pure intellect, and the gain of jewels; he shines like a lord of the earth. Likewise, through **lending** <note>original: kushidena; usury or banking</note> and long-term business, he finds success in his endeavors—

<summary>This page describes the astrological effects of the Moon's placement in the first five houses of a birth chart, detailing impacts on wealth, physical appearance, family life, and professional authority.</summary>

Therefore, there will be various and many gains. This completes the meaning. || 5 ||

When the **Moon** <note>original: Vidhu; a name for the moon</note> is in the **sixth house** <note>the house of enemies and illness; original: ripo-shatru-bhavane</note>, the person shines with the brilliance of majesty even through **royal disputes** <note>original: raja-vigrahena; conflicts with authority or war</note>. He shines with a beautiful luster. His enemies <note>original: arthah-shatravah</note>, even if conquered and defeated many times, may rise again before him. They lose their splendor in his presence, yet they reappear. Furthermore, regarding his mother, it is said that he possesses a certain character or disposition, but he may not be fully like her. || 6 ||

In the **seventh house**, the **Moon** <note>original: shitarashmi; "the one with cool rays"</note> brings pleasure from the company of women. From wealth and commerce, or through foreign trade, they grant victory and success. However, during the **dark fortnight** <note>original: krishnapaksha; the waning phase of the moon</note>, the person seeks pleasure with women and feels its power, but does not find the desired happiness. His mind becomes greedy for fine food, and his body becomes thin and weak. He may be overwhelmed by his rivals and defeated by his enemies. || 7 ||

Now, the results of the Moon in the **sixth house** <note>the text here provides a secondary commentary on the 6th/8th house themes</note>:
In his home, there is a gathering of **physicians** <note>original: bhaishaji bhishajam vaidyana; healers or doctors</note>. This is because his body is always plagued by illness.

> || When the **Lord of the Constellations** <note>original: nakshatranatho; the Moon</note> is in the **fifth house**, he gives satisfaction through children, a clear intellect, the acquisition of gems, and various riches gained through lending or effort. || 5 ||
> 
> || In the **sixth house**, the person shines even during **royal disputes**. Even if conquered, enemies may rise again. Before him, their power is lusterless. One has a character like their mother’s, yet not entirely so. || 6 ||
> 
> || In the **seventh**, the **Moon** brings wealth, perhaps even through trade. He seeks pleasure with women and is greedy for fine food, but during the **dark fortnight**, he is thin and overwhelmed by rivals. || 7 ||
> 
> || There is a gathering of **physicians** in his house; he is preoccupied with anxiety and the body's humors <note>original: udaka; literally "waters," referring here to bodily fluids or edema</note>. The Moon here causes great diseases, fears arising from water, and suffering caused by the mixing with wicked people. || 8 ||

When one is overwhelmed by fever and other ailments, many physicians will be present in his life to remedy them—this is the meaning. Sometimes, **great diseases** <note>original: mahavyadhayo; chronic or severe illnesses</note> like **tuberculosis** <note>original: rajaroga; "the royal disease," a traditional term for consumption or wasting diseases</note> arise from water-related causes. There are fears of drowning in water and bondages or sufferings caused by wicked people. The Moon thus becomes a cause of distress and difficulty for the person in whose chart this placement exists. || 8 ||

Now, the results of the **ninth house** <note>original: nava-bhava-phalam</note>:
The person in whose **house of penance** <note>original: tapobhava; the 9th house of luck, father, and spirituality</note> the Moon is situated becomes a source of inspiration. **Brahmins** <note>the priestly class</note>, **Kshatriyas** <note>the warrior/ruling class</note>, and **bards** <note>original: vandina; those who sing praises</note> will praise him and dwell upon his virtues. Because of his youth, wealth, character, and conduct, he is considered exceptionally fortunate.

<vocab>नक्षत्रनाथ (Nakshatranatha): Lord of the Constellations (the Moon), राजविग्रह (Rajavigraha): Royal dispute or war, सप्तमे (Saptame): In the seventh house, कृष्णपक्षे (Krishnapaksha): The dark fortnight/waning moon, भैषजी (Bhaishaji): Medicine or physicians, तपोभाव (Tapobhava): The house of penance/9th house, राजरोग (Rajaroga): Tuberculosis or severe wasting disease, ज्योतिष (Jyotisha): Astrology/Light-science</vocab>

...it certainly happens. Likewise, he is endowed with physical comfort and obtains wealth that was previously acquired. It is as beautiful as the **Moon** <note>original: chandra-vac-chobhanam</note>. Or, sometimes his honor increases and sometimes it decreases <note>literally "wanes," comparing his reputation to the phases of the moon</note>; this is the connection here. || 9 ||

**Now, the results of the Moon in the Tenth House** <note>original: Dashama Bhava; the house of career, status, and public life</note>. When the **Moon**—the one born of the ocean <note>original: Samudragaje or Abdhisute; titles for the Moon who, in Hindu mythology, emerged from the Churning of the Ocean</note>—is in the tenth house, which is like the zenith or heaven, the person performs religious acts and meritorious deeds <note>original: dharma-karma-punya</note>. He receives happiness from his relatives. Even among the people of the king <note>original: naresha; "lord of men"</note> and other high-ranking individuals, he attains a status like that found in the city of Indra <note>original: shakralyam; the king of the gods</note>. He attains **great popularity** <note>original: supriyatvam</note> and mastery over the splendor of young women. Regarding his **first-born child** <note>original: puro-jataka; "the one born before"</note>, it is said there may be less happiness, or alternatively, he finds only limited joy in his early progeny. || 10 ||

**Now, the results of the Moon in the Eleventh House** <note>original: Ekadasha Bhava; the house of gains and aspirations</note>. With the **Moon** situated in the house of gains, the person gradually attains authority over vows and high administrative positions <note>original: dhyakshatvam; "superintendency" or "directorship"</note> from the king or ruler.

---

> ->|| The Moon in the **ninth** house gives the person many children and prosperity.<-
> ->Indeed, he becomes very fortunate. In his youth, he possesses **happiness**<-
> ->and courage like the **Moon**. || 9 ||<-
> ->He finds happiness from relatives and high status, performing religious<-
> ->acts when the Moon, born of the ocean, is in the zenith.<-
> ->Even from kings, he finds favor. With young women,<-
> ->he attains **popularity** and great splendor, though for the first-born son<-
> ->he causes only limited happiness. || 10 || Through the Moon<-
> ->in the house of gains, he receives boons and authority from kings.<-
> ->Women and wealth circulate in his household.<-
> ->From various actions, a daughter and wealth are gained. || 11 || The **Moon**<-
> ->in the twelfth house causes anxiety regarding enemies and the eyes; one should always<-
> ->consider this. There is immediate sorrow through the loss of<-
> ->paternal uncles or the mother. He does not attain his<-
> ->desires and faces a lack of affection from his beloved. || 12 ||<-

---

This means that within the inner quarters of his home, women and wealth circulate or remain steady. Likewise, there are gains from various types of objects. Furthermore, from certain difficult or "cruel" actions, a daughter may be born. || 11 || 

**Now, the results of the Moon in the Twelfth House** <note>original: Dvadasha Bhava; the house of loss, expenses, and the subconscious</note>. If the Moon is in the twelfth sign, one must consider the anxiety arising from fear of enemies and disorders of the eyes. Similarly, there is a sudden lack of auspicious events <note>original: mangalona; "devoid of auspiciousness"</note> such as weddings. Regarding **expenditure** <note>original: dravya-tyaga; "letting go of wealth"</note> for the family: there is distress and sorrow caused by paternal uncles <note>original: pitrivya; the father's brother</note>, their wives, or their children, as well as from the mother's side of the family. There is a "lack of dearness" <note>original: apriyatvam</note> regarding the beloved, meaning a state of little satisfaction or a sense of detachment between them. Furthermore, he does not attain the desires <note>original: kamam; "wish" or "pleasure"</note> that his heart craves. || 12 ||

->|| Thus end the results of the Moon situated in the twelve houses. **May there be auspiciousness.** ||<-

### Now, the Results of the Houses starting from Mars in the Ascendant

When **Mars** <note>original: Kuja/Bhauma</note> is in the **Ascendant** <note>original: Vilagna; the 1st House, representing the self and physical body</note>: There is a danger of injury from rods or iron weapons <note>original: danda-loha-nibhatih</note>, and harm to the spouse or others. There is loss of wife and sons, and pain in the head or eyes. Even when actions reach their conclusion or result, there will always be obstacles or disturbances in every task; this is the intended meaning. Why is he like a **lion** <note>original: kesari</note>? It implies he possesses a fierce or wicked nature like a lion. || 1 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Second House

In whose **House of Wealth** <note>original: Dhana-bhava; the 2nd House</note> **Mars** <note>original: Angaraka</note> is placed: even if wealth is acquired and present, or in the context of the family and kinsmen, there is effectively no benefit. Just as a **monkey** <note>original: markata</note>, when someone places a necklace around its neck, simply protects it with its arms without knowing how to wear it properly, so too does this **miserly person** <note>original: kripana</note> guard his wealth. The meaning is that such wealth is never used for charity nor for the benefit of the family. Furthermore, in an argument, even if he is defeated or broken, he will face his opponent again to continue the dispute; thus, he becomes quite talkative or loquacious. <margin>This is the intent. || 2 ||</margin>

->॥ Regarding Mars ॥<-

> When **Mars** <note>original: Kuja</note> is in the **Ascendant**, one suffers from injuries by rods or iron, and the mind burns. Is he a **lion** <note>original: kesari</note>? Or is he like the second? There is harm to the spouse and others, pain in the head and eyes, and in the fulfillment of results, there is always an obstacle. || 1 || In whose **House of Wealth** **Mars** <note>original: Angaraka</note> resides, what is the use of that family wealth? Just as a **monkey** guards a necklace, so he guards his gains. Even when defeated in an argument, he turns back to face the opponent. || 2 || Where is the **valor of the arms** <note>original: bahu-virya</note> or the **fortune of the lineage** <note>original: kula-lakshmi</note> if **Mars** <note>original: Mangala</note> is not in the third house for men? There is distress from siblings <note>original: sahottha-vyatha</note>, and for them, how could there not be mockery regarding their penance and efforts? || 3 || When the **Son of the Earth** <note>original: Bhu-suta; a name for Mars</note> is in the **Fourth House** <note>original: Turya-bhava</note>, how can the planets be favorable to people? The happiness of the circle of friends is not even worth considering. Yet, one might receive land and clothes from a **ruler** <note>original: bhumipala</note> out of his grace. || 4 || Prosperity! ||

---

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Third House

Where is the **valor of the arms** <note>original: bahu-virya</note> or physical strength? Where is the **fortune of the lineage** <note>original: kula-lakshmi</note>, meaning wealth earned by one's own efforts? Who can describe the distress caused by siblings <note>original: bhratri-pida</note>? It means no one can. Furthermore, how can there be anything but public mockery of the penance or religious efforts of those men who do not have **Mars** <note>original: Mangala</note> in the third house? If Mars is in the "born-together" house <note>the 3rd house governs siblings</note>, those are the results. This is the underlying meaning. || 3 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Fourth House

When the **Son of the Earth** <note>original: Mars</note> is in the **Fourth House** <note>original: Turya-bhava; the house of home, mother, and land</note>, then what is the use of other planets being favorable or giving good results to people? It implies they are of no help. Therefore, the happiness derived from the circle of friends, relatives, home, or mother is nothing extraordinary. However, one may receive favor and compassion from a **ruler** <note>original: bhumipala; a king</note>, as well as gifts of clothing. One might obtain land, such as villages, through this grace. || 4 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Fifth House
In the **Fifth House** <note>original: Panchama-bhava; the house associated with intelligence, children, and past merit</note>, if **Mars** <note>original: Kuja</note> is present, the **digestive fire** <note>original: jatharagni; the internal heat responsible for digestion</note> becomes exceedingly strong and powerful. Even in matters of **sin or wickedness** <note>original: kilbishe pape</note>, the person possesses a great and abundant intelligence. However, they suffer in their own mind like **boiling milk** <note>original: dugdhavat-tapyate</note>; this means they are perpetually agitated. Furthermore, in this position, Mars alone kills or causes the death of the offspring that are born. || 5 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Sixth House
When **Mars** <note>original: Angaraka/Bhauma</note> is in the **Sixth House** <note>original: Shashtha-bhava; the house of enemies, debt, and disease</note>, the person's enemies—even if they are powerful and strong—are threatened and driven away by the person's own associates or ministers. They do not remain in the **field of battle** <note>original: sangare sangrame</note>; rather, they flee. The intelligent person who subdues them remains happy with his brothers and kin. However, wealth and property may dissolve or be destroyed, and one may encounter many obstacles. || 6 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Seventh House
In the **Seventh House** <note>original: Saptama-bhava; the house of marriage and partnerships</note>, **Mars**—born of passion and strength—repeatedly causes defeat and suffering to opponents and litigants through harassment and pain.

> || When Mars is in the fifth, the digestive fire is strong; he destroys the born
> offspring alone. At that time, there is much
> intelligence even in sin, yet the mind suffers like boiling
> milk; always. || 5 || In the sixth, the enemies do not
> remain if Mars is there; they are driven from the battle despite their power.
> The wise one is happy with his brothers, yet his
> wealth dissolves, though he may also gain much. || 6 || Because of 
> marriage, one obtains a journey through the world,
> not a return. Repeatedly causing defeat to opponents,
> Mars, born of passion, in the seventh destroys the couple. || 7 ||
> What can other auspicious planets do even in a good
> arrangement if the Son of the Earth (Mars) is in the eighth? A friend
> acts like an enemy even if truth is present or effort is made, O King,
> in the face of calamities. || 8 ||

Furthermore, he is a "destroyer of the couple," meaning he causes the death of the husband or wife. Specifically, if Mars is in the seventh house of a man's birth chart, it kills the wife; if it is in a girl's birth chart, it kills the husband. Additionally, because of the **marriage** <note>original: panigrahana</note>, one may travel or find themselves [unclear: occupied/attained] in business or important affairs, such that they do not return home for a long time. The implication is that out of a desire for dispute or greed for business, they may reside in a **foreign land** <note>original: videsha</note> for a long duration. || 7 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Eighth House
If Mars is in the **Eighth House** <note>original: Ashtama-bhava; the house of longevity and obstacles</note>, then even if other auspicious **planets** <note>original: khetayah</note> <gloss>planets</gloss> are present in his destiny, they are hindered. Because Mars gives wicked results here, he blocks the auspicious effects. Even a **friend** <note>original: sakha</note> acts like an enemy. Even when great effort is made, obstacles appear in the work that has been started. <margin>115</margin>

->|| This is the meaning. || 8 ||<-

<margin>Now, Mars in the 9th House</margin> Now, **Mars** <note>original: Mangala</note> situated in the **Ninth House** <note>original: Navama-bhava; the house of fortune, father, and religious merit</note> makes a man **fortunate in wealth** <note>original: bhagyavittam</note>, meaning one whose riches are gained through good fortune. It makes him **majestic and fiery** <note>original: mahojam</note>. Likewise, his **intellect** <note>original: matih</note> may be **extremely cruel** <note>original: ati-kruram</note>. He has no **brother-in-law** <note>original: shyalaka; specifically the wife’s brother</note>, and from where would a **brother** <note>original: sodara; a uterine brother</note> come? This means he lacks close male relatives of his own generation. Furthermore, the **reward of his valor** <note>original: vikrama-phalam</note> will manifest as personal gain at the **time of fruition** <note>original: vipake; the period when karmic results mature</note>. || 18 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Tenth House
If **Mars** <note>original: Mangala</note> is in the **middle house** <note>original: madhyama-bhava; a term for the 10th house, the peak of the chart</note>, what need is there for any other **auspicious signs** <note>original: mangalam</note> in his family? For in that house, Mars alone is the bringer of welfare <note>the author uses a pun: the planet's name 'Mangala' also means 'auspiciousness'</note>. This is an **overstatement for emphasis** <note>original: atishayokti</note>. Such a person is surrounded by **many people** <note>original: janair-bhuyaih</note>, meaning he will have many servants. Furthermore, that man is **self-accomplished** <note>original: svatah-siddha</note>, meaning he naturally succeeds in his undertakings. <margin>He is like a lion among people. 18</margin> <note>Note: The margin refers to the verse numbering, though the text transitions to house ten here.</note>

->With a very fierce mind and fortunate wealth, the majestic Mars<-
->occupies the house of penance. Whence would there be a brother-<-
->in-law or a brother? The result of his valor appears in the<-
->end. || 18 || What need is there for other auspicious signs in his<-
->family if Mars is in the middle house? He is surrounded by<-
->people. If he is there, does that poor man not<-
->become a second lion? || 20 || Mars in the house<-
->of gain afflicts children and enemies; he becomes<-
->foul-mouthed due to his majesty. Wealth increases<-
->through cattle or vehicles, yet a sense of emptiness<-
->from other houses occurs once. || 21 ||<-
->The brother is distressed and may be wounded by a<-
->stroke of iron; Mars destroys the wealth of<-
->those he knows. False rumors, fear from thieves,<-
->or quarrels and sorrow due to hunting should<-
->be considered. || 22 ||<-

Even if born in a humble family, he becomes like a **second lion** <note>original: dvitiyah kanthiravah</note>, meaning his prowess and power are equal to that of a lion. || 20 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Eleventh House
**Mars** <note>original: Kuja</note> situated in the **House of Gain** <note>original: Labha-bhava; the 11th house</note> may cause suffering to **offspring** <note>original: apatyani</note>, **enemies**, and **brothers**. He may be **foul-mouthed** <note>original: durmukha</note> or abusive, but due to his great **majesty** <note>original: pratapa</note>, his face is **impressive to behold** <note>original: sanmukha</note>. His **happiness and wealth** <note>original: saukhya-dhanam</note> increase through **cattle, horses, and vehicles** <note>original: go-ashvadi</note>. However, due to the influence of other houses, there may be a **singular instance of total loss** <note>original: shunya-pratha; a period of being 'zeroed out' or empty</note>. || 21 ||

### Now, the Results of Mars in the Twelfth House
**Mars** <note>original: Kuja</note> situated in the **Twelfth House** <note>original: Dvadasha-vyaya-bhava; the house of loss and expenditure</note> causes the **destruction of wealth** <note>original: artha-nasham</note>. Likewise, his **brother** <note>original: bhratri</note> may suffer a **wound from a stroke of iron** <note>original: loha-ghata</note> or a weapon. Such a man remains a **slayer of enemies** <note>original: ari-ghataka</note>. There is fear from **false rumors** <note>original: mithya-jana-shruti</note>, fear from **thieves** <note>original: dasyutah</note>, and frequent **quarrels** <note>original: kalaha</note>. Also, **sorrow caused by hunting** <note>original: mrigaya-janita-duhkham</note> should be considered <margin>for the twelfth house. || 22 ||</margin> This concludes the results for Mars in the twelfth house. || 22 || <margin>Thus ends the primary section on Mars.</margin>

<meta>This page transitions from the analysis of Mars (Mangala) to the planet Mercury (Budha). It begins a new section detailing the influence of Mercury as it occupies the first three houses of the natal chart, focusing on intellect, physical appearance, and professional skills.</meta>

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the First and Subsequent Houses

When **Mercury** <note>original: Budha; the planet of intellect and communication</note> is situated in the **Ascendant** <note>original: Murti-ga or Lagnastha; the first house representing the self and physical body</note>, he wipes away and destroys any **planetary afflictions** <note>original: rishta; misfortunes or negative omens</note> or defects arising from other houses. Such individuals possess **excellent intellects** <note>original: varishtha dhiyah</note> and **superior wisdom**. Their bodies appear like **divine gold** <note>original: divya-camikara; specifically refined or celestial gold</note>. They are **experts in medicine** <note>original: cikitsa-vidah</note> and skilled in the science of healing. They possess the **faculty of eloquent speech** <note>original: vaikhari-vritti; the most manifest form of sound/speech</note>, often employing it in professions related to writing and business. They are devoted to 

<margin>6</margin> **supporting their families** and kin. Alternatively, those who rely on this eloquent speech may find their own medical ailments difficult to cure.

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Second House

When Mercury is in the **Second House** <note>original: Dhana-bhava; the house of wealth and speech</note>, the person is truly **intelligent**. He possesses **radiance in his arms** <note>original: bahu-teja; often implying strength or heat</note>. When present in an **assembly of scholars** <note>original: sabha-sangatah</note>, he shines and appears like the great sage **Vyasa** <note>original: Vyasa-vat; referring to the legendary author of the Mahabharata</note>. This is the form he takes. In terms of enjoyment, he is like an **eight-legged bee** <note>original: astapado bhramarah</note> that gathers the essence of all worldly pleasures. Similarly, even with small means, he is characterized by **great generosity** and is **capable of giving much** <note>original: vahu-dana-shaktah</note>.

---

> When Mercury is in the **First House**, he wipes away other afflictions. Such people have **excellent minds** and **eloquent speech**. Their bodies are like **divine gold**, they are **learned in medicine**, yet their own illnesses are **hard to treat**. || 1 || In the **Second House**, the person is **intelligent**, with **radiant arms**, and shines in an **assembly** just like **Vyasa**. Like an **eight-legged bee** seeking enjoyment, he possesses the **generosity of a wish-fulfilling tree**. || 2 || With **excellent generosity**, his character is shaped by **restraint and virtue**, and through his **wisdom**, he gains **mastery over the uncontrollable**. Modest and seeking **pleasure like a clinging vine**, he may eventually **renounce his attachments** at a holy place with his younger brothers. || 3 || In the **Fourth House**, he is **most excellent**, yet may be a **difficult friend** to the twice-born. He is a **distinguished scribe** or writer, as noted by others in the **tenth or ninth houses**. || 4 ||

---

How is this described by the **learned scholars** <note>original: budhaih; a play on words, as 'Budha' is both the planet Mercury and a 'wise man'</note>? || 2 ||

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Third House

When Mercury is in the **Third House** <note>original: Tritiya-bhava; the house of siblings, courage, and efforts</note>, the individual practices a **profession of trade or commerce** <note>original: kraya-karinim vrittim</note> through **affection and friendship** with those who earn their living by various means. He is described as **modest** and **virtuous**, and through his own **intelligence**, he attains **mastery** <note>original: vashitvam</note> over those who are otherwise uncontrollable. Like a **clinging vine** <note>original: lata-vat</note> wrapping around a tree, he enjoys **many worldly pleasures** <note>original: bahu-vishayanubhavam</note>. There is another meaning: after enjoying these pleasures, he may **renounce them** <note>original: sannyaset</note>, meaning he leaves behind worldly objects. Alternatively, when surrounded by his **younger brothers** <note>original: sahajaih</note>, he pursues the enjoyment of his senses. || 3 ||

### Thus ends the results of Mercury in the Third House.

<summary>This page describes the astrological effects of Mercury in the first three houses, emphasizing its power to grant intelligence, golden physical beauty, medical skill, and the eloquence of the sage Vyasa.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exposition of the planet Mercury’s influence through the astrological houses, picking up from the Second House. It covers the Fourth, Fifth, Sixth, and the beginning of the Seventh House. The transition highlights Mercury's shift from personal intellect to social standing, family legacy, and conflicts.</meta>

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Fourth House

When **Mercury** <note>original: Chandraja; literally "the Moon-born"</note> moves through the **Fourth House** <note>original: Caturtha-bhava; the house representing home, mother, and happiness</note>, the individual becomes a charming friend and a leader among companions. They may become a **high-ranking official** <note>original: visheshadhikrita</note> at the royal gate or palace, possessing mastery over all types of learning. Such a person is often a skilled **scribe or writer** <note>original: lekhaka</note> and leads a virtuous life. 

It is also said that during the major period of this planet, one may hold an administrative position involving records. Some traditional teachings suggest that regarding **ancestral property** <note>original: paitrike; relating to the father</note>, the individual might not possess wealth earned by the father, or perhaps there is a lack of inheritance from the paternal line.

Thus ends the results for Mercury in the Fourth House.

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Fifth House

When Mercury, the **son of Rohini** <note>original: Rauhineya; a poetic name for Mercury</note>, is situated in the **Fifth House** <note>original: Panchama-bhava; the house of children, creativity, and intelligence</note>, a child may not be successfully born or remain in the early years of the individual’s life. However, this placement greatly increases **intellectual power** <note>original: medha-vriddhi</note> and the acquisition of wealth.

-> ॥ In the early years, a pregnancy may not be sustained ॥ <-
-> The producer of the sun-like light of intellect ॥ Mercury is described <-
-> in the Fifth house... <-
-> due to the influence of magic <note>original: abhichara; often referring to protective or aggressive rituals</note> ॥ 5 ॥ <-
-> Opposition of people, restraint of enemies, awakening of <-
-> ascetics, and the obstruction of the bodily winds <note>original: anilanam; referring to Vata or the airy humor in Ayurveda</note> ॥ <-
-> When Mercury is present, the management of treasures through strength <-
-> and the acquisition of wealth occurs in the house of enemies ॥ 6 ॥ <-
-> Praise to Lord Krishna ॥ <-

The individual is clever in the production of wealth. Experts in **horoscopy** <note>original: hora-vida</note> describe Mercury’s influence here as producing a great or immeasurable effect regarding protective rituals and the means of success.

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Sixth House

When Mercury is located in the **Sixth House** <note>original: Shashtha-bhava; the house of enemies, debts, and illness</note>, there may be **opposition from people** or quarrels. However, there is a **restraint of enemies**—a lack of cooperation that ironically leads to the establishment of one's own household. Regarding health, there is a balance of the **bodily winds** <note>original: anila</note>, though it may imply a "heavy abdomen." For **ascetics** <note>original: yati</note>, this placement brings **awakening or spiritual knowledge** <note>original: prabodha</note>. Furthermore, such a person becomes a manager of various forms of wealth and treasures; through their own capability and strength, they become an **acquirer of means** <note>original: arthakrit</note>.

Thus ends the results for the Sixth House.

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Seventh House

When the son of the Moon, Mercury, has risen and is joined in the **Seventh House** <note>original: Saptama-bhava; the house of marriage and partnerships</note>, he stands before a young woman...

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of Mercury (Budha), moving from the discussion of the Seventh House (begun on the previous page) into the Eighth, Ninth, and the beginning of the Tenth House. The focus shifts from physical constitution and vitality to longevity, religious merit, and professional standing.</meta>

In pleasure and at the time of sexual union, Mercury <insert>in the Seventh House</insert> bestows a seed and vitality that is weak or lasts only a short time <note>original: *svalpa-kāle*; implying a lack of sexual stamina</note>. Similarly, no one—not even a dear friend or loved one—is able to imitate or equal the golden <note>original: *hemavat*</note> beauty of the person's body or the splendor of their physical form and wealth. This is the concluding thought: their luster or fortune shall be most excellent. However, the implication is that if Mercury is unassociated <note>original: *asaṅgato*; not joined or aspected by other planets</note>, it renders all these results incomplete or defective. ॥ 7 ॥

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Eighth House

When the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: *Rajaputra* or *Chandra-suta*; a common title for the planet Mercury</note> is situated in the **Eighth House** <note>original: *Randhra-bhava*; the house associated with death, longevity, and hidden things</note>, the individual lives for a hundred years. They are widely heard of and famous both here in their own country and in foreign lands. From the king, they obtain legal success <note>original: *vyavahāra*</note> and the discovery of hidden treasures or the accumulation of wealth. They find great joy and happiness in the pleasures and play arising from the company of young women. ॥ 8 ॥

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Ninth House

> When the Son of the Cold-Rayed One <note>original: *Śītagoh*; the Moon</note> is in the **Seventh House**, he causes a person to have weak vitality in pleasure. When unassociated, one cannot imitate that person's golden body-splendor or wealth. ॥ 7 ॥ Those with the Prince <note>Mercury</note> in the **Eighth House** live for a hundred years and become famous in foreign lands; they gain treasure from royal dealings and take pleasure in sporting with young women. ॥ 8 ॥ When Mercury goes to the **House of Dharma** <note>the Ninth House</note>, one becomes righteous and very wise, an initiate <note>term: *Dikshita*; one who has undergone a formal hallowing or consecration</note> or one who has bathed in the River of Heaven <note>the Ganges</note>. Such a person illuminates their family like the sun, possesses more majesty than a king, and is an obstacle to the foul-mouthed. ॥ 9 ॥

When Mercury is in the **Ninth House** <note>original: *Dharma-bhava*; the house of religion, philosophy, and luck</note>, the person becomes very fortunate, righteous, and a consecrated **performer of the Soma sacrifice** <note>original: *Somayaji*</note>. They are devoted to the vow of bathing in the River of Heaven <note>original: *Svadhuni-snataka*; referring to the holy Ganges</note>. They are the "illuminator of the lineage," brightening their family name just as the sun lights up the world. In terms of power and majesty <note>original: *pratāpa*</note>, they are superior even to a protector of the earth <note>a king</note>. They become a punisher or a source of obstruction to the wicked or the foul-mouthed. ॥ 9 ॥

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Tenth House

When Mercury is in the **Tenth House** <note>original: *Karma-bhava*; the house of profession, actions, and public status</note>, the individual is particularly distinguished by the wealth of their father. They are to be honored and respected by the public, being masters of policy and justice...

# The Auspicious Laukoshahala-Kutuhala

<gloss>To the Auspicious Five [Gurus]</gloss>
> Salutations to the Guru, who has revealed that state which pervades the entire universe of moving and unmoving things, and whose form is an unbroken circle. ॥ 1 ॥
> The Guru is Brahma, the Guru is Vishnu, the Guru is the Great Lord Shiva. The Guru is truly the Supreme Absolute. Salutations to that Guru. ॥ 2 ॥

...from the [Tenth] House. Through the grace <note>original: *prasāda*; favor or divine gift</note> and influence of the planets, one receives a transformative power. Under a good reign or in a state of prosperity, such a person is inclined towards legal affairs <note>original: *vyavahāra*; can also mean commerce or social conduct</note>. He speaks in a measured way <note>original: *mitaṃ saṃvadet*; with moderation</note>, rather than being talkative or boastful. He speaks with limits, and subsequently obtains divine garments and other luxuries. ॥ 10 ॥ Now, the results of Mercury in the Eleventh House. When the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: *Tārāpati-putraṃ*; literally "the son of the lord of the stars"</note> is situated in the **House of Gains** <note>Eleventh House</note>, there is no lack of profit, such as wealth and physical beauty. He possesses a heart-stealing attractiveness and a life free from the threat of sudden death. He has the means to perform the marriage ceremonies of his daughters <note>original: *kanyakodvaha*; a significant social and religious duty</note>. From where and how does this wealth come? Through the favor of Brahmins <note>original: *bhūsura*; "gods of the earth"</note> and others, he becomes one whose desires are fulfilled by the abundance of wealth given to him. Indeed, when Mercury is in the eleventh, all these fruits of gain and prosperity manifest. ॥ 11 ॥ Auspiciousness.

->॥ He speaks measuredly; he does not obtain things without measure. Through favor, etc.<-
->one receives horses and elephants from the King. When Mercury is in the house of action <note>the Tenth House</note>,<-
->he is particularly worthy of honor, possessing his father's wealth and the authority<-
->to administer justice and punishment. ॥ 10 ॥ Without the Lord of the Stars being in the House of Gains,<-
->there is no profit, nor beauty, nor attractiveness.<-
->From where would come the ability to give a daughter in marriage?<-
->How would the Brahmins be satisfied? ॥ 11 ॥<-
->If the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note> is in the Twelfth House,<-
->how would the Brahmins <note>original: *bhūmi-devā*; "gods of the earth"</note> honor him?<-
->Why would enemies in battle feel fear? From where would a deceptive person<-
->obtain a treasury of gold? He would not. ॥ 12 ॥<-

Now, the results of Mercury in the **Twelfth House**. For one who has the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: *Śītāṃśu-janma*; "born of the cold-rayed one"</note> in the twelfth, how do the Brahmins approach or serve him? Why would enemies in a conflict or battle feel fear toward him? What deceptive person would enjoy a treasury of gold or other riches? From where would he find the means to spend his accumulated wealth? The meaning is that these positive results would not occur for such a person. ॥ 12 ॥

### ॥ Thus ends the results of Mercury in the twelve houses starting from the Ascendant ॥

### Now are told the results of the twelve houses for Jupiter, beginning with the body.

When **Jupiter** <note>original: *Devapūjye Gurau*; literally "the Teacher [of the gods] who is worshiped by the divine"</note> is in the **Ascendant** <note>original: *Lagna*; the first house, representing the physical body and self</note>, the person is well-dressed, abundant in beauty with fine garments and ornaments, and possesses a divine and handsome body. He is happy and free from sorrow, though he is a man of little virility <note>original: *alpa-vīrya*; can refer to physical strength or potency</note> and low energy. Furthermore, through scholarship and by pleasing the public, he gains weightiness <note>original: *gurutva*; refers to both physical gravity and social status/influence</note> and great respect. His mind will be focused on the future and the afterlife, specifically on residing in heaven. His objects and wealth are spent on provisions and food. ॥ 1 ॥

Now, the results of Jupiter in the **Second House**. When Jupiter is in the House of Family <note>original: *kuṭumba*; the second house, relating to wealth and relatives</note>, there is an inclination toward poetry and the composition of literature. He possesses the power of leadership and punishment <note>original: *daṇḍa-netṛtva*; authority in legal or administrative matters</note> and the capacity to exercise royal authority. He may be afflicted by faults in the mouth <note>original: *mukhe doṣa*; could refer to speech impediments or dental issues</note> or be a person with a diseased face. Alternatively, his enjoyments may come to a swift end, or his lineage may end due to his low virility. Acquisition of wealth occurs with great effort and difficulty. ॥

->॥ When Jupiter, the one worshiped by the gods, is in the Ascendant, one is well-dressed,<-
->happy, has a divine body, but low virility. The mind is focused<-
->on the future and the afterlife; objects and wealth<-
->are spent on provisions. ॥ 1 ॥ There is poetic talent,<-
->the power of leadership and justice, but faults in the mouth,<-
->and a swift end to pleasures. When Jupiter is in the House of Family, wealth<-
->is obtained through hardship; wealth does not rest/remain stable<-
->despite constant effort. ॥ 2 ॥ He whose Jupiter is in the Third House<-
->is the smallest of the small. To his younger brothers,<-
->he is a benefactor. He may be ungrateful; though he has friendship with<-
->his circle of friends, wealth like a father’s is not gained by luck<-
->even when meeting kings. From the gate of his house is heard<-
->the neighing of horses and the sound of the Vedas recited by<-
->the twice-born. Rivals perform service to him when<-
->Jupiter is in the Fourth. ॥ 4 ॥<-

Even with constant effort, wealth does not "rest"—that is, it does not achieve stability or stay in one place. ॥ 2 ॥

Now, the results of Jupiter in the **Third House**. He for whom the Minister of the Gods <note>original: *Devamantrī*; another name for Jupiter</note> is in the House of Siblings <note>original: *duścikyaga*; the third house</note>, that man is the lowliest among the lowly <note>original: *laghūnāṃ laghīyān*; very small or insignificant among peers</note>. However, he is a benefactor who brings happiness and welfare to his brothers. He may be ungrateful. Furthermore, although he has friendship with his circle of well-wishers, even with a rise in luck or when coming into the company of kings, he does not obtain wealth inherited like a father’s. ॥ 3 ॥

Now, the results of Jupiter in the **Fourth House**. When Jupiter is in the fourth, even the sound of the Vedas <note>original: *veda-ghoṣa*; the rhythmic chanting of sacred texts</note> recited or studied by the twice-born <note>original: *dvija*; members of the priestly, warrior, or merchant classes</note> is heard there like a great sound. In the same way...

...whatever is heard as a word uttered by the tongue. <margin>6</margin> One’s own personal duties are fulfilled, and even enemies increase or thrive. Nevertheless, the inner heart <note>original: *antargatamanas*</note> becomes distressed; the sense is that this occurs due to a lack of contentment. ॥ 4 ॥

**Now, the results of Jupiter in the Fifth House.** When the Divine Teacher <note>original: *Devapūjye*; a common epithet for Jupiter as the priest of the gods</note> is in the fifth house of intelligence, there is a mental inclination toward luxury and enjoyment. He is a talkative speaker <note>original: *jalpako vaktā*</note>. He is a clever writer <note>original: *kalpakalādi ko lekhakaḥ*</note> with fine penmanship. However, at the time when results should ripen, there is an obstacle or calamity regarding the "fruit" <note>original: *phalopadrava*; may refer to agricultural harvests or the metaphorical fruits of one's labor</note>. Despite this, if children are present to help, there is a specific result: wealth and prosperity <note>original: *bhūti-dhana-samṛddhir*</note> will eventually flourish, just as a plant matures in its final stage. ॥ 5 ॥ **Now, the results of Jupiter situated in the Sixth House.** When Jupiter, the giver of contentment, is in the sixth house of enemies <note>original: *ripau*</note>, the person—even if afflicted by disease—attains the power to slay his foes. He is forcefully exalted in battle; no one can become his conqueror or cause his defeat. He is a knower of all states of enjoyment, possessing buffaloes and other cattle. He performs endless deeds for his maternal uncles...

He has a powerful abundance of wealth and prosperity. He has a great love and pleasure <note>original: *rati-prīti*</note> for beautiful women. He is proud and full of self-esteem, superior among his kinsmen <note>original: *sapiṇḍa*</note>, and full of strength and beauty. In all matters, he becomes like the God of Love <note>original: *Kandarpa*</note>. ॥ 7 ॥ **Now, the results of Jupiter situated in the Eighth House.** A man for whom Jupiter is in the house of death <note>original: *mṛtyugo*</note> may reside in a house for a very long time. Upon the end of the body, he goes to the world of Vishnu <note>original: *Viṣṇu-loka-gāmī*</note>. However, it is also said that even if he has a house, it is not long-lasting; he does not stay in one place for long, or he does not live a long life. Likewise, his limbs may be free of disease <note>original: *nīroga*</note> for a long time. ॥ 8 ॥ **The results of Jupiter in the Ninth House.** When Jupiter is in the ninth house of piety <note>original: *dharmage*</note>, there is the power of a landlord or king. However, due to arrogance or laziness, he may possess great knowledge but be idle. If his piety is deficient, he becomes a "taker" or "omitter" of the daily obligatory rituals <note>original: *sandhyāvandanādi nitya-karma*</note>. ॥ 9 ॥ **Now, the Tenth House...**

# Results of Jupiter's Placement

..The phrase "In the pavilion of the flag" <note>original: *dhvajāmaṇḍape*</note> refers to a place of assembly or a hall of paintings <note>original: *citraśālaṃ*</note> where one stands before the people, adorned with banners. It is a house filled with various artworks and a temple for the master of the home. There is a greater splendor and power <note>original: *pratāpa*</note> than even that of one's ancestors. It should be understood that this brilliance arises from one’s own inherent light. Thus, through the peace brought to his children, their bodies and prosperity will be beautiful. Every day, one cooks a <term>prastha</term> <note>a traditional unit of dry volume</note> or sixteen <term>palas</term> <note>a unit of weight, roughly 1.5 to 2 ounces each</note> of sea salt. This salt is not for a small ritual offering, but rather, it implies that in his great kitchen, he cooks and feeds many people daily. This result pertains to the chapter on **Jupiter in the Tenth House**. Alternatively, the phrase "In the pavilion of the flag" is a reading for "Jupiter in the house of action" <note>the 10th house is the *Karma Bhava* or house of profession</note>. ॥ 10 ॥ **Now, the results of Jupiter in the Eleventh House.** When Jupiter is in the house of gain <note>original: *lābhage*</note>, what can even be said? One obtains gold, silver, and all that was possessed by his father. Others—even famous kings like Indra—praise him, as he is a master of grammar and logic...

->..Surrounded by four walls, the earth-lord’s assembly<-
->is loved by the earthly gods <note>original: *bhūmi devāḥ*; refers to Brahmanas</note>. When Jupiter is in the **House of Dharma**,<-
->the relatives are humble; he is always a performer of righteous deeds.<-
->॥ 9 ॥ In the **pavilion of the flag**, the house is a hall of paintings;<-
->possessing wealth, his glory exceeds that of his ancestors. ॥ 10 ॥<-
->Not content with just work, for the sake of his children<-
->he cooks a measure of sea salt every day. ॥ 10 ॥ When gold and<-
->**non-rusting wealth** <note>original: *akuṣyaṃ*; refers to gold/silver</note> are gained through Jupiter, what more<-
->is there to say? Sages and wise men speak of this. He bears the **burden of his father**;<-
->five sons, elephants, and horses are his, yet this wealth is for the<-
->sake of others, not for his own worldly enjoyment. ॥ 11 ॥<-
->What is his **fame** like? It exists alongside pride. What is his<-
->intellect? It is used for the deception of others. What is his<-
->use of wealth? It is the **destruction of wealth**, by which<-
->three things are lost: his lineage, his wealth, and his goal. ॥ 12 ॥<-

...great and wealthy men say this. He bears the burden of his father; he has his own horses and elephants, and five sons are born to him. However, his wealth is for the sake of others <note>original: *parārtha*</note> and not for his own grandeur, or for the purpose of charity, religion, or personal enjoyment. The sense is that there may be a degree of miserliness <note>original: *kārpaṇyaṃ*</note>. Another reading for "bearing the father's burden" is "attaining the father's heaven." ॥ 11 ॥ **Now, the results of Jupiter in the Twelfth House.** For one who has **Jupiter in the twelfth** <note>the house of loss and liberation</note>, even if he has pride and some immediate success, what kind of fame is it? It is only ill-fame. His intellect is inclined toward the deception of others. But he is like a Kubera <note>the god of wealth</note> in reverse. By this, the **highest purpose of wealth** <note>spiritual merit</note> is destroyed; he suffers the loss of his worldly goods in vain. What sort of destiny is this? It is the result of his own actions. These three things—fear of relatives, deception of others, and the abandonment of wealth through improper means—will come to pass. ॥ 12 ॥ **Thus ends the results of Jupiter in the various houses from the Chamatkara Chintamani.** ॥ 12 ॥ **May there be auspiciousness.**

<meta>Continuity Note: This page marks a transition from the results of Jupiter to the results of Venus. The previous page concluded the effects of Jupiter in the 10th, 11th, and 12th houses. # Now, the results of Venus in the twelve houses, beginning with the Ascendant, are told. ॥

When the planet **Venus** <note>original: *śukra*</note> is well-placed in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the first house, representing the self and physical body</note>, the person possesses a beautiful body. Their associations are noble—they keep the company of virtuous people and those endowed with qualities such as beauty and cleverness. They are joined with the enjoyment of **excellent women** <note>original: *varāṅganānāṃ*</note>. Their actions, such as sacrifices and charity, are performed well. Whether in this world or the next, such a person becomes the enjoyer of the fruits of their good deeds, which take the form of sensual and material pleasures. ॥ 1 ॥

## Now, the results of Venus in the Second House ॥

For one whose Venus is situated in the **house of family** <note>original: *kuṭumba-sthito*, the 2nd house</note> or the house of wealth, and who is worshipped by the "First God" <note>likely referring to the ancestors or the primary deity of the family</note>, their face is pleasing and their speech is beautiful. Their **intellect** <note>original: *manīṣā*</note> is also elegant and skillful. They are devoted to righteousness or possess various types of beautiful worldly pleasures. Their **garments** <note>original: *vāsāṃsi*</note> are

> || A beautiful body, noble company,
> and union with the enjoyment of excellent women;
> one of good deeds, good comfort, and good consumption,
> occurs when Venus is in the Ascendant. ॥ 1 ॥
> A pleasing face, beautiful speech, and an elegant intellect;
> happiness, beauty, and beautiful garments belong to him.
> When situated in the family house, he is worshipped by the
> First God; his family is great, his voice is lovely,
> and he is filled with desire. ॥ 2 ॥
<margin>(of the long...)</margin>
> He takes pleasure in women; for him, there is no
> destruction of kin; Venus in the third house <note>original: *duścikyā*</note>
> brings gifts and vehicles.
> Yet, he is not fulfilled even in the happiness of sons;
> he is an army commander who is timid
> in times of charity or combat. ॥ 3 ॥
> Great and penetrating is he for whom the Teacher of the Demons <note>Venus</note>
> is in the fourth house; what need has he for people,
> whether they be his own kin or others who are faultless?
> Is there any obstacle?

of many beautiful varieties. Through their family and their own people, they find great splendor, though they remain thoughtful. There is a desire for women with beautiful bodies <note>original: *cārvāṅgī*</note>. This result should be applied as follows. ॥ 2 ॥

## Now, the results of Venus in the Third House ॥

For one whose **Teacher of the Demons** <note>original: *guruḥ*, here referring to Venus as the preceptor of the Asuras</note> has gone to the **third house** <term>duścikyā</term>, there is a delight in women. This means a love for them. There is also no destruction of one's kin—but what of that happiness? Furthermore, the person's desires regarding the happiness of having sons are not fully realized. Even if he is an **army commander** <note>original: *senāpati*</note>, he becomes timid or hesitant in times of giving or in the heat of **combat** <note>original: *saṃgrāma*</note>. This should be understood to apply everywhere. ॥ 3 ॥

## Now, the results of Venus in the Fourth House ॥

For one who has the Teacher of the Demons, **Bhṛgu** <note>another name for Venus</note>, in the **fourth house** <note>original: *turya-bhāve*</note>, they become superior in terms of women, wealth, and respectability. Through faultless people, whether they are their own kin or even strangers...
