<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of the planet Venus (Shukra) as it moves through the houses of the birth chart. It concludes the section on the Fourth House (related to the mother and domestic happiness) and proceeds through the Fifth House (children and intellect), the Sixth House (enemies and obstacles), and the Seventh House (marriage and partnerships).</meta>

...What is the use of being born to those who are dependent, even if they are provided for by others or offered gifts? From the moment of birth, how can one describe the immeasurable **maternal happiness** <note>original: *mātuḥ... sukhaṃ*</note> and great nourishment that should be provided? ॥ 4 ॥

### Now, the results of Venus in the Fifth House ॥

If **Venus** <note>the planet of beauty and desire</note> is auspiciously placed in the **Fifth House** <term>Panchama Bhava</term> <note>the house associated with children, intelligence, and past merits</note>, what is the point of even seven sons if they do not bring the proper fruits of birth? This means that if wealth is not earned through effort, then that labor is merely exhaustion. What is the use of water without the specific "higher fruit" such as sovereignty? This refers to the **contentment of the mind** <note>original: *manas-santoṣa*</note>. Through the "mantra" of a poet, or by the repetition of a mantra <note>sacred chants</note>, or through moderate food, one gains the intellect to compose poetry. If one does not have this through study, what is the use? Therefore, for one desiring the birth of a son, sovereignty is achieved through the repetition of mantras, which acts as the best "food" to satisfy the fire of greed. The ability to compose poetry comes from study.

> || He who, from birth, is full of wealth offered by those 
> dependent on him... ॥ 4 ॥
> Even if there are seven sons, if Venus is there, 
> what is the use if the son does not achieve wealth 
> through effort? 
> Like a mantra without its power, what is the use 
> of study if there is no capacity for poetry? ॥ 5 ॥
> For the **Preceptor of the Demons** <note>original: *dānavajye*, an epithet for Venus/Shukra</note>, 
> a tree sprinkled with nectar may wither in the 
> House of Enemies. One should be the best. 
> That which is earned might be destroyed; 
> one might suffer from bad mantras. 
> Happiness comes from the worshipful, not from wealth. ॥ 6 ॥
> A wife, and happiness from a wife; yet no happiness 
> from a wife if the wife is in the loins. 
> She becomes pregnant with a child. 
> One is considered highly fond of pleasures, 
> a traveler, with little effort—how is he the chief? ॥ 7 ॥

This is the meaning. ॥ 5 ॥

### Now, the results of Venus in the Sixth House ॥

When the **Preceptor of the Demons** <note>Venus</note> is in the **Sixth House** <term>Shashtha Bhava</term> <note>the house of enemies, debts, and diseases</note>, it is like a tree sprinkled with nectar or the drink of the immortals; it is irresistible. One’s own expenditure or the giving away of wealth becomes "excellent." By one's own nature, through virtuous actions, one becomes superior. This means wealth will arise even from those designated as enemies. And yet, that which is accomplished or "earned" might be destroyed or lost. Through a "bad mantra," one might experience distress. Happiness comes from those who are worthy of worship, or from children, etc., but not necessarily from wealth alone. ॥ 6 ॥

### Now, the results of Venus in the Seventh House ॥

When Venus is in the **Seventh House** <term>Saptama Bhava</term> <note>the house of marriage and partnerships</note>, there is immeasurable happiness from a **wife** <note>original: *kalatra*</note>. This happiness comes from the "region of the wife," though there may be pain in the hips. According to the **Amarakosha** <note>a famous 4th-century Sanskrit lexicon</note>, the term <term>kalatra</term> refers to both "the loins/hips" and "a wife." In the wife, there will be a "jewel of a womb," meaning she will be pregnant with an excellent son. Such a person is "highly fond of pleasures" <note>original: *vilāsādhiko*</note>, meaning they are very playful. They are a **traveler** <note>original: *pravāsī*</note>, prone to going to foreign lands. Their "effort" is said to be very slight, yet they enjoy life. Thus, from what man or for what reason...

### Now, the results of Venus in the Eighth House ॥

If at the moment of birth **Venus** <note>original: *daityapūjyaḥ*, the one worshipped by the demons</note> is in the **Eighth House** <term>Aṣṭama Bhava</term> <note>traditionally the house of longevity, secrets, and obstacles</note>, the native exerts effort regarding cows, horses, and livestock. Likewise, in matters such as gambling <note>original: *dyūta*</note>, the person does not lead a long or beautiful life, nor do they live happily. Victory and wealth are only achieved through conflict <note>original: *kalaha*</note>, and are not easily gained through steady accumulation. Regarding the quality of wealth, if it is given to a worthy person, it is preserved; if given to the unworthy, it is lost or destroyed. ॥ 8 ॥ 

### Now, the results of Venus in the Ninth House ॥

When **Venus** <note>Bhrigu</note> is in the **Ninth House** <term>Navama Bhava</term> <note>the house of fortune, dharma, and father</note>, which is one of the three "trine" positions, who are the citizens in that person's city? Those who live there do not provide growth or livelihood through usury <note>lending money at interest</note>. This means that in that city, there are no people remaining except for those who are debtors to him. Furthermore, his house is characterized by the "Flag of Righteousness" <note>original: *dharmadhvaja*</note>, which implies a sense of hypocrisy or public display of piety. Comfort is gained through brothers and attendants, and physical health is maintained. The *Amarakosha* <note>a famous Sanskrit dictionary</note> defines the "Flag-bearer of Dharma" as one who makes a living through religious symbols <note>original: *liṅgavṛtti*</note>, specifically those who wear the marks of a religious order for the sake of profit. ॥ 9 ॥

->**॥ At birth, Venus provides a long and beautiful life and happiness in matters like gambling. ॥**<-

### Now, the results of Venus in the Tenth House ॥

When **Venus** is in the **Tenth House** <term>Dashama Bhava</term> <note>the house of career and public action</note>, does he not block the "lineage-seed" or the seed of offspring? The native's own wandering takes the form of an expansion of ethics or policy. <margin>॥ Sha ॥</margin> What of the destruction of wealth? Daily "Brahmin-slaying" <note>original: *viprahatyā*; here used metaphorically to mean the sin of depriving a priest of his livelihood</note> occurs because of public displays and disputes over gold. Whether it is gold weighed on a scale or a hundred *palas* <note>a traditional unit of weight</note> of gold, it results in the same fruit. This is the decision regarding the great lineage of Janaka <note>a reference to King Janaka, implying a noble or royal standard of judgment</note>. ॥ 190 ॥ <note>The numbering jumps here, possibly a scribal error or a reference to a larger verse count</note>

### Now, the results of Venus in the Eleventh House ॥

For one who has **Venus** in the **Eleventh House** from the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term>, which is the house of gain <term>Labha Bhava</term>, the planet bestows profit and creates an influx of wealth. The native possesses a virtuous character...

...adorned with an attachment to truth, fame, and the act of pleasing others in the afterlife. It makes one a leader of men, a king who rules excellently. From the strength of these positions, the following results occur in the form of this verse: ॥ 11 ॥

### Now, the results of Venus in the Twelfth House

For one who has **Venus** <note>original: *sitaḥ śukro*, the "Bright" or "White" planet</note> in the **Twelfth House** <term>Dvādaśa Bhava</term> <note>the house of expenditures, liberation, and the subconscious</note>, there are good deeds, religious duties, play, and sacrifices. There is happiness and a reputation for many virtues, yet also the destruction of wealth. This placement always creates opposition and quarrels with friends and kin. Sometimes wealth comes—meaning the person attains riches—but then that wealth dissolves or vanishes. It also signifies an "excess of phlegm" <note>original: *kaphādhikya*, referring to the Kapha Dosha in Ayurvedic medicine</note>. Some manuscript readings say this happens "daily." ॥ 12 ॥

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Twelve Houses, starting with the Body

When **Saturn** <note>original: *arkaje*, the son of the Sun</note> is in the **First House** <term>Tanu-sthāna</term> <note>the house of the self and physical body</note>, then with wealth...

---

-> ॥ One who follows the path of the Ascendant and is endowed with servants, [this position] grants gains and makes one a true king. He is full of excellent fame, love of truth, and many virtues; he is a person of great enjoyment, endowed with lordship and a good character. ॥ 11 ॥ Sometimes wealth comes and then it dissolves; Venus in the Twelfth brings play, good deeds, and comfort. The fame of his virtues declines; he always creates opposition and enmity among friends and people. ॥ 12 ॥ Though fully filled with wealth, he is thereafter dejected; when the son of the Sun (Saturn) is in the First House, a man has poor vision. Because of "unequal vision" or through anger, his intellect is obstructed; he is personally pained and wears the guise of envy. ॥ 1 ॥ Seeking happiness, he is abandoned by his family; what object does a person with Saturn [in the second] not consume? ...though full [of wealth], he is daily lacking in satisfaction, dejected, and unhappy. He has "poor vision," meaning he is incapable of subtle discernment or deep thought. The "poison born of vision" refers to being a destroyer of enemies through his gaze. He creates mental suffering <note>original: *ādhi*</note>, or it refers to an obstruction of the eyes. Then there is pain and the "guise of envy" <note>original: *matsaraveśa*</note>. Being unable to tolerate the excellence or progress of others, he remains personally pained and agitated. ॥ 1 ॥

<margin>Saturn</margin> ### Now, the results of Saturn in the Second House

When **Saturn** is in the **Second House** <term>Dvitīya Bhava</term> <note>the house of wealth and family</note>, that man—out of a desire for happiness or a wish for a fragment of comfort—is separated from the family he has earned and reaches a foreign country. What object does he not enjoy? Rather, he becomes a consumer of all sensory experiences. Likewise, even without constant application or service, he deals in manufactured iron, weapons, or metals like gold and other precious materials.

...Therefore, in the human wheel <note>the zodiac or cycle of life</note>, what does one obtain and what should one do? To friends, he speaks words that are truthful, yet sharp and difficult to endure. ॥ 2 ॥ 

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Third House

When **Saturn** is in the **Third House** <term>Tṛtīya Bhāva</term> <note>the house of siblings, courage, and communication</note>, the person's mind never becomes truly cool or peaceful despite their efforts and power. **Good fortune** and gains are never steady or without interruption at any time. He is a **measured speaker** <term>mitavaktā</term> <note>original: *yukta-bhāṣī*, one who speaks only what is necessary</note>. From this, even if he is honored, he may become sullen or loud-mouthed. ॥ 3 ॥ 

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Fourth House

When **Saturn** is in the **Fourth House** <term>Caturtha Bhāva</term> <note>the house of home, mother, and happiness</note>, the "treasure chest" of paternal wealth and the family home are destroyed or moved far away. There is **reproach from kin** <note>original: *bandhu-nindā*</note>. One suffers the loss of houses and vehicles. He brings distress to both his father and his mother. Furthermore, it is said he will be a **sufferer of wind-related ailments** <note>original: *vāta-rogī*, referring to an imbalance of the Vata Dosha in Ayurvedic medicine</note>. ॥ 4 ॥

> Without an attachment to a friend, who would accept even a bitter word? ॥ 2 ॥ When Saturn is in the third, the mind is never calm, though the person is industrious and a measured speaker. Fortune is never without interruption; though firm in hope, he may be foul-mouthed even when honored. ॥ 3 ॥ When Saturn is in the fourth, the treasure, wealth, and home go far away; there is reproach from the circle of kinsmen. He causes distress to the father, mother, and step-mother; there is loss of house and vehicle, and he suffers from wind-ailments. ॥ 4 ॥ In the fifth, Saturn causes sorrow on account of children, even if there is prosperity; his intellect is not faithful. He has a liking for the deity and the scriptures of words <note>grammar or philosophy</note>, yet there is conflict with friends and pain in the belly from mantras. ॥ 5 ॥ O King, what fear of thieves or enemies is there for one who has the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> in the house of enemies? No warrior can stand before him in battle; he has happiness through buffaloes and such, but destruction comes to the maternal uncles. ॥ 6 ॥

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Fifth House

When **Saturn** is in the **Fifth House** <term>Pañcama Bhāva</term> <note>the house of children, intelligence, and creativity</note>, the person suffers sorrow on account of their offspring. While there may be a gain of wealth and prosperity, the intellect is **unsteady or crooked** <note>original: *na śruddhā*, meaning lacking in simple faith or directness</note>. There is a love and trust <note>original: *rati*</note> for the divine and for the **Codes of Law** <term>Dharma-śāstra</term> because they represent the Vedas. However, there is conflict and quarreling with friends. Finally, he suffers from **pain in the gut** <note>original: *kukṣi-kleśa*, abdominal distress</note>. ॥ 5 ॥ 

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Sixth House

If for anyone the **Son of the Sun** <note>original: *ina-sya putraḥ*, an epithet for Saturn</note> is in the **Sixth House** <term>Ṣaṣṭha Bhāva</term>, which is the House of Enemies, what fear has he of enemies or thieves? In fact, he has none. In the "argument of battle," no opponent or warrior can stand before him. An alternative reading suggests he is simply the **conqueror in battle**. He finds happiness through four-legged animals such as buffaloes and the like. However, the destruction of his maternal uncles may occur. ॥ 6 ॥ **Auspiciousness.**

<meta>This page continues the astrological exposition of Saturn's influence as it moves through the houses of a birth chart. Having previously discussed the first through sixth houses, the text now addresses Saturn’s placement in the Seventh, Eighth, Ninth, and Tenth houses, shifting from personal character to matters of marriage, longevity, spirituality, and career.</meta>

# Now, the Results of Saturn in the Seventh House

When **Saturn** is situated in the **Seventh House** <term>Saptama Bhava</term> <note>the house governing marriage, partnerships, and external relations</note>, the individual may have **good spouses** <note>original: *sudārāḥ*</note>, meaning beautiful or noble partners, and **pleasant friends** <note>original: *cāru mitraṃ*</note> who are well-wishers. However, the **fine wealth** <note>original: *cāru vittaṃ*</note> they possess is often acquired through means devoid of justice; this wealth may not endure for a long time, meaning true happiness from it is fleeting. Additionally, it is implied that both members of the **couple** may be **afflicted by disease**. 

Furthermore, such a person is characterized by a **lack of enthusiasm** <note>original: *anutsāhaḥ*</note>; they are constantly uninspired in their various undertakings. They are **low-minded** <note>original: *hīnacetā*</note>, possessing a diminished or discouraged mental state. They are **ineffective** <note>original: *vikalaḥ*</note> and **covetous** <note>original: *lolupaḥ*</note>, driven by excessive desire. The text asks: "From where would such a person derive strength?" implying a general lack of vitality in these areas. ॥ 7 ॥

# Now, the Results of Saturn in the Eighth House

When **Saturn** resides in the **Eighth House** <term>Aṣṭama Bhava</term> <note>often called the "House of Holes" or *Randhra*, governing longevity, secrets, and sudden changes</note>, there is a **separation from genuine people** <note>original: *anaupādhikānām*</note>, meaning a lack of virtuous company or unconditional friendships. There is a **destruction of wealth**, and the person may become attached to anyone or anything indiscriminately. 

Likewise, the person is **sickly** <note>original: *vyādhitaḥ*</note> and is a **finder of faults** <note>original: *tudadarśī*</note> who seeks out the flaws in others. Before such an individual, what can the **deceit** <note>original: *ketavaṃ*</note> of others accomplish? The implication is that they themselves are so shrewd or "rogue-like" that they cannot be easily outmaneuvered by the trickery of others. ॥ 8 ॥

# Now, the Results of Saturn in the Ninth House

When Saturn has entered the house of Dharma, the **Ninth House** <term>Navama Bhava</term> <note>the house of fate, wisdom, and religion</note>:

<margin>
॥ Extensive virtue spreads through the world; seventy are the gods. ॥ 
॥ 20 ॥
</margin>

<insert>
With Saturn in the seventh, there are good wives but no lasting friends; fine wealth does not stay long, and the couple is sickly. ॥ 
Lacking enthusiasm, low-minded—how can he be powerful? He is ineffective and greedy. ॥ 7 ॥ 
There is separation from people and wealth; the destruction of unconditional riches occurs for him. ॥ 
With Saturn in the eighth, he is sickly and a fault-finder; what can another's deceit do in his presence? ॥ 8 ॥ 
The mind is sharp but the character is not bitter; he finds pleasure in the scriptures of Yoga; his nature is passionate. ॥ 
He is distressed for his friends and humble-minded; Saturn in the ninth makes one a worker of deeds or a renunciant. ॥ 9 ॥ 
The mother or father of the unborn may be lost; there is hardship in all work due to his authority. ॥ 
With Saturn in the tenth, he gives peace but life is a struggle; wages are small for his livelihood. ॥ 10 ॥
</insert>

<margin>
Saturn in the 7th: good wives, no friends, fleeting wealth, sickly couple. ॥ 7 ॥ Saturn in the 8th: separation from people. ॥ 8 ॥ Saturn in the 9th: sharp mind. ॥ 9 ॥ Saturn in the 10th: mother and father of the unborn. ॥ 10 ॥
</margin>

The person's **mind is sharp** <note>original: *matistiktā*, literally "bitter" or "pungent," implying a keen, perhaps detached intellect</note>, being disinterested in worldly objects, yet their character is **not bitter** or harsh. They find **pleasure** and practice in the study of **Yoga Shastra** <note>texts outlining the philosophy and practice of liberation</note>. Their nature is governed by the **passionate quality** <term>Rājasa Guṇa</term> <note>the energy of action and desire</note>. They are **humble-minded**; because of their compassionate nature, they feel **distressed for their friends** and relatives when they are in need. Despite a heavy heart, they act as a **doer of good** for everyone. Alternatively, they may **renounce the world** <note>original: *sannyasedvā*</note> and become an ascetic. ॥ 9 ॥

# Now, the Results of Saturn in the Tenth House

For one whose **Saturn** is in the **Tenth House** <term>Daśama Bhava</term> <note>the house of career, public life, and the father</note>, the "mother-influence" may be diminished or absent, perhaps meaning he was not nourished by her milk. He earns wealth **by his own arms** <note>original: *bāhureva*</note>, through his own effort. The father may be short-lived or of little help. 

Furthermore, due to his **authority** <note>perhaps as a treasurer or administrator</note>, he becomes a cause of distress. In the eyes of the world, he is associated with **difficult labor** and the administration of punishments. Conflicts and disputes arise in sequence. While he may grow in some respects, his **livelihood** is often hindered, resulting in very **low wages** or a meager income for his life's work. ॥ 10 ॥

->Thus ends the results of Saturn in the Tenth House.<-

<summary>This page details the astrological effects of Saturn in the 7th through 10th houses, noting its tendency to cause physical illness in marriage, shrewdness in the face of deceit, a penchant for yogic study, and a career marked by self-reliance but low financial reward.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Shani, Seventh House, Eighth House, Ninth House, Tenth House, Marriage, Yoga Shastra, Asceticism, Livelihood, Astrology</keywords>

--- Page 32 ---
<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis from the Chamatkara Chintamani. It concludes the discussion on Saturn (Shani) in the tenth house, moves through the eleventh and twelfth houses, and begins the section on Rahu’s influence in the first house (the Ascendant).</meta>

To facilitate understanding, the author provides an alternative opinion regarding **Saturn in the Tenth House** <note>original: *dashamastha shaneḥ matāntaram*</note>. When **Saturn** is situated in the **Tenth House** <note>the house of career, public life, and the sky/zenith, referred to here as *vyomage*</note>, does the individual find happiness from their mother? Or from their father, the progenitor? The remaining meaning implies that for the child, while the state of infancy and childhood is seen, they do not find guidance or leadership from parents. Furthermore, one does not receive a treasury or inheritance from the father; rather, the father himself may be sustained by "good seeds" <note>likely referring to the child's own merits or virtuous actions</note>. In terms of what is seen regarding the state of one’s own kingdom or position, it will certainly not be destroyed by the "circles" <note>political cycles or armies</note> of others, nor by the fear of deities, water, or fire. ॥ 10 ॥

### Now, the Results of Saturn in the Eleventh House

<margin>
Greed <note>original: *lobhaḥ*</note> will generally be excessive for such a person. This is the inner sentiment. ॥ 11 ॥
</margin>

When the **Son of the Sun** <term>Bhanu-putra</term> <note>a common epithet for Saturn</note> is in the **House of Gains** <term>Labha-bhava</term> <note>the eleventh house, governing income, profit, and elder siblings</note>, one’s **wealth is steady** and immovable. Income and honor are stable, yet the text suggests that health—or rather, the absence of disease—is not entirely fixed, meaning the body may fluctuate between states of health. **Progeny** <note>original: *apatyāni*</note> or children are not stable; the underlying sentiment is that there may be **grief regarding children**. The individual is a **donor**, prominent among many, and perhaps overly involved in the complexities of the priestly class <note>original: *vipra-prapañca-adhikaḥ*</note>. Free from the extremes of passion and hatred, they are distinguished, unique, and truly courageous. ॥ 11 ॥

### Now, the Twelfth

> Wealth is steady, life is long, honor is firm,
> but health is never fully certain.
> Courageous among a hundred, yet progeny brings grief,
> and worldly entanglements abound when the Son of the Sun is in the House of Gains. ॥ 11 ॥
> 
> When the **Son of the Sun** is in the **House of Expenditure** <term>Vyaya-sthana</term> <note>the twelfth house, governing loss, liberation, and foreign lands</note>, the man becomes **cruel**. He is timid rather than fearless, and possesses **dull vision** <note>original: *manda-netraḥ*, literally "slow-eyed" or weak-sighted</note>. He is **happy abroad** but not in his own home. If the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lagnapa</term> is also in the twelfth house, he becomes a consumer of wealth who destroys his enemies once and for all. ॥ 12 ॥

In the family <note>original: *kutumbe*</note>, "Darkness" <note>Saturn</note> does not foster a great sense of belonging; instead, the person becomes a fearless protector of wealth. There is a risk of the destruction of one's own kin and a fear of weapons or wicked people from whom one might receive harm.

<margin>Results of Saturn.</margin>

To clarify: when **Saturn**, the **Son of the Sun**, is in the **Twelfth**, the person is cruel and cowardly, not fearless. They are **shameless** <note>original: *nirlapo/nirlajjaḥ*</note> and have poor eyesight. They find joy in foreign lands but not at home. Furthermore, if the **Lord of the Ascendant** is situated in the twelfth house, they will destroy their enemies. By spending wealth on sacrifices or noble causes, they attain a specific kind of spiritual excellence. ॥ 12 ॥ 

Thus ends the section on Saturn in the twelve houses in the Chamatkara Chintamani.

### Now, the Results of Rahu in the Ascendant and other Houses

For one who has **Darkness** <term>Tamas</term> <note>an epithet for the shadow planet Rahu</note> in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, the strength of their enemies is broken; they are a **conqueror of foes**. However, due to the majesty and brilliance of others, they are **unable to keep their word**. Because of the influence of others, they may cover up or fail to protect the interests of their own people or their own wealth. Such a person might harm the wives of enemies, or there may be strife or affliction regarding their own spouse...

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of the shadow planet Rahu, following its placement in the First House from the previous page. It details the effects of Rahu as it moves through the Second, Third, Fourth, Fifth, and Sixth Houses.</meta>

...[one] will attain satisfaction. The inner meaning is that he will have excessive desires. ॥ 1 ॥

<insert>Now, the results of Rahu in the Second House.</insert>

If **Rahu** <note>the shadow planet and north node of the moon</note>, also called **Tamas** <note>meaning darkness</note>, is situated in the **Second House** <term>Kutumba-bhava</term> <note>the house of family, wealth, and speech</note>, the person's family and personal wealth will be destroyed. Consequently, due to the influence of wicked or "vile" people, he may suffer from dependence on others, loss of independence, or even imprisonment. He may also face fear from weapons. Furthermore, he becomes a speaker of falsehoods <note>original: *mṛṣā bhāṣite*</note>, yet remains fearless. He is a "protector who agitates Brahmins," which implies he is a miserly or ungracious person. One should conclude that his own kin will perish. ॥ 2 ॥

<insert>Now, the results of Rahu in the Third House.</insert>

In this birth, if Rahu is in the **Third House** <note>the house of courage, younger siblings, and efforts</note>, the person attains the strength of arms and valor equivalent to an **elephant** <note>original: *nāga*</note> or a **lion**. He gains respect throughout the world and from his brothers. Although he may attain good fortune, the "cause" of that fortune is often destroyed—meaning he might struggle to understand the source of his luck, or it may be acquired through unconventional means. He might find it difficult to gain profit from certain sources. ॥ 3 ॥

->|| Like an elephant or a lion, he moves here with the strength of his arms and valor;<-
->The Son of Simhika <note>an epithet for Rahu</note> brings such equality. In the third, he attains<-
->The world and brotherhood; though fortune is reached, where is the cause? ॥ 3 ॥<-
->In the fourth, how can there be health for the mother’s body? A flame in the heart,<-
->Can the body be cool? If it is otherwise, in Aries, Taurus,<-
->Cancer, or in Mercury's lands <note>Gemini and Virgo</note>, he is a friend to the King. ॥ 4 ॥<-

->Through worry over a woman, there is heat in the heart. When there is abdominal disease,<-
->What is the use of food? In this worldly web, why seek what is destined to be hidden? ॥ 5 ॥<-

<insert>Now, the results of Rahu in the Fourth House.</insert>

If **Rahu**, the son of **Simhika**, is in the **Fourth House** <note>the house of the mother, home, and happiness</note>, how can the mother’s body remain healthy? It cannot. With a "flame in the heart" <note>original: *hṛdi-jvālyā*</note>—meaning mental anguish—can the body remain cool? This implies the person will be afflicted by constant internal stress. However, there is an exception: if Rahu is placed in **Aries, Taurus, or Cancer**, or in the signs of **Mercury** <note>Gemini or Virgo</note>, the negative results do not occur. Instead, the person becomes a "friend of the King" <note>original: *bhūpate bandhur*</note>, meaning someone very dear to those in power. ॥ 4 ॥

<insert>Now, the results of Rahu in the Fifth House.</insert>

When Rahu is in the **Fifth House** <term>Suta-bhava</term> <note>the house of children, intellect, and romance</note>, the person's son may become a renunciant <note>original: *yati*</note> or monk. Due to an angry or worrisome woman <note>original: *bhāminī*</note>, there will be "heat in the mind" or constant anxiety. In the presence of **abdominal disease** <note>original: *kroḍa-roge*</note> or stomach ailments, what use is food or medicine? This suggests a condition like chronic indigestion or a weak "digestive fire." Without the grace of the divine, extensive efforts in this world bring very little gain. ॥ 5 ॥

<insert>Now, the results of Rahu in the Sixth House.</insert>

For those who have Rahu situated in the **Sixth House** <note>the house of enemies, debts, and health</note>, Rahu acts like a god to destroy the host of their enemies <note>original: *shatru-sanghātaṃ nāshayet*</note>. Similarly, through physical strength, intelligence, valor, and cleverness, the person forcibly acquires wealth and sustains themselves.

...The mind remains steady. It means the mind does not become fickle toward one's parents, and no harm comes to the mother or father. This conclusion should be applied everywhere in this context. ॥ 6 ॥

**Now, the results of Rahu in the Seventh House.**

If **Rahu** is in the **seventh house** <note>the house of partnership and marriage</note> from the Moon, there are certain specific results. Like **Jatavedas** <note>an epithet for Agni, the god of fire</note> who consumes everything it touches, this placement burns. Through the maturation of diseases <note>original: *pākādi rujā*</note>, one may suffer the loss of a young woman <note>likely referring to a spouse</note>. Likewise, one suffers the sorrow of separation <note>original: *viyoga*</note> from kinsmen. Public censure <note>original: *apavāda*</note> and social infamy do not find peace. ॥ 7 ॥

**Now, the results of Rahu in the Eighth House.**

Rahu situated in the **eighth house** <term>Randhra-bhava</term> <note>the house of longevity, secrets, and obstacles</note> acts as an enemy to the spouse. It causes a "knot of confusion" <note>original: *bhrama-granthi*</note>; through great exertion, it produces a swelling or "knot" in the throat <note>original: *vātagālakaṃ*; possibly referring to a goiter or glandular issue</note>. Furthermore, people may abandon such a person. As with the placement in the first house, wealth is gained through paternal uncles. The individual is honored and worshipped by kings and scholars, yet remains censured by his own kinsmen. Gain of fortune <note>original: *bhāgyalābhaḥ*</note> occurs only once.

->|| Strength, intellect, valor, and wealth are found when situated in the belly of the youth, in the house of enemies;<-
->The people are submissive. ॥ 29 ॥ This person, in death or in the body, a god...<-
<margin>Ma</margin> ->Rahu: a steady mind, not in the scales of the intellect...<-

There may be a loss of work once or repeatedly—this is the conclusion. ॥ 8 ॥

**Now, the results of Rahu in the Ninth House.**

Where Rahu is in the **Trine** <term>Trikona</term> <note>specifically the 9th house of fortune and father</note>, that man is a thinker <note>original: *manīṣī*</note> and intelligent. He is honored by his teachers <note>original: *gurus*</note> and is compassionate. He finds joy in divine pilgrimage sites. He becomes a "light of the assembly" <note>original: *sabhā-dyotakaḥ*; a prominent public speaker or leader</note>. He does not abandon his duties <unclear>p</unclear> and should always protect his kinsmen. ॥ 9 ॥

**Now, the results of Rahu in the Tenth House.**

When Rahu is in the **tenth house** <note>the house of profession and social status</note>, even in one's own country, the person spends wealth at the time of a public crisis <note>original: *jana-vadhāna-samaye*</note>. Consequently, he is agitated by people. He may acquire wealth through hundreds of different ways. He constantly associates with **Mlecchas** <note>foreigners or those outside the traditional social order</note> and is extremely proud. He enjoys the company of a proud, desirable, and intelligent woman. ॥ 10 ॥

**Now, the results of Rahu in the Eleventh House.**

If Rahu is in the **house of gain** <term>Labha-bhava</term> <note>the 11th house</note>, there is happiness regarding children. Wealth is constantly obtained from pilgrimage places. He is full of pride. He moves like a "lord of servants" <note>original: *kiṃkareśa*</note>; the meaning is that he goes nowhere without attendants or followers.

Why should one go to a foreign land? For what purpose would one travel? One should obtain everything while remaining at home. This is the sentiment. 

One whose friends are deceivers finds that his circle of relatives consists of those who are treacherous; they bring about all kinds of misfortune through their harmful influence. One might lose or have to give away the best of one's possessions. 

The rest follows the saying, "That which is given..." ॥ 111 ॥ 

### Results of the <term>Twelfth House</term>

In the **twelfth house**, the planet of darkness <note>original: *tamo*, referring here to Rahu</note> brings about a state of misery, pain in the sides, or wind-related suffering <note>original: *vāta pīḍā*</note> in the joints. There is friendship with wicked people and enmity toward the virtuous or good people. Even when efforts are made, the results are contrary to one's goals, leading to a waste of resources. In the end, it produces mental anxiety, though it also brings about the fulfillment of certain objectives—this principle applies throughout the analysis. ॥ 122 ॥ 

Thus ends the section on the results of the planetary positions. ॥ 22 ॥

***

Through constant association with suffering, one attains an extremely harsh nature. One finds no pleasure in the main enjoyments, such as the company of women. Such a person is agitated among people and is not a leader. He spends his wealth on intoxicants and performs cruel deeds. ॥ 120 ॥ 

One gains wealth only through constant hardship and is characterized by excessive pride. Why then go to a foreign land? The "friend of ruin" <note>likely referring to a specific planetary aspect</note> takes away one's mental peace and the best of one's possessions. If the planet of darkness <note>Rahu</note> is in the house of gain, even a son's happiness is compromised. ॥ 121 ॥ 

Darkness in the **twelfth house** creates poverty and pain in the side. Even when an effort is made, it results in worthlessness. It fosters friendship with the wicked and enmity toward the virtuous. However, once that phase passes, the fulfillment of the mind's desired goals is achieved. ॥ 122 ॥ 

**Shikhi** <term>Ketu</term> situated in the body-house <note>the First House or Ascendant</note> causes distress among kinsmen, as well as fear and agitation from wicked people. There is constant worry regarding one's wife and others, an ever-present state of anxiety, and the body may suffer from various types of wind-related disorders <note>original: *mārutī*, referring to Vata imbalances in Ayurvedic terms</note>. ॥ 1 ॥ 

**Ketu** in the house of wealth <note>the Second House</note> brings agitation. Does it not cause various types of loss of wealth or grain due to the king <note>the government or authorities</note>? It also causes diseases in the mouth. There is opposition from family and friends. Even if one's speech is refined, it may be misunderstood; however, if situated in its own house or a sign of a benefic planet, it may bring some happiness. ॥ 2 ॥

***

### The Results of Ketu in the <term>Twelfth House</term>

Now, the author explains the specific effects of **Ketu** in the twelve houses. First, the results for the **First House** <term>Tanu-bhava</term>:

If at the time of birth **Shikhi** <note>another name for Ketu, meaning "the crested one"</note> is in the first house <note>the Ascendant</note>, he causes distress and quarrels with kinsmen and brothers. There is fear of wicked people and mental agitation. One worries about one's wife and family. There is constant anxiety and the body suffers from various types of ailments related to "wind" <note>original: *mārutī*, often manifesting as joint pain, gas, or nervous disorders</note>. ॥ 1 ॥ 

**Now, the results of Ketu in the Second House.**

When **Ketu** is in the house of wealth <note>the second house</note>, there is always agitation regarding monetary matters or pressure from the authorities <note>original: *nareśād*, literally "from the lord of men/king"</note>. There is the destruction of stored crops or grains. It causes diseases in the mouth. There is conflict with family, friends, and others. Even well-spoken or beautiful words are not well-received. However, if Ketu is in its own house, such as Aries, or in the signs of a benefic planet like Gemini or Virgo, then happiness may result.

<meta>Following the previously blank and damaged folio, this page begins the actual text of the manuscript. It contains the "Jayalakshmi" commentary by Harivamsha on the "Narapati-jayacarya," a significant work on divination and astrology. This page establishes the spiritual lineage and historical context of the work.</meta>

# Salutations to the Illustrious Ganesha

**Om! Salutations to the Illustrious Ganesha.**
Having bowed to Ganesha, the destroyer of all obstacles, and to the <term>Lord of Kamala</term> <note>original: "Kamalapataye"; a name for the god Vishnu, husband of the goddess Lakshmi/Kamala</note>, I offer my salutation for the successful completion of this work without hindrance. || 1 ||

The poet **Harivamsha**, for his own benefit and for the benefit of others, now provides an explanation of the "Mother" sounds <note>original: "Matrika"; referring to the mystical powers of the Sanskrit alphabet</note>, the vowels, and the names associated with birth within the *Svarodaya* <note>original: "Svarodaya"; a system of divination based on the patterns of breath and sound</note>. || 2 ||

Having heard much from various teachers, and according to my own knowledge and intellect, I, the poet, compose this commentary titled **Jayalakshmi** <note>"The Goddess of Victory"</note> for the King. || 3 ||

Now, the poet bows to <term>Brahman</term> <note>The ultimate reality or universal soul</note>, whose nature is pure bliss, as the "Unmanifest."

The Unmanifest is without hands or feet; He is the direction, yet beyond the reach of the senses; He is peaceful and untainted. He is exceedingly dear to the hearts of <term>yogis</term> <note>Spiritual practitioners</note> and sages like Sanaka. He is the embodiment of all bliss, all-pervasive, and omnipresent. Salutations to that Reality which is beyond comparison. || 2 ||

Again, he bows to **Saraswati** <note>The goddess of knowledge, music, and the arts</note>, stating she is "known by various wise ones."

She is known as **Bharati** or **Saraswati** by various wise gods. Wishing to enjoy the clever mouths of those who praise her, she is invoked. The poet, known in the world by the name **Narapati**, creates this scripture titled *Narapati-jayacarya* <note>"The Victory-Conduct of the King"</note>. || 2 ||

Now, the poet Narapati explains his own country and lineage.
In the delightful city of **Dharapuri**, a place where the stream of the message of the gods flows and where knowledge is served...

---

**Om! Salutations to the Illustrious Ganesha.**
To those drowned in the darkness of delusion, I bow to that **Sun-like Shiva**, who has provided the "Good Vision" <note>original: "Sudarshanam"; a pun referring to both clear sight and the divine discus of Vishnu</note> through the rays of his knowledge. || 1 ||

To the Unmanifest, the Imperishable, the Peaceful, who is extremely dear to the yogis; to the non-dual Brahman, the all-pervasive nature of all bliss. || 2 ||

Bowing to **Bharati** <note>Saraswati</note>, who is known by various wise men, and desiring to grant an abundance of clever words to the people, the one famous in the world by the name **Narapati** shall compose this scripture named ***Narapati-jayacarya***. || 3 ||

In the *Treta* age, this work was known as the pure ***Jayarnava*** <note>"Ocean of Victory"</note> among the Brahmins. In the *Dvapara* age, it was known as ***Vijaya*** <note>"Victory"</note>, and in this *Kali* age, it is known as ***Svarodaya***. || 4 ||
<note>In Hindu cosmology, time is divided into four Yugas or ages. The text claims this divine knowledge has existed in different forms across these epochs.</note>

The first of these is the *Brahma Yamala*; second is the *Vishnu Yamala*; the third is called the *Rudra Yamala*; and the fourth is the *Adi Yamala*. || 5 ||
<note>The "Yamalas" are a specific class of ancient Tantric texts; the author is anchoring his work in this prestigious tradition.</note>

---

[The poet Narapati's lineage]
He was a wise man, worshipped by the world of various scriptures, famously named **Naradeva**. || 1 ||

He was a knower of the fruits of the strength of breath <note>original: "Svara-bala-phala-vetta"</note>, diligent in the truth of the body, a knower of the science of omens <note>original: "Shakuna-shastra"</note>, and a hero in the meaning of mantras. He was filled with the essence of beautiful and meaningful words. His son was named **Narapati**. || 2 ||

In knowledge, he was devoted to all scriptures. By the grace of Saraswati, he was granted success in his compositions, which are filled with many virtues and free from defects. || 3 ||

He who knows this scripture composed by the poet **Kusumarava**, which is proven by logic and tradition, will always obtain wealth and the Goddess of Victory will reside in his lotus-like hand. || 4 ||

Having conquered his enemies, he shall grant wealth to the king; of this there is no doubt. This work contains the methods for results related to the three periods of time <note>Past, present, and future</note>. || 5 ||

In the prosperous city of **Avanti** <note>Modern Ujjain</note>, during the reign of the famous King **Shri Ajayapala**, this work was composed by the poet **Shri Narapati** while residing there. || 6 ||

[The poet describes the inspiration or context, possibly a dedication or a description of a royal court scene]
Seeing a young woman with a fair body, a moon-like face, holding betel leaf in her mouth, appearing like the goddess of desire, her clothes slightly loosened, and anointed with sandalwood paste... looking at her, even those without desire become desiring. In the mind of the person who reaches this city, the three worlds seem to open up. || 7 ||

Thus ends the praise of the poet of the *Narapati-jayacarya Svarodaya*.
The meaning of this is easy to understand. No further commentary is written here because there is no need. || 8 ||

<summary>This introductory page includes the opening salutations and the first eight verses of the "Narapati-jayacarya," along with the "Jayalakshmi" commentary. It establishes the text's divine origin, its evolution through the four cosmic ages, and the lineage of the author, Narapati, during the reign of King Ajayapala.</summary>

Now, the author, to demonstrate his own competence in composing this work, names the scriptures he has consulted. || 7 ||

**Regarding the power of the vowels.** || 7 || **The Meaning of Sound.**

Now he speaks of the primary importance of the power of breath/sound. || 8 ||

**The Science of Breath** <note>original: "Swarodaya"</note> is the scripture of phonetic resonance. He who knows this is a "Swarodayi" (a knower of breath). Even a King who is a great conqueror, possessing a vast army of elephants, horses, and foot soldiers, will face total ruin in the midst of battle if he is devoid of the protection of this science—how much more so for a king with a small army? || 22 ||

Now he speaks of the power of a King who is supported by the strength of this breath-science: "Those heroes advance..." || 23 ||

Those heroic soldiers, the sons of the knowers of breath, being rich in the power of sound, cross over the **Ocean of Battle** <note>original: "Ahavasagara"; a common metaphor comparing a chaotic battlefield to a treacherous sea</note>. They achieve victory and reach the "other shore" of the conflict so long as they do not break their own rhythmic breath <note>original: "swaswara"</note>, which brings success without obstacles. Until that time, they remain like the tide held within its bounds. || 23 ||

He destroys fortresses with his voice and can pacify enemies. How can he conquer the enemies within—desire, hatred, pride, and malice—which are seated in the senses of form, taste, and smell? He conquers them through these resonances. How much more easily then can he conquer enemies armed with swords? He speaks further of this: "So long as..." <gloss>●</gloss> so long as one does not fall into the **Submarine Fire** <term>Vadavanal</term> <note>In Hindu mythology, the Vadavanal is a terrifying fire that lives under the ocean, preventing it from overflowing but also capable of consuming everything.</note>. In the circle of the breath, which is like the mouth of that fire, one does not fall into the cycle of the Great Fire of the breath-sounds.

---

> The *Skandayamala*, the *Varuna*, and the *Karmashyamala*. The *Seventh Yamala of the Goddess*—these are the **Seven Yamalas**. || 6 ||
>
> Having heard these <gloss>●</gloss> seven *Yamalas* <note>A class of Tantric texts usually structured as dialogues between deities</note>, as well as the *Yuddhajayarnava* (The Ocean of Victory in War), the *Kaumari Kaushala*, and the *Yogini Jala Sanchara* (The Movement of the Yoginis' Net). || 7 ||
>
> The *Ratnoccaya*, the *Trimunda*, the *Swarasimha* (The Lion of Sounds), and the *Swararnava* (The Ocean of Sounds). The power named *Bhairava*, and the *Pataleshvara Bhairava*. || 8 ||
>
> The famous *Tantraratna* (The Jewel of Tantra), the *Siddhi Tantra* which grants success. The *Mallava*, the *Danda*, and the *Dakshina Jyotisha* (Southern Astrology). || 9 ||
>
> Various **Mantras and Yantras** <note>Sacred chants and mystical diagrams used as tools for focus or power</note>, and the methods of secret warfare. Having understood the application of the Tantras through the science of the **Submarine Fire**. || 10 ||
>
> The essence of all these scriptures has been gathered in my heart. I have extracted this essence for the sake of compassion toward all living beings. || 11 ||
>
> A king's army of foot soldiers, horses, and elephants may perish in the dissolution of battle. Thus, a king who is not a knower of the **Science of Breath** meets with defeat. || 12 ||
>
> Those heroes travel across the ocean of battle so long as they do not break the circle of rhythmic breath in the presence of the **Submarine Fire**. || 13 || 

---

Even a person who knows only the first letter of their own name is considered a knower of the Science of Breath. In this **Ocean of Battle**, one does not fall into the power of the "Submarine Fire Circle" as long as they possess the strength of breath. They cross the battlefield as if it were a shore. Or again, when the time of one's own breath is mastered in the forest of battle, even the "Ocean of Battle" dissolves. Just as animals cross the sea by the strength of their limbs, men cross the sea of battle by the strength of their breath. They only perish where the "whirlpool of the submarine fire" exists. This is well known. For a King, the primary cause of victory is the mastery of breath. Therefore, those who desire victory must study the **Science of Svarodaya**. || 23 ||

Now, he explains how victory occurs even without the breath-science in certain encounters. || 24 || 

One should not lead a king who is devoid of the science of breath to the battlefield. For a king who has no mastery over his breath, his kingdom becomes like a blade of grass <note>original: "rbhaka"; meaning fragile or insignificant</note>.

# Now, the Unrivaled Questions and Answers as spoken in the Svarodaya
### Praise for the Power of Breath || 1 ||

One who is strong becomes the essence of reality itself; through this strength, even an ignorant man is joined with the source. || 15 || Through the power of tens:
Even a single man, if he is strong in his **Breath** <note>original: "Svara"; refers to the flow of prana through the nostrils and its resonance</note>, can defeat a King. Two such kings, strong in breath, can easily and effortlessly conquer their enemies. || 16 || With the power of these kings, who are strong in their breath, hundreds are conquered; and thus, hundreds can conquer thousands. To this extent, the power of breath alone is the measure of victory. || 17 || 

Now, to attain this power, the power of breath is the only true authority. Even if one is devoid of constant meditation, if he possesses the science of breath, he will not be intoxicated by pride. He becomes fit for battle. Even when facing an enemy who is swift and armed, as long as his breath is diminished, he must learn the timing. Through the strength of the **Arising of Breath** <note>original: "Svarodaya"</note>, one can attain a power of breath that allows him to battle against millions of armed men. || 18 || 

Now, I shall describe the gathering of the host of diagrams and how they are to be viewed. || 19 || 
The Breath-Chakras || 20 || 
The position of the three names || 21 || 
The power of the measures || 22 || 
The twelve-fold arising || 23 || 
The timing of death || 24 || 
This is easily understood. Thus are the names of the charts in the Svarodaya. || 4 || Thus, these are twenty-five. || 25 || The eighty-one-square chart. || 26 ||

---

How can one desire victory in battle without the lordship of the breath? || 9 || Such is the opinion of the world. Just as the letters of the alphabet are arranged... || 24 || 
He who has no knowledge of the Science of Breath during a conflict is like a **plantain tree** <note>original: "rambhastambha"; a common metaphor for something that looks sturdy but is actually hollow and weak</note>; his fall is certain. || 25 || 

One who is always practicing the Science of Breath, whose essence is conquered and whose senses are restrained, becomes the creator of that country. Victory and Fortune, O Lotus-Born One, will belong to him at the end. || 26 || 
Through tens, a hundred is obtained; through that, a thousand-fold portion is placed in my mouth. In the Arising of Breath, he destroys the enemy's land with ease. || 27 || 
Through the lack of discipline regarding this, one wanders far from the Arising of Breath. But through the power of the Arising of Breath, one can battle against millions of weapons. || 28 || 

The one who knows the breath, who knows the omens, and who has mastered the mantras—this is the Five-fold Tradition known as Kerala, Chitra, and Dhara. || 1 || 
When I remember the Breath-Time-Charts, both the powerful and the weak, they are like the six limbs of the **Science of Light** <note>original: "Jyotish-shastra"; Astrology</note>. || 2 || 
Among all these names, everything is expressed within the Arising of Breath. || 3 || 
I shall now describe these five: the twenty Breath-Time charts. || 12 || 
The Power of the Measure, the Great Soul, the sixteen-fold combination of the Zodiac and the Mansions. || 22 || 
The Twelve-fold Arising Chart, and the monthly cycle. || 22 || 
The Seven-Day Channel Chart, the lunar day, the weekday, and the combinations. The wood-like stiffness associated with the time of death, and the body of the breath. || 23 || 

Thus ends the Breath-Charts. 
These charts were previously spoken by the mouth of the Sage. I shall now explain the names of the forty-four charts. || 24 || 
The Eighty-one Square Chart, the Nine-part Chart, the **Throne Chart** <note>original: "Simhasana-chakra"</note>, and the five types of the **Tortoise Chart** <note>original: "Kurma-chakra"</note>. || 25 || 
25: The Country, the City, the Field, the Home, and the fifth is the Self. 
These are the five charts in the scripture, as well as the **Fire of Time** <note>original: "Kalanal"</note> doctrine. || 26 || 
Here, there is the Fire of Time Chart, the Fire of Time of the Moon, the Terrible Fire of Time Chart, and the Secret Fire of Time. || 27 || 
When the three moons are combined, it is known as the Fire of Time Chart. Dividing by seven hands, there are seven Fires of Time. || 28 || 
The various Rahu charts, and the three-fold arrangement of the arts. The **Pot Chart** <note>original: "Kumbha-chakra"</note> is said to be of two types, originating from the zodiac and the lunar mansions. || 29 || 
The piercing of the stars, which is three-fold and very secret. The piercing of the letters, and the three-fold Channel Chart. || 30 || 
The Cycle of Time, the Pair of Elephants, the Seven Sages, and the Moon. The **Monkey Chart** <note>original: "Kapi-chakra"</note> is said to be of two types, dealing with the lordship of place. || 31 || 
The work of the Breath-power is two-fold, starting with the position of the elephant. The Heron Chart, the Chisel Chart, and the various four-fold divisions. || 32 || 
The Great Elephant, the Banner, the Fragment, the Half-mountain. Service, immortals, leather, and leaves. || 33 || 
The Seven-Line Chart of the Self, the Five-Line Chart of the Moon and Sun. The three-fold Matrika Chart should be drawn according to the scriptures. || 34 || 
The Serpent, the Plow, and the Seed, along with the two doors for auspiciousness. The Chart of the Year, the Chart of the Place, and the Movement of the Limbs. || 35 || 
These are...

<margin>Page 2. The Commentary <note>original: "Tīkā"</note> of Jayaratha</margin>

After knowing all the **Mystical Diagrams** <note>original: "Chakras"</note>, one should indeed make an effort. One will triumph in this world without a doubt, even if the enemy is as powerful as the **King of Gods** <note>original: "Shakra," an epithet for Indra</note>. **36** || Thus ends the list of the names of the Diagrams. || Now, I shall speak regarding the **Fixed Strengths** <note>original: "Dhruva-bala"</note>. This was spoken by the **Deity** <note>original: "Devatā," referring to Shiva</note> and others as helpful in battle. **37** || When an enemy is equal or superior in their **Power of Breath** <note>original: "Svarodaya"</note> and their use of **Mystical Diagrams**, then the King who desires victory must apply these Fixed Strengths for the sake of the battle. **38** || I shall now speak the names of those strengths renowned in the world, totaling eighty-four, by whose power one becomes a conqueror in war. **39** ||

The North-Western Seat <note>original: "Uḍḍī"</note>, the Net-Bearer Seat <note>original: "Jālaṃdharī"</note>, the Full Mountain Seat <note>original: "Pūrṇā"</note>, the Seat of Desired Love <note>original: "Kāmā"</note>, the Noble Family Seat <note>original: "Kauleka"</note>, the Bilī, the Holy Bowl <note>original: "Śrī Camasā"</note>, the Māri, the Protector of the Field <note>original: "Kṣetrapāli"</note>, and the One of the Bamboo Lineage. **40** || The Auspicious Black One <note>original: "Bhadrakālī"</note>, the Great Black One <note>original: "Mahākālī"</note>, the Line of Time, the One Free of Disease. The Goddess of Fortune <note>original: "Jayalakṣmī"</note>, the Great Goddess of Fortune <note>original: "Mahālakṣmī"</note>, Victory <note>original: "Jayā" </note>, Triumph <note>original: "Vijayā"</note>, and the Terrifying One <note>original: "Bhairavī"</note>. **41** || The Maiden, the Mistress of Yoga, the Illusion, the One who Cuts the Hand. The Tigress, the Mother of Lions, the Great Illusion, and the Great Mistress. **42** || The Fortress of Gods, the Auspicious Energy, the Smoky One, the Bearer of the Garland. The Three-Headed One, the Vast One, the Terrible One, and the One of a Hundred Imperishables. **43** || The One who Removes Bad States <note>original: "Durgati Pravaro"</note>, the Fair One <note>original: "Gaurī"</note>, the Black One, the Man-Lion Strength. The Sky-Walker, the Earth-Walker, the Hidden One, the Twelfth, and the Only Friend. **44** ||

<note>This section is the "Tīkā" or commentary, which provides a line-by-line explanation of the verses above.</note>

The phrase "Having been" refers to verse **27**. "The Fire of Time of the Sun" refers to **28**. "Beloved of the Moon" refers to **29**. "In the holding of the edge" refers to **30**. "The Strike" refers to **31**. "The Wheel of Time" refers to **32**. "The Void of the Ditch" refers to **33**. "The Middle of the Elephant" refers to **34**. "The Two of Seven Lines" refers to **35**. "Thus, the gathering of the meeting" refers to **35**. "These" refers to **36**. || Thus ends the names of the **Mystical Diagrams** <note>original: "Chakras"</note>. || 

"Now in this" refers to **37**. || Now, the author speaks in praise of the **Fixed Strengths** <note>original: "Dhruva-bala"</note>. In the verse "In the Arising of Breath," it is meant that for those who know the timings of the Science of Breath, such as the stages of breath-flow, and who use their own astrological charts: if an enemy is equal or superior, then a King who desires victory must provide "Fixed Strength." If the enemy's breath is equal to one's own, then victory or defeat is uncertain. If a King, desiring victory, is anxious because his breath is similar to the enemy's, he should ask: "How can I be victorious?" To resolve this doubt, he should be prepared for battle by being given these "Fixed Strengths" which are even more powerful than the breath. || "Victory, Victory"—he now states the names of these Fixed Strengths. "Uḍḍī" and so on. **38** || "Of those" refers to **39**. "Uḍḍī" refers to **40**. "Bhadrakālī" refers to **41**. "The Maiden Mistress of Yoga" refers to **42**. "Fortress of Gods" refers to **43**. "Removal of Bad States" refers to **44**. "The Three Worlds" refers to **45**. "Abundance" refers to **46**. "The Black One with Her" refers to **47**. "The Moon with Seven" refers to **48**. Here the Fixed Strengths end in **49**. || The meanings are clear. **49** ||

"Superior to the enemy" refers to **50**. "Here to me" refers to **51**. || Just as an enemy becomes easily conquerable, I shall now speak of the power of **Spells** <note>original: "Mantras"</note> and **Talismanic Diagrams** <note>original: "Yantras"</note>. **52** || He states those names. The names of the Mantras and Yantras are: "The Red Anointing" refers to **53**. "The Girdle" refers to **54**. "The Yoga-Band" refers to **55**. "The Osa-kam" refers to **56**. "The Banner" refers to **57**. Because these are clear in meaning, they are stated as such. **58** || Thus ends the Power of Mantras and Yantras. ||

Victory over the Three Worlds <note>original: Trailokyavijayā</note>, the Solar <note>original: Saurī</note>, the Terrible <note>original: Karālī</note>, and the Higher Fire of the Depths <note>original: Vaḍavā Parā</note>. The Fierce One <note>original: Raudrī</note>, the Mother of the Forest Dwellers <note>original: Śiva Mātaṅgī</note>, the Invincible One, and the Destroyer. **45** || The six-fold colors, the Earth-ruler, and the elements of Wind and Fire. The gap between the Moon and Sun, the beams of light, the Great Zodiac <note>original: Mahārāśi</note>, and the Ascendant <note>original: Vilagna</note>. **46** || The fire of Time and Rahu <note>the eclipse demon</note>, the Earth, known as the rays of light. The monthly oceans and the methods of the North. **47** ||

The Moon is of seven types. The Yoginis <note>female spirits or powers of the directions</note> are threefold. The Time-cycles <note>original: Kālavela</note> are divided into three: those born of the lunar day, the star-mansion, and the weekday. **48** || These are the protectors from powerful ailments; when they enter, the enemies are destroyed like clouds scattered by the wind. **49** || Thus ends the section on the Foundations. ||

In the Name-Sound Circle <note>original: Svara-chakra</note> at night, one should persevere with effort. One should understand the strength of the enemy’s position and army. When an enemy is unbreakable, indivisible, difficult to handle, or impossible to defeat, the King should use the power of **Sacred Chants** <note>original: Mantra</note>, **Mystical Diagrams** <note>original: Yantra</note>, and other such strengths. **50-52** ||

The ritual consecration <note>original: Abhiṣeka</note>, the initiation rituals, and the preparation of the Hero's Band, the Jewel Band, and the **Victory Band** <note>original: Jaya-paṭṭa</note>. **53** || The wearing of the ritual belt and armor, the protective seal, and the war-shirt. The wise one also knows the use of medicinal forehead-marks <note>original: Tilaka</note>, the water-clock <note>original: Ghaṭikā</note>, and the movements of time. **54** || Weapons forged at specific astrological unions <note>original: Yoga</note>, the protection of those weapons, and the release of magical arrows. Ointments for weapons, and the knowledge of "World-Binding." **55** || The conquest of villages, cities, and forts. The six magical acts: Killing <note>original: Māraṇa</note>, Enchanting <note>original: Mohana</note>, Paralyzing <note>original: Stambha</note>, Creating Hatred <note>original: Vidveṣa</note>, Eradicating <note>original: Uccāṭana</note>, and Subjugating <note>original: Vaśa</note>. **56** || Diagrams drawn on flags to destroy the enemy's secret knowledge. Rituals of peace for one's own army and the destruction of all calamities. **57** || Knowing the secret science of these powers, the King enters battle. No enemy on the earth remains impossible to conquer. **58** || Thus ends the power of Chants, Diagrams, and Medicines. **59** ||

I shall now speak on the clear knowledge of calculation, practical application, and identifying thieves. This is the **Astrology of the Three Joys** <note>original: Trisandhya Jyotiṣa</note> within the *Victory of the Breath-Science* <note>original: Jayacharya Svarodaya</note>. **59** || The calculation of the **Planets** <note>original: Khechara, literally 'sky-goers'</note>, the five limbs of the calendar <note>original: Pañcāṅga</note>, and the calculation of lunar days. The rising and setting of the planets and the circular diagrams of the lunar days. **60** || The measurements of time past and future, and the calculation of the boundaries of the **Astrological Houses** <note>original: Bhava-sandhi</note>. Along with the Eight-fold divisions <note>original: Aṣṭaka-varga</note> and the results of the zodiac signs. **61** || The entry of the Sun into signs <note>original: Saṅkrānti</note>, the movements of the planets, and the birth charts of men, including the specific degree of the Ascendant. **62** || The planetary combinations <note>original: Yoga</note> of the Moon and others, the planetary periods <note>original: Daśā</note>, and their high and low points. The duration of life-cycles and the entry of those specific times. **63** || From that Ascendant, one calculates the twelve-fold divisions and the results of the days according to the eight-fold vowels. **64** || The **Annual Horoscopy** <note>original: Tājika</note> and the calculation of the birth moment are clearly explained. The results of the year, month, day, and the planetary periods are determined from this. **65** ||

---

Regarding the gathering of fruits... **74** || On the places of affliction... **75** || On the planetary movements... **76** ||
Regarding the external influences... **77** || On the five-fold division of lunar days... **78** || On the twitching of limbs... **79** ||

**Now, therefore:** || After this book, following the chapter on the Collection of the Sciences, I shall explain the rising of the **vowels** <note>original: svara</note>, which are differentiated by their measures and types. I shall also explain the **sacred chants** <note>original: mantra</note> as they have been described, in their many various forms. This is the connection between the topics. || **1** ||

Now, to speak of the sixteen vowels, he names the vowels: In the ancient **Mother-Alphabet Circle** <note>original: Mātrikā Chakra</note>, sixteen vowels were proclaimed. These vowels are:
**A, Ā, I, Ī, U, Ū, Ṛ, Ṝ, Ḷ, Ḹ, E, Ai, O, Au, Aṃ, Aḥ.**
Among these, the last two—**Aṃ** and **Aḥ**—are to be discarded <note>as they are associated with Rahu, the shadow planet</note>. Four are known as **neuter** <note>original: napuṃsaka</note> or "impotent" sounds. These are considered auspicious in the middle of the day, three-fold.

The measure of the day or the solar passage at the time of rising, or six **Ghatis** <note>units of 24 minutes</note>. **66** || Success in works is found in sixty-three parts, while the corrupted or "evil" parts number twenty-seven. To calculate the **Ascendant** <note>original: Lagna</note> at the time of rising in one's own country, one should use the tools for calculating the zodiacal sign. **67** || The measure of the Ascendant, the **Hora** <note>half a sign</note>, the **Drekkana** <note>one-third of a sign</note>, and the **Saptamsha** <note>one-seventh division</note>. Also the **Navamsha** <note>nine-fold division</note>, **Dwadashamsha** <note>twelve-fold</note>, **Trimshamsha** <note>thirty-fold</note>, and the degrees of rising and setting. **68** ||

I shall now speak of the types of **Lunar Days** <note>original: Tithi</note>, as well as the **Weekdays**, the **Lunar Mansions** <note>original: Nakshatra</note>, and the **Astromical Yogas**. That which is called the **Karana** <note>half a lunar day</note>, the **Muhurta** <note>auspicious moment</note>, and those signs arising from the **Sun's Transit** <note>original: Saṃkrama</note>. **69** || The strength of the Moon and the Stars, combined with the difference between the two fortnights <note>waxing and waning</note>. The auspicious and inauspicious combinations <note>yogas</note> arising from the lunar days. **70** ||

I shall speak of the "Accomplished" <note>Siddha</note> and the "Revolution" <note>Varttaka</note>, as well as the nature of the weekdays. In the **Shravana** mansion, I will speak of the "three-day" period, and then the **Tripushkara** combination. **71** || The "Bell of Death" <note>Yama-ghanta</note>, the "Tooth of Death" <note>Yama-dashtra</note>, the "Obstructing Bolts" <note>Argala</note>, and the "Forests." The stages of **Youth**, **Adulthood**, and **Old Age**, the mixture of fruits, and the **Yoginis** <note>directional spirits</note>. **72** || The nature of the weekdays, the specific **Times**, the **Time-Spans** <note>Kālavela</note> clearly defined, the **Planetary Hours** <note>Horā</note>, the half-watches of the day, the "Playful One" <note>Kelika</note>, and the two "Thorns" <note>Kantaka</note>. **73** ||

These results are of two kinds: those produced by the light half and the dark half of the month.
<margin>75</margin>
The results of the ten-fold peace-offerings and the movements of the planets. **74** || The **afflicted positions** <note>Pīḍāsthāna</note> of the wandering planets, the instructions for the "Great Forward-Moving Planet" <note>Shri Pravakra</note>, and the **transits** <note>Gochara</note> as they apply to weddings, consecrations, journeys, and entering a new home. **75** || The placement of the "Lamp of the Quarters," and the twenty-fold divisions of time. The junctions of births, and the divisions of the year, month, and half-day. **77** || The placement of the "Five Essentials" <note>Tithyadi Panchaka</note> starting with the lunar day, and the conclusions regarding the Moon and Stars. In the house of hearing, the "Fist" <note>a divination technique</note> and the avoidance of the "Eight-fold Moon." **78** ||

All the **twitching of the limbs** <note>Anga-spanda, used as omens</note>, and the daily divisions of time. I shall speak of the eight calamities, the remnants of kings, and the rites of pacification. **79** || Wise men who understand this connection of the **stars** <note>original: Jyotishām</note> should know it is like a **lamp**; it is the destroyer of the darkness of ignorance. **80** ||

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of "Svarodaya" (The Science of the Rising Breath/Sound). It transitions from a general list of vowels to their esoteric associations with deities and their application in divination (Prashna). The previous page ended with a discussion on the sixteen vowels of the Matrika (Mother-Alphabet) and the exclusion of certain sounds for specific mystical calculations.</meta>

These sixteen vowels are remembered as: **A, Ā, I, Ī, U, Ū, Ṛ, Ṝ, Ḷ, Ḹ, E, Ai, O, Au, Aṃ, Aḥ.** Among these, from the sixteen, ten are taken in pairs to be understood as one primary sound. These primary vowels are **A, I, U, E, and O**. The second vowels in each pair <note>the long versions like Ā, Ī, etc.</note> are considered equal in fruit to the first. Regarding these ten, placed in pairs and taken as one, it is said: The letter **A** is **Vasudeva** <note>a name for the Supreme God, Krishna/Vishnu</note>; because of his supremacy, **A** is taken first. The letter **I** is **Lakshmi** <note>the goddess of fortune</note>; she is the beloved of Vasudeva, therefore **I** is taken next.

---

|| **Now, I shall duly proclaim the words spoken by the Lord of the Three Worlds** <note>original: Trailokyeshvara; referring to Shiva</note> **regarding the rising of the sixteen vowels, distinguished by their measures and types.** || **1** ||

**The sixteen vowels are proclaimed as the excellent Mother-Alphabet** <note>original: Mātrikā</note>. **At the heart of these resides the first vowel, the "Conqueror of Death"** <note>original: Mrityunjaya; a form of Shiva</note> **himself.** || **2** ||

**The remaining ten vowels produce success when taken in pairs. Among them, the internal vowels are the "Five Vowels" used in the science of the Rising Sound.** || **3** ||

**Profit and loss, happiness and sorrow, life and death, victory and defeat—all these are revealed as meaningful when the vowels "rise"** <note>referring to which sound or breath-flow is active at the time of a query</note>. || **4** ||

**Knowing the Mother-Alphabet beginning with the vowels, one understands the entire Three Worlds through their measures. Therefore, this "Rising of the Vowels" governs...**
->**|| All things, moving and unmoving, in the Three Worlds. || 5 ||**<-

---

The letter **U** is **Rudra** <note>the fierce form of Shiva</note>, the letter **E** is the **Sun**, and the letter **O** is the **Moon**. These are the five vowels taken from the primary sounds because they appear as the governing forces. Regarding the letter **A**, it is expanded through its various forms at the time the "fruit" <note>result</note> is determined; the same applies to the letter **I** and the others. || **2-3** ||

Now, the Great One reveals the meaning of **Verse 4**: **"Profit and loss, happiness and sorrow..."** One should understand profit or loss when a question is asked during the "rising" of these specific vowels. Similarly, one should discern happiness or sorrow, or in the case of a patient's illness, life or death. In every type of inquiry, the vowels alone reveal the results. || **4** ||

He then states the supremacy of the vowels in **Verse 5**: **"The Mother-Alphabet beginning with the vowels..."** The pronunciation and the results of all moving and unmoving beings are produced only through the "Mother" sounds beginning with **A**. No syllable can be uttered without the Mother-vowels. Therefore, the entire world is pervaded by these Mother-sounds. This is why, in divination, the vowels alone reveal the results. || **5** ||

Now, he again reveals the supremacy of the vowels: Regarding **A, I, U, E, O**—these five vowels are the gods beginning with Brahma. Among them:
- **A** is **Brahma** <note>the Creator</note>
- **I** is **Vishnu** <note>the Preserver</note>
- **U** is **Rudra** <note>the Destroyer</note>
- **E** is the **Sun**
- **O** is the **Moon**

There is another purpose for these: When the vowel **A** is rising, one should perform commendable or desired works. When **I** is rising, one should meditate on **Vishnu**.

<summary>This page explains the "Pancha-svarodaya" (Rising of the Five Vowels), assigning deities like Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra to specific vowels. It establishes that these sounds govern all aspects of existence and can be used for divination regarding life, death, and success.</summary>
<keywords>Svarodaya, Vowels, Phonology, Divination, Matrika, Brahma, Vishnu, Rudra, Astrology, Vasudeva, Lakshmi</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of "Svarodaya" (The Science of the Rising Breath/Sound), transitioning from the identity of the vowels as deities to their specific cosmic functions. It details how the five primary vowels (A, I, U, E, O) correspond to the five elemental energies (Kalās), the five great elements (Mahābhūtas), and the five senses.</meta>

At the rising of the sound **U**, one should meditate on **Oṃ**. At the rising of the sound **E**, one meditates on the Sun; at the rising of the sound **O**, one meditates on the Moon. In this context, there are five *Kalās* <note>original: kalā; referring to stages or aspects of creative energy</note> beginning with *Nivritti* (Detachment). 

At the rising of the sound **A**, the aspect called *Nivritti* (Cessation) is active. At the rising of **I**, it is the aspect of *Pratishthā* (Establishment). At the rising of **U**, it is *Vidyā* (Knowledge). At the rising of **E**, it is *Shānti* (Peace). At the rising of **O**, it is *Shāntyatīta* (Beyond Peace). 

If a question <note>referring to divination or "Prashna"</note> is asked during the rising of **A**, due to the nature of the *A-vowel*, one should always predict a return or cessation of the matter. At the rising of **I**, there is establishment. At the rising of **U**, there is union with Knowledge and the Twilight-principle. At the rising of **E**, having meditated on the Sun, one should predict a peaceful outcome. At the rising of **O**, having meditated on the Moon, there is tranquility and a state of complete peace. 

When the breath is well-disposed, the powers of Will (*Ichhā*) and Sovereignty (*Iśā*) manifest in a fifteen-fold division. The five vowels starting with **A** are like a thread. Faith (*Shraddhā*), Intelligence (*Medhā*), and the five-fold Will-power (*Ichhā-shakti*) are situated there. Their rising should be understood according to what they depend upon. || 6 || 

Now, regarding the divisions of *Māyā* <note>the principle of illusion or manifestation</note> and so forth: the five vowels starting with **A** are associated with *Māyā* and the five-fold divisions of the subtle elements <note>original: tanmātra; the subtle basis of sensory perception</note> in sequence. At the rising of **A**, one should...

->The five vowels starting with **A** are the five deities of the *Kalās*.<-
->The five *Kalās* beginning with *Nivritti*, and the fifteen-fold Will and Sovereignty. || 6 ||<-
->The divisions of *Māyā* are described here as the lower five-fold Sovereignty.<-
->They are also called the "Givers of Desire" regarding the objects of sound and the rest. || 7 ||<-
->The Body, the Word, the Form, and that which is Beyond Form—the Stainless.<-
->This state is always achieved through the knowledge provided by the Guru. || 8 ||<-
->These five vowels starting with **A**—behold their mystery!<-
->They are the excellent measures, the life-force, and the union of the zodiac signs. || 9 ||<-
->One who does not approach this with the right intention or through the powers,<-
->To them, the description of these sounds is like a vessel without a measure. || 10 ||<-
->Thus ends the Wheel of Vowel-Measures. || 9 ||<-

A wheel of essence should be worshipped with the power of the *Nāga-vadhū* <note>lit. "Serpent-bride"; likely a specific ritual or yogic energy</note>. At the rising of **A**, one should conquer that wheel. At the rising of **I**, [the square] wheel; at **U**, the "Three-times" wheel; at **E**, the six-pointed wheel <note>a hexagon or Shatkona</note>; and at **O**, the circular wheel. 

Within the vowels starting with **A**, the five elements <note>original: bhūta-pañcakam</note>—starting with Earth—and the five powers—starting with Will—reside. 
- If a question pertains to the Earth, it arises at the rising of **A**. 
- If a question pertains to Water, it arises at the rising of **I**. 
- If a question pertains to Fire, it arises at the rising of **U**. 
- If a question pertains to Air, it arises at the rising of **E**. 
- If a question pertains to Space/Aether, it arises at the rising of **O**. 

One should speak of agitation or movement accordingly. These pertain to the sense-objects starting with Smell: 
- At the rising of **A**, the object is **Smell**. 
- At **I**, the object is **Taste**. 
- At **U**, the object is **Form/Sight**. 
- At **E**, the object is **Touch**. 
- At **O**, the object is **Sound**. 

Based on the rising of these vowels, one should declare whether the outcome of the question will be auspicious or inauspicious. Now, the true meaning is achieved only through the instruction of a Guru. What is the use of mere words? The meaning regarding what will happen in the future is understood through the grace of the Guru and the favor of one's Chosen Deity. 

As will be stated: "The Body, the Word," etc. Here, whatever measure is revealed is for the sake of the Supreme Truth, according to the Guru’s lineage. "The Body, the Word, and the Form" shows the eternal state. By this, what is accomplished here? Through the mastery of the vowels, the residence of the Self is attained. || 7-8 || 

Next, the purpose of the number of vowels starting with **A** is explained. The five vowels starting with **A** are the variations of the measures (*mātrās*), where the fifth becomes... || 9 || Here, the root of the division is explained. For one whose name does not align with the auspicious vowel-rising, success does not come. Therefore, the knowledge of names and the "Vowel-Measure Wheel" is essential. Just as in a vessel, the measures are found, so too is the measure of the sound **E** in this text...
