<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for phonetic divination. It specifically focuses on the "Matra Swara" (the vowel quality inherent in a name) and the "Varna Swara Chakra" (the grid used to correlate consonants with specific vowels). It cites Lampatacharya, a known authority in this tradition of "Swarodaya" or breath/sound science.</meta>

Where the inherent vowel-measure is the sound **E**, and where in a name the letter **Ya** appears as a component, it contains the vowel **A**. Thus, where a name is given, the vowel-measure is **A**. This applies to all names. For names like **Ruma** and others, the vowel-measure should be understood through this teaching; if it is not explicitly stated, it must be inferred. For instance, in names like **Devirudra**, the experience of the vowels **A** and **I** is demonstrated through their respective measures. Therefore, the vowel-measure for the sound **A** is **I**. || 10 || This is the **Vowel-Measure Wheel** <term>Matra Swara Chakra; a diagnostic grid used to determine planetary or elemental influences based on the vowels in a person’s name</term>. || 11 ||

Beginning with the components of the alphabet from the letter **Ka** to **Ha**, one should write the vowels **A, I, U, E, O** in the middle. The practitioner of this method should write the letters from **Ka** to **Ha**. In this wheel, one should exclude the fifth nasal letters of the five consonant groups—**ṅ, ñ, ṇ, n, m** <note>original: "kavarga chavargo... pañchama varṇā"; referring to the nasal endings of the five rows of the Sanskrit alphabet</note>. These should be written in a horizontal sequence within a grid of twenty-five squares, or expanded to a grid of thirty-five squares. || 11 || As shown in the wheel: || 12 || 

Regarding the first letter of a man's name: if we take the name **Devadatta**, the first letter is **Da**. The vowel **O** is written below that **Da**; therefore, the letter-vowel for **Devadatta** is **O**. In the name **Yajñadatta**, the first letter is **Ya**. Since the vowel **U** is written below it, the letter-vowel for **Yajñadatta** is **U**. || 12 || This rule is established for names like **Chaitradanti**.

> One should write the letters from **Ka** to **Ha** in order, aligned beneath the vowels. They should be placed in horizontal rows within twenty-five squares. || 13 || 
> 
> The first letter of a person’s name is located beneath a specific vowel. That vowel is called the **Letter-Vowel** <term>Varna Swara; the specific vowel assigned to a consonant in this system of divination</term> for that letter. || 122 || 
> 
> These are not just names, but are known in sequence as the groups of **Ga, Ja, Da** <note>referring to specific mnemonic groupings in Sanskrit grammar or astrology</note>. || 13 ||
> 
> If there is a conjunct consonant <note>original: "saṃyukta"; a cluster of two or more consonants without intervening vowels</note> in the name, its characteristics must be explained according to tradition. The *Brahmayamala* <note>An ancient Tantric text dedicated to the cult of Bhairava</note> states that one should look to the first letter of the cluster. || 24 || <insert>🌸</insert>

In specific regions, certain letters may vary; this should be understood when calculating the "Ripening of Results" <term>Phala Vipaka; the karmic or predictable outcome of an action or name</term>. The results for the groups **Ga, Ja, Da** are not identical. || 13 || Now, another method is described: regarding the name, if the first letter of anyone's name is a conjunct consonant, one might ask which letter-vowel should be taken. As mentioned before, one looks to the first letter and its corresponding vowel. For example, in the name **Shripati**, the letter **Sha** is the first part of the cluster. Since it is written under the vowel **I**, the letter-vowel for **Shripati** is **I**. This logic applies everywhere. 

Furthermore, if a person has many names, which name's vowel-measure should be used? The masters of sound <term>Swarajña; experts in the science of phonetic resonance</term> say that according to the teachings of **Lampatacharya** <note>A historical sage/author associated with astrological and phonetic works</note>, when a man has many names, one should use the name by which he is most commonly called. This is the opinion of those skilled in the science of sound. 

Regarding another letter-vowel calculation: those letters situated directly below **U** and **O** are used for determining victory or defeat. Finally, regarding the determination of the letter-vowel for a household, **Lampatacharya** states: 

Whether in a region, a village, a city, or a palace, the letter-vowel should be declared based on the first letter of the name. The results will be perfectly accurate. The *Yamala* scripture describes the science of sound and its measures. || 14 || <gloss>11</gloss> <gloss>66</gloss>

⁂ A comparison of victory and defeat in specific directions. 54 ||

Regarding the "dead vowels" <note>original: "mṛtasvarā"; sounds that have lost their potency in a specific divinatory context</note>... **|| 15 ||** Now, the time-measurements for the twelve-year cycle of the <term>Narodaya; literally 'the arising of man,' a calculation used to determine the timing of events based on the sounds of a name</term> are explained. In the period of *Shaka Pushya* <note>a historical or astrological timeframe</note>... **|| 16 ||** Regarding the essence of the nectar... **|| 17 ||** Regarding the "Gaja-Pranagi" <note>a technical term for a specific rhythmic or astrological measure</note>... **|| 18 ||** 

The measures for the twelve-year cycle of Narodaya are 11 | 7 | 8 | 98 <note>these represent specific units: Ghatikas, Palas, and their subdivisions</note>. The annual Narodaya measure is also 11 | 7 | 8 | 98. The measure for the <term>Ayana; the six-month solar progression (solstice)</term> is 7 | 0 | 58 | 09. The measure for the transit is 7 | 23 | 56 | 3. The monthly Narodaya measure in <term>Palas; a unit of time equal to 24 seconds</term> is 1 | 18 | 71. The general Narodaya measure is 7 | 49 | 03. The daily Narodaya measure is 7 | 32 | 8. The hourly <term>Ghatika; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> Narodaya measure is 39. Thus ends the section on the eighteen measures. Now, the importance of the vowel-measures is explained:

In the time of desire... **|| 20 ||** Regarding the sound of the vowels... **|| 21 ||** One should establish the sound of the vowels. **|| 21 ||** Through the syllables: Aries, Leo, and Scorpio <note>original: "meṣa-siṃhāli"; 'Ali' refers to Scorpio</note>. The sound **A** corresponds to Aries, Leo, and Scorpio. These syllables/signs are the forms of the vowels. Whichever vowel corresponds to a sign, the lord of that sign is also the lord of that vowel. For example, the vowel **A** takes the form of Aries, Leo, and Scorpio. The lord of Aries and Scorpio is Mars <note>original: "Bhīma"</note>; the lord of Leo is the Sun <note>original: "Sūrya"</note>.

---

The "dead vowels" are the short sounds... like the star Revati. From that, a youth becomes powerful; a youth who is not red is powerful. **|| 15 ||** In the Shaka [era] or the month of Pushya... twelve and more. The Indra-cycles are seven [3?], according to the standard. **|| 16 ||** Measure 98... Likewise, the measure for the solstice in Palas is "Elephant-Arrows" <note>a mnemonic for the number 58</note>. Fire, barley, and arrows... fire, eyes, and units of Palas. **|| 17 ||** The "form of the elephant" is described as the measure for the month... Nine, full, nine-eyes, six, and parts of the measure. **|| 18 ||** The measure of the asterism (Nakshatra) comes from fire, according to the sequence of the day. Through the measure of the <term>Ghatika; 24-minute unit</term>, the Narodaya vowel is found. **|| 19 ||** <term>Now, the Planetary Vowels</term>: The vowel **A** belongs to Aries, Leo, and Scorpio. The vowel **I** belongs to Virgo, Gemini, and Cancer. The vowel **U** belongs to Sagittarius and Pisces. The vowel **E** belongs to Libra and Taurus. **|| 20 ||** The vowel **O** belongs to Capricorn and Aquarius. Mercury, Venus, the Friend [Sun], Mars, and Jupiter are the lords of Aries and the others in sequence. **|| 22 ||** 

->**|| Thus ends the Wheel of Planetary Vowels ||**<-

---

Mars and the Sun are the lords of the [A-vowel] syllables. Likewise, for Virgo and Gemini, the lord is Mercury; for Cancer, the Moon. Therefore, Mercury and the Moon are the lords of the **I** vowel. For Sagittarius and Pisces, the lord is Jupiter; thus Jupiter is the ruler of the **U** vowel. For Taurus and Libra, the lord is Venus, the ruler of the **E** vowel. For Capricorn and Aquarius, the lord is Saturn; Saturn is the lord of the **O** vowel. 

Now, an example is given: <gloss>The fourth quarter of the star Revati.</gloss> For the syllable **Re** in the star Revati: The star Revati corresponds to the sign of Pisces. The vowel in Revati is **E** <note>the text here explores the phonetic components</note>. Pisces is represented by the **U** vowel. The lord of Pisces is Jupiter, who is the lord of the **U** vowel. Therefore, for a person named Devadatta, the vowel is **U**. Likewise, for the word beginning with **Ya**: The phonetic power is found in the star Jyeshtha. In the word "Yajña," the letter **Ya** corresponds to the star Jyeshtha and the sign of Scorpio. Scorpio's lord is Mars. Therefore, for the word "Yajña," the planetary vowel is **A**.

<note>original: "Grahasvaracakram"; This table correlates vowels with the twelve zodiac signs (Rashis) and the seven traditional planets.</note>

### || The Wheel of the Life-Vowel ||
| A | I | U | E | O |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Group A | Group Va | Group Ka | Group Ca | Group Ṭa |
| 1 | 6 | 5 | 5 | 5 |
| Group Ta | Group Pa | Group Ya | Group Śa | Group Kṣa |
| 5 | 5 | 4 | 4 | 4 |

<note>original: "Jivasvaracakram"; This system is used to identify the "Life-Vowel" (Jiva-svara) which represents the living essence of an individual's name.</note>

Regarding the sixteen syllables... **|| 1 ||** From the groups of letters beginning with 'A', we determine the life-vowel from the name **Devadatta** <note>a traditional placeholder name, like "John Doe"</note> as an example: The letter 'Da' has the value of 3; the vowel 'I' above 'Da' has the value 11; the letter 'Va' followed by 'U' has the value 1; 'Va' itself has the value 4; the 'Ta' following 'Da' has the value 1; the letter 'Da' has the value 3; the joined letter 'La' in the sequence has the value 2; the vowel 'A' has the value 1. The total sum is twenty-six (26). When divided by five, the remainder is one (1). Therefore, for Devadatta, the <term>Life-Vowel; the primary phonetic frequency governing a person's life-force</term> is 'A'. **|| 2 ||** Thus ends the Wheel of the Life-Vowel. 

### || Now, The Wheel of the Zodiac ||

The sixteen groups of syllables are formed from the letters starting with 'Ka' and the five groups ending in 'La'. When four letters are combined, the group number is declared. The group of 'A' and the group of 'La' represent the pure name. **|| 1 ||** In a name, the vowel and the first letter are known as the phonetic markers. These are divided by five, and the remainder is known as the Life-Vowel. **|| 2 ||** Thus ends the section on the Life-Vowel. 

For the vowels 'A' and 'I', there are nine <term>Navamshas; a "ninth-part" or sub-division of a zodiac sign used for precision</term> of Aries, nine of Taurus, and six of Gemini, totaling twenty-four. With three more divisions of Gemini, there are the signs of Cancer and Leo, totaling twenty-one. For the vowel 'U', there are nine divisions of Virgo and nine of Libra. Along with three divisions of Scorpio, these total twenty-one. For the vowel 'E', there are six divisions of Scorpio, and nine each for Sagittarius and Capricorn, totaling twenty-one. For the vowel 'O', there are three remaining divisions of Capricorn and eighteen divisions for Aquarius and Pisces, totaling twenty-one. Thus is the "Vowel-Sign" described, following the sequence of the <term>Navamsha; the sub-divisional chart</term>. **|| 3 ||** Thus ends the Wheel of Zodiac Vowels.

<note>original: "Rashisvaracakram"</note>

Regarding the explanation of the <term>Nakshatra-carana; the "feet" or quarters of the lunar mansions</term>: The vowel 'A' is the ruler of the quarters of the lunar mansions starting from Ashvini through Mula. For the five mansions following Punarvasu, including the 'Ni' sound and the quarter-divisions, the vowel 'I' is the ruler. For Uttaraphalguni, including the 'Ni' sound and the four mansions starting from Hasta, plus two quarters of Anuradha, the vowel 'U' is the ruler. For the remaining two quarters of Anuradha, the four mansions starting from Jyeshtha, and three quarters of Shravana (totaling twenty-one quarters), the vowel 'E' is the ruler. For the final quarter of Shravana and the mansions starting from Dhanishtha to the end (totaling twenty-nine quarters), the vowel 'O' is the ruler. In the name Devadatta, the vowel is 'A' and the sign-vowel is also 'A'. **|| 3 ||** Thus ends the Wheel of the Zodiac.

## || The Wheel of the Body-Vowels ||

| || A | I | U | E | O || |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Ka | Ja | Ga | Gha | Ca |

<note>original: "Pinda-swara-chakram"; This table maps the consonants of the Sanskrit alphabet to the five primary vowels to calculate the "body" or core phonetic vibration of a person's name.</note>

---

The vowel 'A' <note>original: "Akaro"</note> governs the seven stars: Revati, Ashvini, Bharani, Krittika, Rohini, Mrigashira, and Ardra.
The vowel 'I' is the master of five stars starting from Pushya <note>Pushya, Ashlesha, Magha, Purva-Phalguni, and Uttara-Phalguni</note>.
The vowel 'U' is the master of five stars: Hasta, Chitra, Svati, Vishakha, and Anuradha.
The vowel 'E' is the master of five stars from Anuradha <note>likely referring to the next set: Jyeshtha, Mula, Purva-Ashadha, Uttara-Ashadha, and Shravana</note>.
The vowel 'O' is the master of the five stars from Shravana to Uttara-Bhadrapada.

**|| An Example ||**
For the name "Devadatta," the vowel associated with the Revati star is 'A'.
> The vowel 'A' governs seven stars starting with Revati. The vowels beginning with 'I' govern groups of five stars each; this is the <term>Nakshatra Swarodaya</term> <note>The rise of sounds associated with stars</note>. || 1 || **Thus ends the Wheel of Asterism Vowels.**

> Take the vowel-group and the number of letters in the name, multiply by the quantity... when the remainder is found after removing the "Body-Lord," it is called the <term>Pinda-swara</term> <note>The vowel representing the physical aggregate/body</note>. || 1 || **Thus ends the Wheel of Body-Vowels.**

> By the various distinctions of vowels and quantities, the King's vowel is known. When the remainder is found after multiplying by the "Lord of Union," it is called the <term>Yoga-swara</term> <note>The vowel representing combined or situational forces</note>. || 1 || **Thus ends the Wheel of Combination Vowels.**

Regarding the 'A' vowel in the Wheel of Asterism: **An example is given here.**
In the name of Devadatta, for the letter 'Da', the vowel is 'E'. For the letter 'Ja', the number is 5 and the group... <note>The text here details a complex mathematical calculation where numerical values are assigned to the letters of the name "Devadatta" based on their position in the grids</note>.
The sum of the values for Devadatta <note>D-e-v-a-d-a-t-t-a</note> results in 24. After dividing and finding the remainder, the value is 4. Therefore, the "Body Vowel" (Pinda-swara) here is 'Ai'. **Thus ends the Wheel of Body-Vowels.**

Regarding the distinctions of vowels: Here is an example. For the name Devadatta, the quantities are as follows: the quantity vowel is 'E', its number is 4. For the vowel 'O', it is 5. For the planet-vowel 'U', it is 3. For the life-vowel 'A', it is 1. For the zodiac-vowel 'E', it is 5. For the 'A' vowel, it is 1. For the body-vowel 'E', the sum is 5. Thus, the total for the name Devadatta is 20. Dividing this total, the remainder is 3. Therefore, for Devadatta, the "Combination Vowel" (Yoga-swara) is 'U'. || 6 ||
Thus, the various wheels for the natural and calculated vowels of a name are completed.

**Thus ends the second chapter, titled "The Chapter on the Wheels of Vowels and Quantities," in the annotated commentary of the *Narapati Jayacharya Swarodaya* <note>A classic 12th-century Sanskrit treatise on phonology and divination for kings</note> composed by Shri Harivansha Mahadeva.**

Now, regarding the vowels derived from the name <term>Devadatta</term><note>A traditional Sanskrit placeholder name, similar to "John Doe."</note>, we describe his daily routine and prospects starting from the beginning of the Twelve-Year cycle until its conclusion. It is said in the verses that the "natural" vowels are eight in number, beginning with the measure <note>original: "mātrā"</note>. These are called natural <note>original: "naisargika"</note>; those born from a name are "name-born" <note>original: "namaja"</note>.

Therefore, for everyone, there is a separate application for the twelve-year cycle, divided into years, solstices, seasons, months, fortnights, days, and <term>nadis</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note>. To explain the timing of these influences: beginning with the year <term>Prabhava</term><note>The first year in the 60-year Jupiter cycle.</note>, each of the sixty years (Prabhava, Vibhava, etc.) is governed by the five vowels—A, I, U, E, O—in sequence, each ruling for twelve years. This means that for the twelve years starting with Prabhava, the vowel 'A' is the master.

> The natural vowels are eight, beginning with the measures; then there are the name-born vowels. These govern the various spans of time, starting with the twelve-year cycle. || 2 || In the sequence starting with Prabhava, the vowels arise; 'A' is the first vowel. || 3 || That which is the twelve-year period for the vowels is one cycle. || 2 || Within that year, a "year" [proportionally] becomes a month, and a month becomes two days. A half-verse is called a <term>nadika</term>, consisting of twenty-eight <term>palas</term>. || 3 ||

## || The Twelve-Year Cycle Wheel ||

The "rising" <note>astrological onset</note> is thus: Starting from the year named Prabhava, for twelve years the vowel 'A' rules. After that, for the next twelve years, the vowel 'I' rises and becomes the presiding lord. Following that, for twelve years, the vowel 'U' rises and acts as the "King." Then, starting from the year <term>Shobhana</term>, for twelve years the vowel 'E' is the King. Finally, for twelve years starting from the year <term>Rakshasa</term>, the vowel 'O' is the King.

**Here is an example:**
In the year <term>Samvat</term> 1572 <note>approx. 1515 CE</note>, which is <term>Shaka</term> 1437, we must calculate the time elapsed from the year Prabhava. By performing the calculation <note>the text describes a mathematical formula involving multiplying and dividing the Shaka year to find the current position in the cycle</note>, we find that 16 years, 1 month, 25 days, 55 <term>ghatis</term>, and 30 <term>palas</term> have passed since the beginning of the cycle on the first day of the bright fortnight of the month Chaitra in Shaka 1437.

Since 16 years have passed, and the first 12 years belonged to the vowel 'A', the vowel 'I' is currently the ruling "Lord" <note>original: "Isvara"</note>. Now, looking at our example of the name **Devadatta**: his "Body-Vowel" <note>original: "Pinda-swara"</note> is 'U'. Based on the strength of this rising vowel, the "Body-Vowel" will determine whether his actions will meet with success...

Regarding the start of the year *Pramathin* <note>The 13th year in the 60-year cycle</note> within the <term>Twelve-year Cycle</term><note>original: "dvādaśavarṣa"</note>, the text explains the <term>Sub-period</term><note>original: "bhukti"</note> or inner-period of the vowel **I**. It states the precise measurement of time for the "experienced" and "yet-to-be-experienced" sub-periods of the vowels, starting with the phrase "rising at the very end."

The duration is: **1 Year, 1 Month, 2 Days, 43 Ghati, and 39 Pala.** <note>A Ghati is 24 minutes; a Pala is 24 seconds.</note> By these measures of years and other units, the vowels "rise" within the twelve stages. This <term>Inner-period</term><note>original: "antardaśā"</note> of vowels refers to the duration each vowel governs within the annual wheel for years such as *Prabhava* and those following it. This duration of vowel governance must be recorded in the daily practice of dividing time. || 3 || This concludes the **Annual Wheel**.

Next, we describe the vowels that rule each of the years starting with *Prabhava*. It is said that the five vowels starting with **A** arise one by one for the years beginning with *Prabhava*. What does this mean? For each year like *Prabhava*, a vowel starting with **A** becomes the "lord" or ruler.

For example:
*   When the year is *Prabhava* <note>The 1st year: "Origin"</note>, the vowel **A** rises.
*   When the year is *Vibhava* <note>The 2nd year: "Expansion"</note>, the vowel **I** rises.
*   When the year is *Shukra* <note>The 3rd year: "Brightness"</note>, the vowel **U** rises.
*   When the year is *Pramoda* <note>The 4th year: "Joy"</note>, the vowel **E** rises.
*   When the year is *Prajapati* <note>The 5th year: "Lord of Creatures"</note>, the vowel **O** rises.

Thus, for those years currently in progress, the five vowels **A, I, U, E, O** arise individually. Within the twelve-year span, the method for determining the sub-period is based on these vowels. || 2 || This concludes the **Annual Vowel Wheel**. The ruler of this rising vowel is the Lord, as mentioned in the hymns. The experience of the monthly sub-period is determined by the measure of the year's experience. || 3 ||

->।। This concludes the Solstice Vowel Wheel ।।<-

When they rise, specifically when the vowel **A** rises, there is a subsequent rising of the other eleven vowels <note>This refers to a more complex system where primary vowels are further subdivided</note>. This means that during the years of the cycle starting with *Prabhava*, the "Lord of *Prabhava*" governs the sub-periods of the eleven vowels. Regarding the timing of these inner-periods, it is said that for the twelve-year span, the sub-period of the vowels is one year, one month, and so on. Specifically, the sub-period consists of **1 Year, 1 Month, 2 Days, 43 Ghati, and 38 Pala.**

This is the sub-period for the vowels in the *Prabhava* cycle. When *Prabhava* is active, the vowel **A** rules for the year. Within that, the first sub-period belongs to **A**, followed by the sub-period of **I**, which has the same duration. After **I** comes **U**, following the same calculation. After **U** comes **E**, and then **A** rises again. In this way, the eleven vowels rise as sub-periods. By this method, one should divide the twelve-year cycle into eleven parts. This specific calculation for sub-periods is not found elsewhere in the text but will be explained further ahead.

Having divided the Lord of the Day <note>The Sun</note> into three parts, the movement of the Sun through the signs produces the six <term>Seasons</term><note>original: "rtu"</note>, each consisting of two months. While the Sun resides in **Aries** <note>original: "Mesha"</note> and **Taurus** <note>original: "Vrisha"</note>, and then moves into the part of the sign of **Gemini** <note>original: "Mithuna"</note>, this period constitutes seventy-two **72** days. This is how the different days are counted. With these seventy-two days and others, the first vowel "rising" occurs. In this way, twelve **12** degrees of **Gemini**, the full thirty degrees of **Cancer** <note>original: "Karka"</note>, and twenty-four degrees of **Leo** <note>original: "Simha"</note> together make up seventy-two **72** days for the next vowel's rising.

Six **6** degrees of **Leo**, the full degrees of **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanya"</note> and **Libra** <note>original: "Tula"</note> totaling sixty **60**, and fifteen degrees of **Scorpio** <note>original: "Vrishchika"</note> make up seventy-two days for the next sound-rising. Then, the remaining twenty-four **24** degrees of **Scorpio**, the entirety of **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Dhanu"</note>, and eighteen **18** degrees of **Capricorn** <note>original: "Makara"</note> result in seventy-two **72** degrees/days for the rising of the next sound. Finally, the remaining twelve **12** degrees of **Capricorn** and the signs of **Aquarius** <note>original: "Kumbha"</note> and **Pisces** <note>original: "Meena"</note> make up seventy-two days for the final vowel-rising. In this sequence, the five vowels starting with **A** arise during the two-season cycles <note>one vowel per 72 days, covering the 360-day year</note>. **|| 1 ||** <insert>Now follows the specific vowel-periods for each season.</insert> In these vowel periods, it is easy to understand that there are three inner periods, with the vowels rising in the "apertures" <note>body channels</note> in a sequence of six days each.

The vowels beginning with **A** rise along with the seasons starting with **Spring** <note>original: "Vasanta"</note>. In each of these, the Vowel-Lord rises for seventy-two days. **|| 1 ||** The specific rising occurs for six days and [the calculations continue for] the remaining units of **65**. This is the measurement for the rising of sounds in the middle periods. **|| 2 ||** This concludes the **Wheel of Seasonal Vowels** from the *Yamala* <note>A category of ancient Tantric texts</note>.

In the months of **Bhadrapada**, **Margashirsha**, and **Vaishakha** <note>roughly September, December, and May</note>, the vowel **A** rises. The vowel **I** is considered the ruler over **Ashvina**, **Shravana**, and **Bhadrapada** <note>roughly October, August, and September—note the textual overlap in months</note>. **|| 1 ||** The vowel **U** is the lord of the "field" or sign, while the vowel **E** rules in **Jyeshtha** and **Kartika** <note>June and November</note>. The vowel **O** rises during the months of **Magha** and **Phalguna** <note>February and March</note>. **|| 2 ||** In the first three, the time units are thirty **Pala** <note>12 seconds</note>. With the middle periods added, this constitutes the monthly vowel system. **|| 3 ||** This concludes the **Wheel of Monthly Vowels** from the *Yamala*.

The vowel **A** corresponds to the sixth part... [numerical notations **7**]. **|| 1 ||** ...measured by the half-month. **|| 4 ||** This concludes the **Wheel of the Six Vowels**. Following the sequence of vowels starting with **A**, the five-fold <term>Lunar Days</term><note>original: "Tithi"</note> and constellations are assigned. The daily sound-rising occurs according to one's own birth-date/lunar day. **|| 1 ||** The internal rising lasts for five **Ghatika** <note>2 hours</note> and some Palas. For the lunar days, the duration is five Ghatikas and twenty-seven Palas. Combined with the middle periods, this is used by those who track daily sounds. **|| 2 ||** This concludes the **Wheel of Daily Vowels** from the *Yamala*. For the **Wheel of the Minute-Vowels** <note>original: "Ghati-swara"</note>, the duration is five Ghatikas and twenty-seven Palas. The internal rising is said to be half a Ghatika in measure. **|| 1 ||** This concludes the **Wheel of the Minute-Vowels** from the *Yamala*.

Now, he describes the **Intermediate Rising** <term>Antarodaya</term><note>The sub-period within a larger cycle when a specific vowel's influence emerges.</note> within the **Twelve-Year Cycle** <term>Dvadashabdi</term>. Regarding the duration of the Twelve-Year sub-risings: the measurement of time is fivefold here—Years, Months, Days, <term>Yamas</term> <note>3-hour periods</note>, and <term>Nates</term> <note>sub-divisions of a Ghati</note>. By these five measures of time, the fruit <note>the result or prediction</note> is determined. As stated here: one year, one month, and so on, in the ripening of the cycle, for some it is **12 years, 4 months, 3 days, and 38 Ghatis**. For others, the calculation is by months and groups of two days or by <term>Nadis</term> <note>another term for Ghatis</note>. Such is the calculation of years and other units. (1)

Now, he describes the vowels of the "passed" and "remaining" intermediate risings as written by the great masters. Regarding the twelve-year cycle, here is an **example**:

Currently, in the twelve-year cycle beginning with the year **Abhaya**, the vowel **'A'** <note>original: "akara"</note> has finished its rising. In the present time, starting from the year **Pramadi** and lasting for twelve years, the rising of the vowel **'I'** <note>original: "ikara"</note> is occurring. In the **Saka year 1437** <note>approx. 1515 CE</note>, at the time of the first day of the bright fortnight of the month **Chaitra** <note>the Hindu New Year</note>, the passed time of this vowel is **4 years, 2 months, and 25 days**. 

The years are multiplied by 360 days and added to the days, equaling **1495 days**. <note>The text uses "Sharagothisudhamshavah," a poetic way of writing 1495 using numerical symbols.</note> This sum, multiplied by the middle value, results in **89,700**. These are the "passed" <term>Dandas</term> <note>Ghatis</note>. When multiplied by sixty, it results in **5,382,000** <term>Palas</term> <note>the smallest unit mentioned, 1/60th of a Ghati</note>. 

Now, the total <term>Palas</term> in a twelve-year intermediate rising are **24,137,980**. <note>The text uses the symbolic code "Vyoma-kunjara-gola-agni-sudhakara-yugaim" to represent this large number.</note> This number is the "divisor" for the twelve-year intermediate rising. By this divisor, the number of passed vowels starting from vowel 'I' is determined. The 13th vowel from 'I' is **'O'**, which currently holds lordship over the sub-period. Its passed and remaining days are **316** and **521**; the remaining days are **75** and **52** <term>Ghatis</term>. Thus, the rising of the vowel **'O'** exists from the first day of the bright fortnight of **Chaitra** until the first day of the dark fortnight of **Ashadha**. After that, the intermediate rising of the vowel **'A'** will occur.

Now, an example of the intermediate rising within the **Annual Vowel Rising**:
Currently, the 16th year is **Chitrabhanu**. The passed Saka years indicate the form of the "remaining years" of the **Pramadi** cycle. For instance, at the beginning of the <term>Kalpa</term> <note>a vast cosmic age</note>, on the first day of the bright fortnight of Chaitra... <note>text continues to describe calculations from the dawn of time</note>. Then, when the measure of a year's days—**985**—has passed, on the first day of the bright fortnight of Chaitra, it is written that one year has passed. This indicates the second form of the year's "remaining" time. 

And the Saka... <note>catchword for next page</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Swarodaya system, moving from general time-vowel charts into specific astronomical calculations. It bridges the gap between the 12-year Jupiter cycle and the vowel-divination system, using the Shaka year 1437 (1515 CE) as a case study. The text references classical authorities like Varahamihira to validate its mathematical methodology.</meta>

As each individual year passes, the years of the Shaka era <note>A historical calendar era starting in 78 CE, widely used in Indian astronomy</note> accumulate. From these, the "Jupiter years" <note>original: "Brihaspati-varsha"; a cycle based on the orbital period of Jupiter, roughly 12 or 60 years</note> arise. Their derivation is found in the *Siddhantas* <note>Scientific astronomical treatises</note> and the *Varaha Samhita* <note>A famous 6th-century encyclopedic work by the astronomer Varahamihira</note>. The measure of the Sun’s revolutions <term>Bhagana</term> constitutes the solar years.

According to the speech of the sages, the revolutions of Jupiter are 427 <note>original: "jinayama-veda" (427). In Sanskrit numerical notation, 'Jina' is 24, 'Yama' is 2, and 'Veda' is 4. Read right-to-left as 427</note>. In each of these cycles, the planet Jupiter occupies a zodiac sign <term>Rashi</term>. According to Varahamihira, these revolutions are fixed at 17 <note>likely referring to a specific sub-cycle or remainder calculation</note>. By dividing these revolutions by twelve, the Jupiter years are found. Therefore, the difference between the solar years and Jupiter's revolutions is calculated. The difference between 364,224 <note>Jupiter revolutions in a specific epoch</note> and 432,000 <note>The standard total of solar revolutions in a Great Age or Mahayuga</note> is 50,688. The common divisor <term>Apavartaka</term> is 2,304.

At the beginning of the Shaka era, Jupiter’s position was 28 | 36 | 22. The remainder of the Jupiter year is calculated through the solar cycle, obtaining 11 or 22 <note>These represent planetary degrees and positions</note>. For the cycle beginning with *Prabhava* <note>The first year of the 60-year Jupiter cycle</note>, the sign-occupancy of the deity of that sign is determined. Above that, for the next year, the final minutes <term>Kala</term> of the Sun's occupancy are 7,727.

The daily movement of Jupiter is calculated using these minutes. The number of days obtained is sixty-five (65). On the first day of the bright half of the month of **Chaitra** <note>The lunar New Year, roughly March-April</note>, it began with 13 <term>Dandas</term> <note>A unit of 24 minutes</note> and 4 days. Thus, the beginning of the next year occurs with 26 <term>Dandas</term> and 9 [units]. In this way, the entry of all years is explained according to the method in the commentary.

When 59 years of the Shaka era have passed, the year known as **Praharsa** <note>one of the years in the 60-year cycle</note> ends. One must then declare the successful beginning of the following year. This is my own opinion, shown here through the door of mathematics, so that other wise men may know it.

Now, those days of the current year were spoken of; from 58 years, the "consumed" <term>bhukta</term> minutes are not heard <note>meaning they were not accounted for</note>, just like the rising of Jupiter's own revolution.

### ॥ An Example ॥

In the **Shaka year 1437** <note>approximately 1515 CE</note>, from the start of the era, the "passed" and "remaining" years are 16 or 17. For the mean Sun, 55 days have been consumed. Thus, on the first day of the bright half of **Chaitra**, in the Shaka year 1436, on the fourth day of the bright half of the month of **Magha**, the year **Subhanu** <note>The "Auspicious" year, 17th in the 60-year cycle</note> began. The days spoken of for that year are 55 and 45 <term>Dandas</term>.

The presiding lord of the **Subhanu** year is the vowel **I** <note>original: "Ikara-svara"</note>. Its "consumed units" <term>Pala</term> are two hundred thousand and seven hundred (200,700). After the vowel **I**, the second vowel **U** <note>original: "Ukara-svara"</note> begins to operate. Its duration is 9 days and 42 <term>Dandas</term>.

What results from this? On the 10th day of the bright half of **Chaitra**, after 40 <term>Dandas</term>, the "northern rising" of the vowel **I** will occur. On the 12th day of the bright half of **Vaishakha**, it will be 25 <term>Dandas</term> and 40 <term>Palas</term>.

Now, we shall establish the first day of the bright half of **Chaitra**...

Now, the rising of the vowel <term>Swara</term> and its subsequent rising are described. An example is given regarding the Southern Solstice <term>Dakshinayana</term>. Because of the traditional teaching that "The Master and Lord rule the Northern Solstice," on the first day of the waxing moon of the month of Chaitra <note>the lunar New Year</note>, the rising of the vowel "I" <note>original: "Ikara-udaya"</note> begins during the Northern Solstice <term>Uttarayana</term>. Similarly, Varahamihira’s *Decision on the Solstices* states that the Southern Solstice begins when the Sun enters the sign of Cancer <note>original: "Karka"</note>. In the Shaka year 1436 <note>1514 CE</note>, on the 12th day of the bright fortnight of the month of Vaishakha, the Sun is in Capricorn <note>original: "Makara"</note>. Starting from then, until the 13th day of the bright fortnight of Chaitra, the Sun is in Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>. Therefore, on the first day of the waxing moon of Chaitra, the rising of the vowel "I" is indeed present.

**Now, an example of the successive risings:**
In the signs starting with Aries, there are 78 *Kroshas* <note>a measure of distance or arc</note>. These equal 280,800 *Palas* <note>original: "ashta-shatadhika-ashtavimshati-ayuta"; 28 units of 10,000 plus 800</note>. The *Palas* for the interval between solstitial vowel risings are 58,808. This numerical set is the duration of the vowel interval. This itself is the divisor <term>Hara</term>. By this, from the elapsed *Palas* of the solstice, the rising of the vowel at the interval of the directions is calculated as 3 [units], 48 [minutes], and 59 [seconds]. Above the 49th degree on the first day of the waxing moon of Chaitra, the rising of the vowel "U" <note>original: "Ukara"</note> occurs. This continues until the 33rd *Danda* <note>a unit of 24 minutes</note> on the 3rd day of the dark fortnight of Vaishakha.

**Now, an example of the rising of vowels according to the seasons:**
Now, the commencement of the seasons <term>Ritu</term> begins from the Sun’s transit <term>Samkranti</term> into a sign. As the author of the *Samhita* says: "Lead the season through the measure of the transit of two zodiac signs." Based on this teaching, from the transit into Capricorn until the first day of the waxing moon of Chaitra, 18 days have passed without the transit into Pisces <note>original: "Meena"</note> occurring. The Spring season <term>Vasanta</term> begins from Pisces. By this, the rising of the seasonal vowel is determined. The interval for the seasonal rising—that is, the time for the "Eye of the Quarter"—is 6 days, 36 [minutes], and 43 [seconds]. The *Palas* for this are 23,563. This is the divisor for the interval rising. Now, for the rising vowel: the elapsed *Palas* of the rising vowel are 64,800. This is the dividend. Dividing the dividend by the divisor, the vowel rising of the regional interval is 5 [units], 1 *Danda*, 38 *Palas*, and 9 [seconds]. By that calculation, in this interval rising, the vowels "A" and "I" have elapsed, and the interval rising of the vowel "U" is currently active. Its remaining portion on the first day of the waxing moon of Chaitra is 33 degrees and 9 *Palas*. This lasts until the 5th *Danda* of the 33rd [unit] on the third day of the waxing moon of Chaitra.

**Now, an example of the vowel rising according to the month:**
From the first day of the waxing moon of Chaitra until the end of the second day, 16 days are considered passed <note>likely referring to a specific calculation of elapsed tithis</note>. There, the division of the vowel rising for the month-interval is: in [each] day, there are 43 *Nadis* <note>same as Danda/Ghadi</note> and 28 *Palas*. The divisor for these *Palas* is 5,898. For the vowel "U" which has risen, the elapsed *Palas* are 576,003. This is the dividend. Dividing the dividend, we get 5 [units] and 19 *Dandas*. Above that, the interval rising of the vowel "I" occurs, lasting until the 5th *Danda* on the 4th day of the waxing moon of Chaitra.

**Now, the example of the vowel according to the lunar fortnight:**
Here, the vowels function in a specific sequence. In the bright fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha</term>, the vowels are "A" and "I." Starting from the first day of Chaitra, the rising of the "I" vowel is present. On the first day of the waxing moon of Chaitra, the rising of the "I" vowel is also the subsequent rising.

**Now, the rising of the daily vowel:**
This also belongs to the "I" vowel.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Swarodaya (vowel-rising) system. While the previous page focused on the mathematical calculation of when a vowel "rises" during the solstices and specific dates like Chaitra 1514 CE, this page shifts focus to the qualitative "states" or "ages" of these vowels. It explains how the power of a specific vowel changes as it progresses through its allotted time, much like a person aging from infancy to death.</meta>

# The Twenty-Seven Types and the Wheel of Vowel Duration

The twenty-seven types of power and their boundaries are established in this manner. || 1 ||
This concludes the **Wheel of Vowel Duration** <term>Ghati Svara Chakra</term> <note>a system used to calculate the influence of vowels over specific time intervals</note>.

The twelve vowels are then discussed. || 2 || These apply to the year, the month, the day, and so on. || 2 || The twelve vowels are spoken of as follows. || 3 || This is how they are obtained. || 4 ||

### The Five Stages of Vowel Power

Now, after the rising of the twelve vowels, there are five stages beginning with the **Child** <term>Bala</term>. Depending on the specific vowel of a person's name, these five stages—beginning with the "Child" stage—occur for the twelve vowels. These stages must be calculated. There is no difference in how these stages are categorized; they are common to all vowels. Therefore, the five stages are imagined based on the vowel of one's own name. For some, based on the name-vowel, the designation **Child** <term>Bala</term> is given; for others, the designation **Youth** <term>Kumara</term> is given; for others, **Adult** <term>Yuva</term>, and so on. || 5 ||

These five designations are:
1.  **Child** <term>Bala</term> <note>original: "Adyo Balah"</note>
2.  **Youth** <term>Kumara</term> <note>often translated as "Prince"; representing growing energy</note>
3.  **Adult** <term>Yuva</term> <note>representing peak strength</note>
4.  **Old** <term>Vriddha</term> <note>representing declining energy</note>
5.  **Dead** <term>Mrta</term> <note>representing total lack of power</note>

As stated, these names are assigned according to the vowel.

---

The durations of the twelve vowels end in their respective places and times. || 76 || Furthermore, in this system, the ten-fold rise is shown in the lines. || 1 || 

The measurement of time in this **Vowel-Rising System** <term>Swarodaya</term> is taught as being five-fold:
*   **Year** <term>Varsha</term>
*   **Month** <term>Masa</term>
*   **Day** <term>Diva</term>
*   **Hour-unit** <term>Nadi</term> <note>traditionally 24 minutes</note>
*   **Minute-unit** <term>Pala</term> <note>traditionally 24 seconds</note> || 2 ||

The five-fold strength of the twelve rising vowels is then determined. It is obtained according to its own measure, and more is gained if the measurement is exceeded. || 3 || 

From the five vowels obtained, the remaining ones are known as the "risen" vowels. In this "Ten-Fold Wheel" <term>Dashadi Chakra</term>, the vowel is said to have "risen." || 4 || Depending on the name-vowel of the risen vowel, these five stages—Child and the rest—occur according to their own duration of time. || 5 ||

**The Five Stages and Their Effects:**
1.  **Child** <term>Bala</term>
2.  **Youth** <term>Kumara</term>
3.  **Adult** <term>Yuva</term>
4.  **Old** <term>Vriddha</term>
5.  **Dead** <term>Mrta</term>

There is no doubt that these produce results according to the nature of their specific stage. || 6 || 

*   The **Child** stage causes lamentation <note>original: "vilapa"; signifying grief or lack of success</note>.
*   The **Youth** stage grants half-gain.
*   The **Adult** stage is said to bring total attainment.
*   The **Old** stage brings certain loss. || 7 ||

In matters of travel <term>Yatra</term>, war <term>Yuddha</term>, marriage <term>Vivaha</term>, finding lost items, or dealing with the sick: wherever the "Child" vowel is present, marriage and other auspicious acts will not flourish. || 8 ||

---

### Interpretation of the Name-Vowel

The designations are made according to the vowel of the name. How this is explained is as follows: The name-vowel itself marks the end of the rise of the twelve vowels. The second vowel is designated as **Youth** <term>Kumara</term>. The third is designated as **Adult** <term>Yuva</term>. The fourth is designated as **Old** <term>Vriddha</term>. The fifth is designated as **Dead** <term>Mrta</term> <note>the OCR reads "ashtama" (eighth) here, likely a transcription error for "mrta"</note>. In this way, the designations are imagined based on the name-vowel. Each stage gives results according to its own nature. || 6 || Why does the Child cause lamentation? || 7 || In the calculation of time, what is the profit? In travel and war... || 8 || Everything... || 9 ||

**The Auspicious and Inauspicious Results:**
In all auspicious works and at the time of travel, the **Youth** <term>Kumara</term> brings success and constant victory in battle. || 9 || 

In all matters of good or bad, and in the mastery of **Spiritual Formulas** <term>Mantras</term> and **Sacred Diagrams** <term>Yantras</term>, the **Adult** <term>Yuva</term> gives great success, especially in travel and war. || 10 || 

In the pursuit of knowledge, the worship of deities, and in spiritual initiation <term>Diksha</term>, if the vowel is **Old** <term>Vriddha</term>, there will be disease, fear, and excessive effort. || 11 || 

In marriage and all auspicious things, even if it would bring fame in battle, no auspicious work whatsoever should be performed when the **Dead** <term>Mrta</term> vowel has risen. || 12 || 

Regarding auspiciousness... || 10 || Regarding knowledge and worship... || 11 || Regarding marriage and the rest... || 12 ||

It explains how these stages determine success or failure in activities like war, marriage, travel, and mantra recitation based on the timing of a person's name-vowel.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Swarodaya" system, a method of divination based on the sounds (vowels) of names. The previous page established that vowels undergo five stages of life—from infancy to death. This page provides the specific rules for determining the relative strength of these stages and how they dictate victory or defeat in conflicts or endeavors.</meta>

# The Hierarchy of Strength Among the Five Stages

Now, he describes the strength of the states, such as the "Child" stage, in their successive order. The phrase "The Dead and the Child" <note>original: "Mṛto Bāla"</note> is easy to understand. || 13 ||

Whichever person's vowel is in the third state <note>likely referring to the "Adult" or "Successful" stage</note>, they obtain victory over the one whose vowel is in the fourth state. From that point on, they suffer a breakdown or defeat. Whichever person’s vowel is in the second state, that "Second" also obtains victory everywhere. Regarding the rule of "Who is whose": the fifth vowel in a sequence is the **Giver of Death** <term>Mṛtyu Dāyaka</term>, because the fifth position is designated as the "Death" sign. One should not engage in battle against such people. If one's own power is small, and one is in the "Dead" state while the opponent is in the "Child" state, then between these two vowels, the **Child Vowel** is stronger. Between a **Child** and the **Old**, the **Old** is stronger. In a conflict between the **Old** and the **Youth**, the **Youth** <term>Kumāra</term> is stronger. Between the **Youth** and the **Dead**, the **Dead** <note>paradoxically representing a completion or transition</note> is stronger. Between the **Dead** and the **Child**, the Child is stronger. This is the calculation of strength. || 14 ||

Now, he specifically speaks of the visible results regarding skill in battle and other matters. In the phrase "Deficient in the Dead" <note>original: "Mṛto hīna"</note>: between two opponents—

->The **Dead**, the **Child**, the **Old**, the **Youth**, and the **Restless** <term>Tarala</term>—<-
->all these vowels should be understood in their successive strength<-
->by those who know the science of sound. || 13 ||<-
->Whichever vowel stands in the **fifth position** from another,<-
->that vowel is the **Giver of Death**. Success comes from the<-
->**third position**; the others provide their own specific fruits. || 14 ||<-
->There is no victory for a vowel lacking in strength <note>literally "Dead-deficient"</note>,<-
->nor is there victory against a vowel of superior strength.<-
->When names are equal, there is equality: either a treaty,<-
->or a draw between victory and defeat. || 15 ||<-
->With one vowel, there is equality; a second vowel gives<-
->half the result. With the third, the result is full;<-
->a bond of three leads to defeat. || 16 ||<-

---

<margin>SUCCESS: A vowel "deficient in death" means that the vowel which is "Dead" <note>the weakest or terminal state</note> meets its death from a vowel of superior strength. In that system, the **Old** vowel is superior to the **Youth**. The **Dead** vowel obtains its application from the **Child** vowel. The **Child** vowel obtains its release from the **Youth** vowel. The **Youth** is superior to the **Old**. As it was said: "The Dead, the Child, as well as the Old." The vowel **A** <note>Akāra</note> is the primary measure; the vowel **I** is the second; the vowel **U** is the third; the vowel **E** is superior to those; and **Ai** is the highest vowel. This is the calculation for the vowels. Now the meaning is explained: How does a poet's proclamation of a "position" happen? It is said there: "The Dead, the Child, and the Old"—by this, the successive strengths are described. Without this meaning, it cannot be told. "Deficient" refers to a weak vowel. Is it not said that these stages like "Child" are neither weak nor superior? Victory comes through a superior vowel. Between two opponents, whoever has a "deficient" vowel in their name, the one with the superior vowel succeeds. If both are equal, the result—<gloss>here is an example</gloss>—is that if one person has only one vowel as the primary strength in their name, and the other has a superior vowel, the one with the deficient vowel "dies" (loses). Victory belongs only to the one with the superior vowel. Likewise, if one has two vowels and the other has one, victory still goes to the one with the superior/more vowels. The vowels starting with **A** are successively superior. Those who know the **Circular Charts** <term>Kuṇḍali</term> understand this meaning. When names are equal, there is equality. If there is no difference in the vowels of the two opponents, there is a draw. That is called a treaty or a stalemate between victory and defeat. If the vowel measures of one, two, three, four, or five units are equal, then it is a state of "Samya" (balance) or friendship. Or, in such a state of equality, victory may come to both sides after the battle.</margin>

<vocab>पञ्चावस्था (Pañcāvasthā): The five stages of vowel life (Child, Youth, Adult, Old, Dead). स्वर (Svara): Vowel or sound-energy. मृत्यु दायक (Mṛtyu Dāyaka): Giver of death/failure. कुण्डलि (Kuṇḍali): A circular diagram or chart used for these calculations.</vocab>

It also details how the number and specific type of vowels in a person's name determine their success or failure in battle and negotiations.</summary>

# The Relative Strength of Vowels in Conflict

<margin>
Wherever strength is to be determined at the beginning of the sounds, it should be known there. There, one should consider the measures of one unit, two units, and so on. Among these, the path of a single measure of strength is considered the best.
</margin>

Defeat; in this very context, strength determines defeat. This is the principle of equality. As stated in the *Essence of Battle* <note>original: "Samara Sara"</note>, victory belongs to the one whose wind <note>referring to the breath or "Prana"</note> is blossoming and equal. The distinction between those engaged in battle lies in this equality. In cases of equal measures, the rule is as follows: regarding the measures, they are known to be of one unit, two units, and up to five units. Here, one has a <unclear>curved</unclear> banner or sign which is of five units. For another, the name and its letters are determined by the number of the sound's origin. Victory comes to the superior; violence or a truce results when the strength is diminished or equal. || 25 ||

When strength is gathered in one place, or two units of strength are equal, no one achieves victory and no one suffers defeat. Between a **Child** <term>Bāla</term> and a **Youth** <term>Kumāra</term>, the numbers indicate that the Youth has a slight advantage in strength. When the strength is equal to the "Varuni" <note>likely a specific astrological or elemental state</note>, victory is constant. Between a **Child** and the **Old** <term>Vṛddha</term>, the Child's strength is lower; the Child's measure is only 16. Now, for one desiring to conquer an enemy, the text explains the timing of that victory.

### The Timing of Victory over an Enemy

When the enemy’s vowel of death <term>Mṛtyu Svara</term> is active—at that time when the enemy's death-vowel is vibrating with its own sound—one should begin the battle. Victory will surely occur and not otherwise. Indeed, timing is the primary cause of all victory. || 17 || Now, one should perform the mounting of the weapon when the time is in its "Adult" stage. At that time, consider the measures. || 18 || From the "Dead" state onwards, one should not engage in debate or learning. At the time of battle, the measure of the name’s vowel moves to its own fifth sound. In battle, the rising of the pulse-sound <term>Nāḍī Svara</term> or one's own sound occurs.

> When the enemy's Death-Sound is vibrating within one's own, one should begin the battle at that time; victory is certain. || 17 ||
> At that time, as long as the measure does not exist in the sound, one should speak of the grasping of the vowel which arises in the "Life" <term>Jīva</term> stage of the watch <note>original: "Yāma," a three-hour period</note>. || 18 ||
> Just as the sign is known by the season, from which the manifestation of the constellation occurs, the **Pinda Sound** <note>the collective sound-sum of a name</note> is known through the combination of the twelve zodiac signs. || 19 ||
> Strength is always powerful and all-pervading; there is no doubt. Therefore, with all effort, one should determine the strength and weakness of these forces. || 20 ||

When the enemy is in the "Dead" state, if one engages in battle, victory will surely follow.

### Case Study: Devadatta versus Viraja

Take, for example, a battle between **Devadatta** and **Viraja**. At that time, the sound 'A' is the pulse-sound <term>Nāḍī Svara</term>, and 'I' is the measure. By the strength of the vowel sounds, Devadatta’s 'A' is in the **Youth** <term>Yuvā</term> stage, while Viraja’s 'A' is the **Death-Sound** <term>Mṛtyu Svara</term>. With these characteristics, if the battle begins at that time, Devadatta will be victorious in every way.

Furthermore, regarding their time of victory: if at the time of day the sound 'U' arises, and the strength of 'U' and 'Va' is active, while 'Ra' and 'A' are the sounds for the two combatants, then in that condition, Viraja would defeat Devadatta.

However, for Devadatta himself, his own vowel is 'A'. In his period, the sound 'A' arises. If at that time the enemy's sound 'I' arises, that enemy sound is in the "Death" stage, granting victory to Devadatta. Devadatta’s **Life-Sound** <term>Jīva Svara</term> is 'O'. Through its strength, during the months of Vaisakha, Bhadrapada, and Margashirsha <note>approx. April-May, August-September, and November-December</note>, the 'A' sound arises. Therefore, because of the **Youth** status, the strength belongs to Devadatta in battle.

During the months of Ashadha, Shravana, and Ashwin <note>approx. June through October</note>, the 'A' sound arises. Because of the strength of the Life-Sound, these are known as the **Adult** months, granting victory to Devadatta. In the months of Chaitra and Pausha <note>approx. March-April and December-January</note>, the 'L' sound arises; because of the strength of the Life-Sound, it is unfavorable, and one should predict Devadatta's defeat in battle. In the months of Jyeshtha and Kartika <note>approx. May-June and October-November</note>, the 'E' sound arises. Because of the strength of the Life-Sound, this is a month of "Death" for Devadatta in battle. This is the monthly determination of strength and weakness.

Because Devadatta was born in the first quarter of the Revati constellation, he is situated in the sign of Pisces <note>Meena</note>; therefore, his zodiac sound is 'O'. From that, when the sound 'U' arises in the seasonal sounds, the **Youth** stage arises, and there is victory for him. When the sound 'I' arises in the seasonal sounds, there is victory. If the sound 'U' arises through his own gate, Devadatta suffers defeat. But if the 'E' sound—

# The Determination of Death and Victory through Vowel Strength

Death is determined by the strength of the vowel combined with the strength of the lord of the zodiac sign. For a person designated as Devadatta <note>A traditional placeholder name in Sanskrit texts, similar to "John Doe."</note>, the vowel associated with his birth asterism <note>original: "Nakshatra"</note> is **'A'**. By the logic of the mistress of the Ashwini asterism, the vowel for the sun’s progression <note>original: "Ayana"</note> is in the **Child** stage. The vowel for the Southern progression is in the **Youth** stage. A **Child** vowel in movement results in defeat or failure in battle, while a **Youth** vowel from the asterism leads to victory. Therefore, the <term>Aggregate Vowel</term> <note>original: "Pinda Svara," the total phonetic value of a name.</note> must be understood by this rule. For Devadatta, the vowel is **'A'**. During his yearly period, if the **'Ka'** sound or a **Death-vowel** arises, and it is aspected, death will occur in battle.

Similarly, with the rise of the vowel **'I'** in the **Child** stage, there is defeat. With the rise of the yearly vowel **'U'**, designated as the **Youth** stage, there is victory. With the rise of the yearly vowel **'A'**, there is total victory. With the yearly vowel **'O'**, there is defeat in battle.

Now, Devadatta’s "Combination Vowel" <note>original: "Yoga Svara"</note> is **'U'**. In its twelve-year cycle, under the designation of **Child**, there is defeat in battle. Under the vowel **'Am'** <note>The nasalized 'A'.</note>, in the twelve-year aggregate sound known as **Youth**, there is victory. Under the vowel **'I'**, in the twelve-year cycle where the combination vowel is known as **Young Adult**, there is total victory in battle. Under the vowel **'U'**, in the twelve-year cycle where the combination vowel strength is known as **Old**, there is defeat. Under the vowel **'E'**, in the rise of the twelve-year vowel known as **Dead**, death occurs. In this manner, strengths should be assigned in close combat. || 18 ||

Now, having considered the strength of all vowels, the text explains the total strength of all letters at all times. And at all times—

---

-> Just as all footprints enter the footprint of an elephant, <-
-> just as indeed all rivers flow into the oceans, <-
-> just as all the gods reside within the body of Vishnu <note>original: "Hari"</note>, <-
-> so do all letters reside within the family of vowels. || 21 ||
-> In the accomplishment of mantras, yantras, tantras, <-
-> and in yoga at all times, <-
-> one should perform downward-facing tasks <-
-> according to the strength of measures and vowels. || 22 ||
-> All actions, whether auspicious or inauspicious, <-
-> should be performed based on the strength of the powerful vowel. <-
-> It grants success in all undertakings, <-
-> especially during times of war. || 23 ||
-> In acts of killing, enchanting, paralyzing, causing hatred, <-
-> expulsion, and subjugation <note>original: "Marana, Mohana, Stambhana, Vidvesha, Ucchatana, Vasham" - the six traditional tantric karmas.</note>, <-
-> or in disputes and conflicts, <-
-> one should act according to the rise of the sound. || 24 ||
-> In matters of food, drink, and all such things, <-
-> strength lies in the beginning of the time. <-
-> The start of education and other such days <-
-> should occur during the rise of the Life-vowel. || 25 ||
-> In the building of palaces and gardens... <-

---

The strength of vowels is such that in all times—including twelve-year cycles, half-years, seasons, months, fortnights, days, and minute-units—one should apply these strengths to determine victory or defeat. || 20 ||

Again, the text supports this strength by saying, "Just as footprints..." meaning that by the strength of the vowels, the other letters [which are weaker] are subsumed. || 21 || What is the purpose of stating these things? It says, "**In the accomplishment of mantras and yantras...**" || 22 || "**The strong vowel...**" || 23 || "**Killing, enchanting...**" || 24 || "**Food, drink, and so on...**" || 25 || "**Palaces, gardens...**" || 26 ||

## Conclusion of the Description of the Sixteen Vowels

The lunar day <term>Tithi</term>, the weekday <term>Vara</term>, and the constellation <term>Nakshatra</term> are each explained separately. Where these three converge, the masters of the Vowel-Breath science should perform their calculations. ॥ 1 ॥ One should avoid a day where the strength of one's own name-vowel is defeated by the strength of the lunar day or weekday. One must abandon that day, as it will surely result in loss or harm. ॥ 2 ॥ By this union of vowels, the death of enemies or other outcomes are determined. Through this mystical diagram <term>Yantra</term>, the destruction of the day <note>meaning: the inauspiciousness of a specific time</note> is countered. ॥ 3 ॥ 

### The Vowel-Chart of Lunar Days, Weekdays, and Constellations

The stages of life, beginning with the **Child** stage, are determined by the number of the Sun <note>original: "Bhanu sankhya," likely referring to the number 12, as there are 12 sun-signs or 12 stages mentioned here</note>. They appear in the sequence of the rising breath, according to the measure of its own power. ॥ 4 ॥ In the duration of a **Ghatika** <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note>, based on the lord of the day, the month, the season, or the year, twelve distinct stages arise within the vowel. These range from the Child to the Dead stage over two days in the waxing and waning periods. The stages of the breath should be understood in this order. ॥ 5 ॥ Now, the measure of the vowel...

<margin>
Regarding the stages: There is a question as to how the twelve stages occur. The text answers: by the **Ghatika**. The duration of these stages is explained. Here, an example of the twelve stages for the Child vowel and others is given: On the first day of the bright fortnight of the month of Chaitra, the first 33 Ghatikas are analyzed. The first six stages are spoken of in measures of five Ghatikas each. In the second period, the seventh stage occurs. For the first lunar day <note>Pratipada</note>, the sequence is: **Child**, **Prince**, **Youth**, **Old**, **Dead**. 
Where the **Child** vowel is the seventh stage, it is called **Prince** <note>Kumara</note>, which brings peace. 
Where the desired outcome is sought, the **Youth** stage is best.
The **Old** stage is for sleep at night. 
The **Dead** stage brings burning or destruction. 
Thus, the **Child** stage and others are observed within the measure of 33 Ghatikas, divided into five-Ghatika intervals. 
In the daily routine of a person like Devadatta, the attainment of goals depends on the power of his vowel.
</margin>

<margin>
Na. Pa. Ja.
Ga. Ti. Bha.
52 ॥

In that context, the state known as **Youthful** <note>original: "Kumara"</note> will be a peace-making condition for a person named Devadatta <note>Devadatta is a traditional Sanskrit placeholder name, similar to "John Doe."</note>. By this example, the results of all desired actions should be determined. There are five "jewels" <note>likely referring to types of sounds or planetary influences</note> and twelve "phases" <note>original: "Kala"</note> of the vowel. Each phase consists of twelve divisions based on its own duration. These are measured in units of twenty-four minutes <term>Ghatika</term> which determine the measure of the "body" of the sound.

At these times, such as the "triple-six" period, there are many states of being. These include: **The Root** <note>original: "Mula"</note> and **The Child** (5); **One's Own** (6); **Effort** (7); **Impairment and Grief** (8); **Direction** (9); **Adorned** (10); and **Thus** (11). These constitute the **Sixfold States of Vowel-Vibration** <term>Shatsvaravastha</term> starting with the "Child-Vowel."

In this system, the experience of the vowel-breath must be calculated. By taking the elapsed units and looking at the remainder, one identifies the state at that specific moment. (4) The twelve sub-states are: 1. **Root**, 2. **Child**, 3. **Infant**, 4. **Thirsty**, 5. **Youthful**, 6. **Adolescent**, 7. **Power-Giver**, 8. **Crying**, 9. **Blameworthy**, 10. **Feverish**, 11. **Wandering**, and 12. **Dead**. These are the **States Born of Vowels** <term>Svaraja Avastha</term>. (1)

The auspicious or inauspicious results of these states are as follows: The "Infant" state brings great sorrow and grief, while the "Youthful" state is always peace-making and the "Adolescent" state brings auspiciousness. (6) In the state of "Effort," one gains strength and through the union of these factors, the mind's desires are fulfilled. (12) Through the "Broken Voice," "Wandering," and "Calm" states, one sees various results. (7)

The twelve physical states are: 1. **Impairment**, 2. **Grief**, 3. **Futile**, 4. **Failing Senses**, 5. **Sorrowful**, 6. **Shining**, 7. **Increasing**, 8. **Thundering**, 9. **Heat**, 10. **Action**, 11. **Agile**, and 12. **Dead**. (8)

Regarding the "Death-born" states: 1. **Stationary**, 2. **Bound**, 3. **Killer of Women**, 4. **Splendor**, 5. **Great Burning**, 6. **Visible**, 7. **Adorned**, 8. **Piercing**, 9. **Striking**, 10. **Death**. These are the twelve conditions of death. (9)

Thus, the one who knows the **Sixfold States of Vowel-Vibration** <term>Shatsvaravastha</term> is a master of the five vowels. By understanding these, one can predict the fruits of actions based on the names of people. (10) This concludes the section on the six states beginning with the "Child-Vowel."

Now, the directions: The vowel **'A'** is the master of the **East**. The vowel **'I'** rules the **South**. The vowel **'U'** is known in the **West**, and the vowel **'E'** is in the **Center**. (11) Whichever direction a vowel rises in, that marks the first direction. One must avoid the **fifth direction** from that starting point in all undertakings, and especially at the time of travel. (12) If one acts in the direction where the vowel-lord is weakened, there will be loss of wealth and failure; of this, there is no doubt.

### ॥ The Diagram of Directional Enjoyment ॥

Regarding the placement of the vowels starting with 'A': (11) As for avoiding the "fifth direction," since the vowels have a specific order, the fifth direction from one's own powerful vowel is considered forbidden. This is the author's intent. For example, if for Devadatta the vowel **'U'** is the strongest, and 'U' is situated in the West, then counting from the West, the **South** becomes the fifth direction. Therefore, Devadatta should always avoid the South for travel, war, or marriage. One should not rely on a direction that "eats" the name-vowel. (12)

This explains the results of queries regarding the friendship of vowels. (3)

# The Chart of the Enjoyment of Vowel-Names

# ॥ The Chart of Directional Vowels ॥

| 0 | East: Lunar Mansion | 0 |
|---|---|---|
| NE: Empty | N: Full | S: Auspicious |
| 0 | 1 | 0 |
| 0 | NW: Victorious | 0 |

<note>The terms 'Empty' (Rikta), 'Full' (Purna), 'Auspicious' (Bhadra), and 'Victorious' (Jaya) refer to traditional classifications of lunar days (Tithis) that govern different types of activities.</note>

---

Regarding the start of the lunar day <term>Tithyadi</term>: At the commencement of a lunar day, the vowel-sound associated with that date is determined by the systems. For example, if five Ghatikas <note>2 hours</note> and twenty-seven units have passed on the fifth day of the bright half of the month of Chaitra, I shall now describe the result for the vowel-sound active at that rising sign <term>Lagna</term>. (1)

The method for this calculation is as follows: take the elapsed time of the lunar day in Ghatikas. For example, on the first day of the bright half of Chaitra, let the vowel be 'A' (5). If fifteen Ghatikas have passed, the result is nine hundred (900). When divided by the divisor 317 <note>original: "bharaṇaya vihṛtāni"</note>, the quotient is the vowel-state. If the remainder is 266, it corresponds to the third vowel 'I'. If a person named Devadatta <note>A standard placeholder name</note> asks a question, I will provide the result based on the rising vowel of that lunar day, the hour, and the time-vowel according to the rules of the **The Chart of Universal Auspiciousness** <term>Sarvatobhadra-chakra</term>. (2)

Take the number of elapsed Ghatikas of the lunar day and convert them into minutes <term>Pala</term>. Divide this by 327 <note>original: "ṛkṣavi 327 hṛte"</note>; the remainder indicates the vowel-state active at that moment. (3) The result will manifest for whatever purpose the question was asked. If no specific purpose is mentioned, then the result applies generally to good or bad fortune. (3)

**The Rise of the Child** <note>original: "Balodaya"</note>: When a question is asked during this state for the purpose of profit, it yields only small gains. For a king, it indicates disease; in a great war, it suggests loss or doubt. (4)

**The Rise of the Youth** <note>original: "Kumarodaya"</note>: During this time, profits will be plentiful. In battle, it brings the destruction of enemies and victory. For travel, it grants success in all directions. (5)

**The Rise of the Mature** <note>original: "Praudhodaya"</note>: This state is excellent for studying the scriptures, for mathematics, or for moments of quick action. In combat, one becomes a slayer of enemies, and any journey undertaken will be entirely successful. (6)

**The Rise of the Old** <note>original: "Vriddhodaya"</note>: No profit shall come of this. For those with hair <note>meaning those seeking growth/vitality</note>, it does not increase. In battle, one meets with defeat, and one who departs on a journey does not return. (7)

**The Rise of the Dead** <note>original: "Mrityodaya"</note>: Whatever task is the subject of the question, the failure of that task must be predicted. In war or other courageous deeds, there is fear and ruin for the asker. One should advise the abandonment of such efforts based on these indicators.

Now, regarding the "Child-State" for kings and others: If Devadatta asks a question on behalf of someone else—for example, "Will this work be successful for Yajñadatta?"—then one should determine the result by focusing on the strength of Yajñadatta’s name-vowel and the direction of the "Child-State." If no specific name is mentioned regarding profit, then determine the result based on Devadatta's own name-vowel...

# ॥ Na - Ja ॥ ॥ Na - Ti ॥

Regarding the directions of the vowels beginning with the "Child" state <term>Baladi-dik</term>, one should understand the designated directions based on the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term>. Now, let us discuss the "Child-Sun" <term>Baladitya</term>.

The text states:
The Rising of the Child (4);
The Rising of the Youth (5);
The Rising of Awakening (6);
The Rising of the Old Man (7);
The Rising of Death (8).

<note>These five states represent the "age" or strength of the vowel currently active in the practitioner's breath. Like the sun, the breath-vowel begins weak (Child), reaches peak strength (Youth/Awakening), and then fades (Old Age/Death).</note>

Now, the text describes the results of inquiries <term>Prashna-phalam</term> based on the sequence and nature of the vowels:

The "Odd Vowels" <term>Ojah-svara</term> are **A**, **U**, and **O**. These three are considered uneven or masculine (1). Others are "Even Vowels" <term>Sama-svara</term>, which are **I** and **Lṛ**. These are considered feminine. When a masculine vowel is rising in the breath, men possess strength. When a feminine vowel is rising, women possess strength. Therefore, success in any task <term>karya-siddhi</term> is achieved when the gender of the person matches the nature of the rising vowel (10).

### The Eight-Fold Calculation for Pregnancy
When an inquiry is made regarding pregnancy—asking whether the child will be a son or a daughter—one must observe the breath at that moment. If the vowels **A**, **U**, or **O** are rising, it should be predicted that a son will be born. If at that time **I** or **Lṛ** are rising, and a person asks about their purpose, the seeker should be told the outcome will be auspicious and feminine (8).

The Odd Vowels are masculine; know the even ones to be feminine. When an individual’s gender matches the vowel’s rise, they obtain strength and success (9).

In matters of pregnancy, a masculine vowel indicates a son; a feminine vowel indicates a daughter. If the breath is in a "paired" or transition state, twins may be indicated. In cases of "lost" items <term>Nashta</term> or transitions <term>Sankrame</term>, death or loss may be predicted (10).

When the "Day-Pulse" <term>Dyu-nadi</term> and the vowels are both masculine, it favors men. When both are even, it favors women. If there is a mix between the two, predict a combination of male and female in the womb (11).

Thus concludes the various types of inquiries in the "Instantaneous Vowel-Chart" <term>Tatkalike Swarachakra</term> of the Yamala tradition. (65)

---

<margin>This</margin> <note>original: "iyam"</note> pertains to inquiries about pregnancy. If a feminine/even vowel is rising, one must declare a daughter. If the vowel rise is "lost" <term>nashta</term>—calculated by the time of the lunar mansion—the expert should declare the loss of the fetus in the womb. At the transition <term>sankrame</term> or the end of a masculine vowel rise, one must speak of the "death of the mother's purpose" (20).

Now, the method for the "Paired Vowel-Rise" is explained: The Day-Pulse <term>Dyu-nadi</term> refers to the vowel rising within the specific five-<term>Ghatika</term> <note>two-hour</note> interval. If both the Day-Pulse and the momentary vowel are uneven (masculine), then a son is predicted. If both are feminine, a daughter is predicted. If one is masculine and one is feminine, then both a son and a daughter (twins) will be born. If one of these is "lost" or weakened by the influence of a specific lunar mansion, then that portion of the wealth or the child is lost (11). This is the method for the pulse-vowel.
