<meta>This page continues the technical instructions on Svarodaya (the science of breath), moving from the general effects of the elements to specific divination techniques for success, pregnancy, and the arrival of travelers based on "Full" (active) and "Void" (inactive) breath states.</meta>

# || 42 || 43 || Now he speaks of another [rule].

Regarding the "void" <note>original: "shunya"; the nostril that is not currently breathing</note> at the beginning: if one goes to the empty side and asks a question, or if they sit in the place of the "full" breath <note>original: "purna"; the nostril through which the breath is actively flowing</note>, then success in all matters is achieved. In questions regarding life or death, gain or loss, victory or defeat, success is certain. Whether it concerns living or <gloss>death</gloss> dying, one's own health, gain from an enemy, one's own victory, or the <insert>defeat</insert> of an enemy—success in every such query should be known. If one goes to the "full" side at the start and asks, but if the five questions are asked while the person is in the "void" position, one should declare the outcome accordingly: life as death, death as life, gain as loss, and victory as defeat. Thus says the Teacher of the Left Breath. || 44 || Now, concerning pregnancy:

## The businessman, the rogue, the debater, the enemy, or the half-blind; the angry master, and the thief—
## For these, the "full" breath is a bringer of good fortune. || 44 || When the Sun <note>the right nostril</note> moves unevenly, it is not equal
<margin>mo-</margin> ## to the movement in the Moon <note>the left nostril</note>. In the natural flow of the breath, if it is not given at the proper time, it will not be otherwise <note>i.e., the result will fail</note>. || 45 ||

If one goes to the "void" <gloss>asks</gloss> and enters the "void," if <gloss>success</gloss> he stays there, one should know for certain that success in all matters and so on [will follow]. || 44 || Regarding the question of pregnancy: as the messenger [stands], if the breath is "full," a son is born. If in the "void," a daughter; in a combination, twins; but if the breath transitions during the query, the pregnancy may be lost. || 45 ||

Now he explains the science of breath regarding the pregnancy query. Regarding the query of the womb: whether the breath flows in the left or the right, or upwards, or in front, and so on—by these characteristics, if the questioner is in the "full" state and asks, "Is there a child? Will it be a son or a daughter?" then because it is "full," the pregnancy is confirmed. If the questioner is in the "void," then one should say the child will be a daughter. Regarding "twins in the son [position]": if the question is first made in the "full" state, but the query [ends] in the "void," then one should predict twins. Here, some [authorities] say: if the question starts in the "void" and moves to the "full," then it indicates a girl first, then a boy. By the power of practicing these breaths, one can understand the whole world. If the breath is flowing in the left or the right and then suddenly crosses over <note>original: "sankramana"; the transition of breath from one nostril to the other</note> during the question, one should predict the death of the mother or the protector of the womb. || 45 || <margin>Ga</margin>

# Whether one arrives or departs, if seen in the left breath, it is a sign of happiness. In departure
# or in speech elsewhere, if the breath is dead <note>inactive</note>, one should speak of it there. || 46 || When the rising Moon
# is equal with the meeting breaths, then in the aim without qualities, the
# reverse is forgotten. || 47 ||

Now another matter regarding the question of arrival. Concerning the question of someone who has gone away: by considering the full breath of the person who has gone, one knows the good or bad fortune. One should speak of the arrival or the return based on the query. If the breath flows in the right or the left, then the person will arrive. In that very place, if there is a happy breath, one should say they are happy there. If the Wind element <note>original: "vayu-tattva"</note> flows, they have gone elsewhere. If the Fire element <note>original: "anala-tattva"</note> flows, one should speak of distress or theft. Here too, one should speak of gain or death based on the good or bad signs. || 46 || Now another [rule] regarding the meeting of the two [breaths]. || 47 || When the rising Moon <note>the flow in the left nostril</note> is active, if the Solar breath <note>the right nostril</note> is also flowing, or if the Sun breath is flowing and then sets <note>ceases</note>, then a meeting of the two occurs. This is "the reverse is forgotten": when the Sun is flowing, it is sunrise; when the Moon flows, it is sunset; then many obstacles occur. Or, if the Solar breath is active during a marriage, there will be distress in the marriage. Or, if the lunar breath is active at the time of rising or setting, it always results in much distress. || 47 ||

Now he speaks of another [rule] regarding the "cycles" <note>original: "paksha"; usually meaning a lunar fortnight, but here referring to the duration of dominance of one nostril</note> of the left and right breath. One should speak of good fortune when the breath is as it should be. If the breath moves into the opposite side, bypassing the auspicious results, a different outcome occurs. If it stays on the wrong side for one cycle, then disease <term>Vyadhi; physical illness</term> shall occur. If it stays for two cycles, there is total loss. If it stays on the wrong side for three cycles, death occurs. || 48 ||

Now he speaks of the knowledge of the elements <note>original: "tattvas"; the five fundamental states of matter: Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Ether</note>, such as Earth and others, in the left or right [nostril]. The Earth element flows to a length of eight fingers. In this practice, the student of breath should make the breath reach sixteen fingers for Water. When it flows in either the left or the right, one should test it by placing a probe at the nostril to see which element is flowing. When the breath flows eight fingers and carries a scent, it is the Earth element. In this way, through the observation and knowledge of the others, one should state the results. || 49 || Now, regarding the questioner's desired object, it should be known through the element [flowing at that moment]:

> If the breath stays on the wrong side for one cycle, disease occurs; from two cycles, the destruction of one's destiny.
> If it stays on the wrong side for three cycles, death occurs for certain. || 48 ||
> In the left or the right, Earth is eight fingers long.
> Water is sixteen fingers, and Fire is four fingers. || 49 ||
> Wind is twelve fingers long, but Ether has no measurable length. || 50 ||
> Earth is yellow, Water is white, Fire <note>original: "Dhananjaya"; here a name for the fire element</note> is red. || 51 ||
> Ether is the color of a dark blue cloud. These are the five powers. || 50 || <note>Repetition of verse number 50 in original manuscript</note> 

The Earth power is a square; in its center is the [seed syllable] *Va*... <note>The text here is slightly garbled or technical regarding the mystical diagrams of the elements</note>. In the center of the Water element is the shape of a six-petaled lotus, and the circle of Varuna <note>the deity of Water</note> is white. || 51 ||

Now he speaks of the strength of Earth and the others. [Regarding the phrase] "Earth is yellow": in a question about a specific object, one should declare the strength or result based on the colors of Earth and the other elements. This is easy to understand. || 50 || Now the Teacher speaks of the different names and types of the five elements. [Regarding the phrase] "The yellow power is a square": these different forms of the five elements are declared in the Tantras <note>original: "Agama"; traditional scriptural texts</note>. Here, the seed-mantras <note>original: "mantra-vija"</note> for the Earth and other elements are given. One should apply these to the various sets of the five elements. Depending on the form [of the breath], one should give the answer to the questioner.

In a question regarding one's thoughts, if the Earth element is present, one should describe a yellow, square power. If it is the Water element, one should describe a white, six-petaled power. If it is red and triangular, it is the Fire element. If it is green and eight-sided <note>octagonal</note>, it is the Wind element. If it is smoky and circular, it is the Ether element. Furthermore, wherever a particular purity or vibration occurs, one should give the answer to the question accordingly. 

Furthermore, in the Science of Yoga <note>original: "Yoga Shastra"</note>, through the distinction between the Left (Moon) and Right (Sun) breaths, the Moon remains for the growth of all living beings in the mechanism of the body. The Sun is in the lower part <note>or represents the consuming aspect</note>. One should avoid the time of the "vulture" or "jackal" <note>likely referring to inauspicious astrological moments</note>. The Moon is the feminine source; the Sun is said to be the masculine. Both of these are the creation and destruction of living beings; there is no doubt about this. This is easy to understand and not of ultimate purpose [for the beginner]. || 53 ||

[The Fire-circle] is red, triangular in shape, and resembles a wheel. In the middle of that, the Air-circle is formless and green. || 52 || In the middle of the Ether-circle, it is circular and smoke-colored. This is the distinction between the five elements <note>original: "pancatattveshu"; referring to Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Ether</note> as stated by the ancient masters. || 53 ||

Perform acts of killing, enchanting, paralyzing, sowing discord, driving away, subjugation, <term>presharana; the act of sending or dispatching</term>, drawing near, and causing agitation while the Sun <note>original: "bhanuna"; referring to the breath flowing through the right nostril</note> is active. || 54 ||

Acts of peace-making, nourishment <note>original: "paustikam"; rituals for prosperity or health</note>, the preparation of divine medicines and elixirs, and the practice of Yoga and similar works should be performed when the Moon <note>original: "nishakare"; referring to the breath flowing through the left nostril</note> is active. || 55 ||

Those men who always act according to the breaths of the Moon and the Sun possess knowledge of the past and the future, which remains forever in the palm of their hands. || 56 ||

Thus ends the second chapter, titled "The Movement of the Breath" <note>original: "Hamsacharo"; literally 'The Gait of the Swan,' a metaphor for the rhythmic flow of inhalation and exhalation</note>, in the *Brahmayamaliya Svarodaya*.

The distinctions that have been spoken of in the five breaths are the source of all results born of Time. They have states of infancy, youth, maturity, old age, and death. Through the method of the Guru's instruction, one understands these distinctions and sub-distinctions. When this knowledge is established, the illusions of the three periods of time <note>past, present, and future</note> are definitely resolved. || 1 ||

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded a chapter on the science of breath (Svarodaya) and transitioned toward practical astrological tools. This page begins the detailed explanation of the Sarvatobhadra Chakra, a complex grid used for divination and forecasting, often referred to as the "Lamp of the Three Worlds."</meta>

"Now, therefore, I shall fully explain..." following the sequence after the *Hamsachara* <note>original: "hamsachara"; refers to the movement of the vital breath or "swan-motion" described in the preceding section</note>, I shall now describe the chart named *Sarvatobhadra* <note>original: "sarvatobhadra"; literally "auspicious on all sides"</note>. What are its qualities? It is the *Trailokya-Dipaka* <note>original: "trailokya-dipaka"; the Lamp of the Three Worlds</note>—it illuminates and reveals the three worlds just as a lamp reveals objects like a pot or a cloth. This famous chart is well-established in the world; through it, one can understand the influence of islands and nations, providing immediate realization and a means to deliberate upon the signs of good and bad fortune.

### Verse 1: The Introduction

**Now, I shall describe the construction of the chart. The instruction "Draw ten lines..." is easily understood.**

**The Vowels: Starting with 'A' and so on.** One should sit facing the East and write in the chart. There, the vowels <note>original: "svara"; refers to the sixteen vowels of the Sanskrit alphabet</note> starting with 'A' and ending with 'Am' should be written. In the first enclosure of squares, beginning from the Northeast <note>original: "ishana"; the corner presided over by Shiva</note> and continuing through the intermediate directions in sequence. The order is as follows: the vowel 'A' in the Northeast corner, 'Ā' in the Southeast, 'I' in the Southwest, and 'Ī' in the Northwest. Then 'U' in the Northeast corner again, below the 'A'; 'Ū' in the Southeast, below 'Ā', and so on for the sixteen vowels across the four corners.

->**Now, therefore, I shall fully describe the chart called the Lamp of the Three Worlds.**<-
->**Famous as the Sarvatobhadra, it grants immediate results. || 1 ||**<-
->**By drawing ten vertical lines and ten horizontal lines,**<-
->**A chart of eighty-one squares is produced; of this there is no doubt. || 2 ||**<-
->**The vowels starting with 'A' should be placed in the squares beginning in the Northeast,**<-
->**And following the path of creation, these sixteen vowels should be placed in four rotations. || 3 ||**<-
->**The lunar mansions starting with Krittika and the letters starting with 'Ha'**<-
->**Should be written in sequence, seven by seven, totaling twenty-eight. || 4 ||**<-
->**The letters A, Va, Ka, Ha, Da are in the East; Ma, Ta, Pa, Ra, Ta are in the South;**<-
->**Na, Ya, Bha, Ja, Kha are in the West; Ga, Sa, Da, Cha, La are in the North. || 5 ||**<-
->**The signs starting with Aries and the letters Ya, Ra, La, Va are placed by the wise in order,**<-
->**Along with the twelve zodiac signs, following the path of creation. || 6 ||**<-
<margin>Verse 24</margin>

One must place these in the squares. While writing the vowels in this manner, the writing of vowels should follow four rotations <note>original: "chatur-bhrama"; four circular movements or layers within the grid</note> according to the path of creation. || 3 ||

**After writing the vowels, the text describes the writing of the lunar mansions <note>term: Nakshatras; the 28 mansions of the moon used in Vedic astrology</note> starting with Krittika.** Inside the corners of the vowel-squares, one should write seven mansions in each of the cardinal directions. Specifically, seven are in the first row of the East (7). After Krittika <note>original: "krittika"; the first Nakshatra in this system, associated with the Pleiades</note>, one writes in the square below it, and so on. In this way, twenty-eight squares are to be known across the four directions. One should write Krittika and the others using the numerical markers. Seven starting with Magha are written in the South; seven starting with Anuradha <note>original: "maitra"; another name for the Anuradha Nakshatra</note> are written in the West; and seven starting with Dhanishtha are written in the North. || 4 ||

**Now, the text describes the writing in the squares below the lunar mansions. Regarding "A-Va-Ka-Ha-Da":** These five letters are to be written in the five squares below the Eastern lunar mansions. The five starting with Ma-Ta-Pa-Ra-Ta are written below the Southern lunar mansions. Na-Ya-Bha-Ja-Kha are placed in the five squares below the Western mansions. And the five letters Ga-Sa-Da-Cha-La are written below the Northern mansions. || 5 ||

**Now, the third row starting from the East—the empty squares to be written in—is described.** In the second row of squares, one should write the zodiac signs <note>term: Rashis; the twelve signs of the zodiac</note>. Write the three signs starting with Aries <note>original: "vri-sha-adi"; shorthand for the first signs</note> in the Eastern squares; the three starting with Leo in the Southern squares; the three starting with Scorpio in the Western squares; and the three starting with Aquarius in the Northern squares. || 6 ||

**Now, regarding what is to be written in the remaining squares:** In the remaining four squares in the four directions, and in the central square, one should write the five types of lunar days <note>term: Tithis; lunar days categorized as Nanda, Bhadra, Jaya, Rikta, and Purna</note> in sequence, starting with *Pratipada* <note>original: "nanda-adi"; the first day of the lunar fortnight</note>.

### The Placement of Lunar Days and Weekdays

<margin>27</margin>

One should write the first category of lunar days in the eastern cell of the grid. The second should be written in the southern cell, the third in the western, the fourth in the northern, and the fifth in the center. In the same way, the second set of five—starting with the sixth lunar day—should be written in the same order. Then, the third set of five—starting with the eleventh—should be written in that same sequence. By doing this, the fifth, tenth, and fifteenth <note>The full moon or new moon</note> lunar days are written in the **marmas** <term>marma</term> <note>original: "arma" or "marma"; refers to the sensitive "vital points" or the center of the grid</note>. 

Regarding the days of the week, they are to be written starting with Mars and following the sequence of the seven planets. The weekdays are drawn into the cycle alongside the **Nanda** and other categories of lunar days. In the cells where Nanda and the others are written, one should also write Mars and the other planets. Specifically, one should write Mars below the Nanda days. Mercury should be written in the cell for the **Bhadra** days. Jupiter is to be written in the **Jaya** cell. Venus is written in the **Rikta** cell. Saturn should be placed in the cell for the **Purna** days. Furthermore, the Sun is placed with Mars, and the Moon is written in the cell with Mercury and the Bhadra days. When one finishes writing the days of the week, the construction of the *Sarvatobhadra* <note>The "Auspicious-on-all-sides" chart</note> is considered complete.

<margin>27</margin>
**Verse 7:** In these cells, the five categories of lunar days starting with Nanda are placed. The seven days of the week are also to be written, beginning with Mars and the Sun in sequence.

**Verse 8:** Mars and the Sun are placed in the **Nanda** category; Mercury and the Moon in the **Bhadra**; Jupiter is said to be in the **Jaya**; and similarly, Venus is in the **Rikta**.

**Verse 9:** Saturn is placed in the **Purna** category. Thus is the chart constructed. This is the full extension of the **Sarvatobhadra** <term>Sarvatobhadra</term> <note>The "complete auspicious" grid used for complex astrological forecasting</note> as proclaimed by me.

### The Theory of Planetary Aspects (Drishti)

**Verse 10:** The Sun and Mars have a "path-gaze" <note>original: "adhvadrishti"; looking toward the 3rd and 9th positions</note>. Mercury and Venus have a "squinting-gaze" <note>original: "kekara"; looking toward the 4th and 8th positions</note>. Jupiter has a "level-gaze" <note>original: "samadrishti"; looking toward the 5th and 9th positions</note>, while Saturn and Rahu have a "downward-gaze" <note>looking toward the 7th and 8th positions</note>.

**Verse 11:** A planet in its sign of debilitation looks upward; one in its sign of exaltation looks downward. When a planet is in its own level rank, its gaze is steady. Thus, the "gaze" <term>drishti</term> is described as being of three types.

**Verse 12:** Malefic planets make the stars they influence malefic; the remaining are auspicious planets. The Sun, Mars, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu are cruel. Mercury is considered cruel if associated with a malefic, and the Moon is cruel when it is waning.

**Verse 13:** From the position where a planet is situated, three types of "piercing" <term>vedha</term> <note>original: "vedha"; a technical term for the line of sight or influence a planet casts across the grid</note> occur. Due to the influence of the planet's gaze, these are directed toward the Left, the Front, and the Right.

---

This is the **Sarvatobhadra-prastara** <term>Sarvatobhadra-prastara</term> <note>The mathematical expansion or arrangement of the auspicious grid</note> as proclaimed by me. Following the ancient schools of architecture and according to what was spoken by Brahma, it is easy to understand.

**Verse 19:** <note>Number out of sequence in original manuscript</note> Now, regarding the deliberation on the chart, the division between malefic and benefic planets is explained. 

The malefic planets are the Sun, Mars, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu—this is easy to understand. Now, the method of "piercing" <term>vedha</term> through planetary gaze is explained. From the lunar date or the star <note>Nakshatra</note> where a planet is situated, a triple-piercing occurs through the planet's gaze. Because the gaze of the planets is threefold, with one gaze they pierce the front, with one they pierce the right, and with one they pierce the left.

One enjoys what is to be enjoyed, as well as the beloved, [but] one is afflicted by cruel planets. || 44 || One should effortfully avoid [inauspicious times] during the cycles of auspicious planets like Venus and others. || 124 || When a planet is moving retrograde, it has a "rightward" aspect; when it is fast-moving, it has a "leftward" aspect. When moving at a medium pace, the aspect is considered "central." This should be known for the five planets starting with Mars. <note>The five planets are Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn</note> || 125 ||

**|| 94 ||** Now, the author explains the characteristics of the three types of "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> <note>original: "vedha"; an astrological concept where a planet's position "pierces" or influences a specific point in the grid</note> through the three types of "aspects" <term>Drishti</term>. Regarding the phrase "When retrograde, it is rightward," the three types of aspects occur individually for the five planets starting with Mars. This is because these five planets move through the cycle in three ways: they may be fast-moving <term>Shighraga</term>, slow-moving <term>Mandaga</term>, or retrograde. Through these three types of movement/aspect, a three-fold piercing is known.

There is a specific rule here regarding the three-fold piercing in the world: a planet is endowed with a four-fold piercing <note>piercing in four directions simultaneously</note> through its inherent nature. Some teachers say that for the sake of kings and others, one should examine this balance particularly. One should not calculate the years and seasons there [hastily]; rather, when a planet is in its highest state, it has a special significance. In this regard, there are seven verses in the texts that explain the nature of "piercing." They mention the pairs of the Moon, as well as the signs of Gemini, Virgo, and the star Swati. One must understand the entire system—Ashvini, Rohini, Abhijit, Krittika, and the five-fold stars including Chitra and Revati—as they are placed in their respective positions. **|| 15 ||**

<page-num>28</page-num>
<header>N. A. J. || Commentary on Birth || 28</header>

<margin>28</margin> Now the teacher explains the characteristics of high, medium, slow, fast, and retrograde motion, as distinct from [the standard] planetary movement. || First, planets located within the Sun’s orb are [effectively] the Sun. When they are released from the Sun’s conjunction, they rise. When they are in the second sign <note>The zodiac is divided into twelve signs or 'rasis'</note> after the Sun's current position, the five planets—Mars and the others <note>The five non-luminary planets: Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn</note>—are considered fast-moving <term>Shighragati</term>. When situated in the third or fourth signs from the Sun, they are average <term>Samagati</term>, meaning their speed is uniform. When the Sun is in the fourth sign [relative to the planet], they become slow <term>Mandagati</term>, meaning their speed is less than their average daily motion. In the fifth, sixth, and seventh signs, they become retrograde <term>Vakragati</term>. Specifically, when the Sun is in the fifth or sixth signs, Mars and the others have five types of retrograde motion. Some say that from the seventh to eighth positions relative to the Sun, they are extremely retrograde <term>Ativakra</term> in the seventh house. In the ninth, tenth, and eleventh, they have a crooked motion <term>Kutila</term>. When the Sun is in the twelfth or eleventh signs, the planets become fast-moving again. By this, through retrograde, slow, and average motion, the "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> aspect becomes threefold. This is why it was said that in the cycle, the aspect is to the right—

> When released by the Sun, they rise; when entering the Sun, they set. Mars and the others, when in the second sign [from the Sun], are shimmering with light. || 18 ||
> They are known as average in the third sign, and slow in the fourth from the Sun. In the fifth and sixth, they are retrograde, and in the seventh, they are extremely retrograde. || 19 ||
> In the ninth and tenth from the Sun, a crooked motion arises. In the twelfth and eleventh from the Sun, they regain their fast speed again. || 19 ||
> When planets reach the Sun, they travel into invisibility again among people. || 18 ||
> Rahu and Ketu <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon, considered "shadow planets"</note> are always retrograde; the Moon and Sun are always fast-moving. Being of a single nature in their movement, their aspects are always of three kinds. || 19 ||
> Malefics in retrograde are extremely malefic; benefics in retrograde are extremely auspicious. When in their natural state, benefics and malefics are fast-moving. || 20 ||

---

—and so on for the aspects. || 16 || 17 || 18 || Now, he explains the special motions regarding the nature of planets that are otherwise incomprehensible. Regarding "Rahu and Ketu are always retrograde," it means Rahu and Ketu always move backward <note>Astronomically, the lunar nodes move in the opposite direction of the planets through the zodiac</note>. The Moon and Sun are always fast-moving; the Moon and Sun never move retrograde. For the Sun and Moon, fast motion is their single nature; for Rahu and Ketu, retrograde is their single nature. Because of this, they always possess the three types of aspects. Through these three aspects, they look toward the front. Some say that however the motion is, so too is the aspect. By this, the Sun and Moon are always fast, so their aspect is "leftward"; Rahu and Ketu are always retrograde, so their aspect is "rightward." Thus, in the movement of the wheel, those that are fast are seen to stay [in their aspect], while Rahu and Ketu are eternally retrograde and the others are eternally fast, appearing equal in form. || 18 || Regarding those planets: "Malefics in retrograde..." and so on. Benefics are distinguished by their fast motion; malefics are hindered by fast motion. Malefics are "sinners" who perform cruel and wicked deeds by nature. Benefics are of auspicious motion and perform only good. If those malefic planets become retrograde, they become exceedingly cruel. If benefic planets become retrograde, they become extremely auspicious. Benefics when fast...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary motion and begins a detailed section on "piercing" (Vedha), an astrological concept where planets, stars, and specific sounds of the alphabet interact within a grid or "Chakra." <page-num>28</page-num>

If planets are present there, they become natural in their disposition. When they are in fast motion <term>Shighra</term>, they act according to their primary nature. However, when they are in retrograde motion <term>Vakra</term>, they do not act in the same way. Even "cruel" or inauspicious planets <note>original: "krūrā"; referring to planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, when moving at high speeds, have their harsh nature reduced. Mars <note>original: "Mangala"</note>, however, is of a lower nature; it gives results according to its inherent character. Similarly, in a birth chart, benefic planets bring auspiciousness and success in governance, while malefic planets <note>original: "pāpā"</note> bring distress. During its own planetary period <term>Dasha</term>, a planet can cause one to wander here and there. || 20 ||

Now the author explains the concept of "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> <note>Vedha refers to the astrological "aspect" or "influence" one element exerts on another within a specialized grid</note>.

Regarding the vowels starting with 'A' and so on: When a vowel is used, the piercing of the self occurs through the nasal sound <term>Anusvara</term> and the aspirate sound <term>Visarga</term> <note>The Anusvara (ṃ) and Visarga (ḥ) are specific phonetic markers in Sanskrit</note>. These two should be known as a double-piercing. Because they are pronounced with the vowel 'A', it is impossible to sustain their sound without that 'A'. || 21 ||

Now, a specific rule regarding the piercing of the letter 'Va' <note>original: "vavarṇa"</note> is explained: In the chart, the 'Va' from the labial group <note>The 'pa-varga' or group of consonants starting with P</note> is mentioned, and the 'Sa' from the 'Ya' group. If these are placed anywhere:

-> Two by two, the vowels starting with 'A' form a pair; in one piercing, both are affected. <-
-> The piercing of the self-vowel includes the nasal marker and the aspirate marker. || 21 || 'Ba' and 'Va', 'Sha' and 'Sa', <-
-> 'Sha' and 'Kha' are also to be known here as mutually interchangeable. Through one, both are affected, <-
-> whether the result is auspicious or inauspicious. || 22 || The letters 'Dha', 'Da', and 'Ka', <-
-> and similarly 'Dha', 'Sha', and 'Dha' <note>The text repeats variants of these phonetic clusters</note>. These triads are pierced as one, <-
-> in the proper order. || 23 || 'Dha', 'Da', and 'Ka' are linked to the piercing of the constellation Ashlesha; <-
-> 'Dha', 'Sha', and 'Dha' are linked to Purva-ashadha and Uttara-bhadrapada in the north. || 24 || <-
-> The pairs of vowels starting with 'A'—in one piercing, both are affected. The vowels joined with other vowels <-
-> and the nasal and aspirate markers are thus pierced. || 25 || <-

In a name, the 'Va' from the labial group, the palatal 'Sha', and the 'Va' from the 'Ka' group <note>referring to 'Kha'</note>—the piercing of these should be understood through "equidistant piercing" <term>Sama-vedha</term>. || 22 || Regarding the letters 'Dha', 'Da', and 'Ka': these letters correspond to the quarters <term>Charanas</term> of the stars <term>Nakshatras</term>. The sounds 'Dha', 'Da', and 'Ka' correspond to the constellations Purva-ashadha, Uttara-ashadha, and others. Within these quarters, the letters are placed in the chart <note>The Sarvatobhadra Chakra is a grid where every letter of the alphabet is mapped to a specific star and direction</note>. Among them, three letters are pierced simultaneously through the form of the piercing. || 23 || Thus it is said: when a planet enters the initial star, 'Dha', 'Da', and 'Ka' are pierced. In the house of Ashlesha, these letters are pierced; in the house of Purva-ashadha, 'Dha', 'Sha', and 'Dha' are pierced; and in Uttara-bhadrapada, the triad is pierced. || 24 || 25 ||

Regarding the "Corners" <term>Kola</term>: here, in the four corners, the stars are situated.

The piercing of the four letters beginning with the first occurs in full sequence. || 26 ||

Specifically, in the Northeast corner <note>original: "aiśāna"</note> are the stars Bharani and Krittika; in the Southeast <note>original: "āgneye"</note> is Magha; in the Southwest <note>original: "nairṛtye"</note> are Vishakha and Anuradha; and in the Northwest <note>original: "vāyavye"</note> are Shravana and Dhanishtha. When a planet is stationed in a quarter of these stars, it "pierces" the vowels. A planet in the quarter of Bharani or Krittika pierces the vowels A, U, Ta, and Ka. One in Ashlesha or Magha pierces Ā, Ū, La, and Ka. One in Vishakha or Anuradha pierces I, Ṛ, E, and Ka. One in Shravana or Dhanishtha pierces Ī, Ḷ, Ai. The four corners thus pierce the corresponding dates <term>Tithis</term>.

**Now, another interpretation** regarding the "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> <note>In this context, Vedha refers to the invisible lines of influence or "aspects" connecting planets to specific letters, signs, or sounds in an astrological grid</note> of the four vowels beginning with 'A'—namely A, Ā, I, and Ī. The piercing of these four is unique because it is not possible to achieve a piercing through a single numerical unit <note>original: "ekāṅkenāpi"</note>. The piercing of the 'U' sounds and others occurs in conjunction with the letters of the "Yala" group <note>referring to the semi-vowels Y, R, L, V; here specifically Y and L</note>. This is why the method of piercing is explained in this manner. || 26 ||

**Now, the author describes the results of these piercings** || Regarding the verse "Piercing by one or more cruel planets": Saturn, the Sun, Rahu, Ketu, and Mars are the five "malefic" or "cruel" planets <term>pāpā</term> <note>original: "śany-arka-rāhu-ketavaḥ pañca pāpā"; traditionally includes Mars (Maṅgala) to make five</note>. If any one of these pierces a person’s name, vowel, letter, zodiac sign, or lunar mansion, a state of **anxiety** <term>udvega</term> arises; the person becomes troubled in mind. If two planets cause a piercing, **fear** <term>bhaya</term> occurs. || This fear might come from fire, or from some indicative source. If three planets cause a piercing, there is a **breakdown** or **defeat** <term>bhaṅga</term>; for instance, a king might flee from a battle. || In matters of commerce, it means a loss of wealth. || In academic debate, it means a loss of knowledge. || This is how the results should be understood. The calculation of one, two, or three piercings applies to vowels and letters alike. || Regarding the consideration of the planets: the combined influence of the five malefic planets on the vowel, letter, lunar mansion, lunar day, and zodiac sign must be analyzed. If among the letters and other elements there is a piercing by one planet, there is anxiety. If there are two planets, there is much greater distress. In this way, the planetary count determines the variation of the anxiety.

> Through piercing by one or more cruel planets, results are born for men: 
> Anxiety, confusion, fear, loss, and death occur in sequence. || 27 || 
> Here, these are confusion and loss for the country; 
> Fear is indicated by the lines on the lunar day <term>tithi</term>. 
> In the zodiac sign <term>rāśi</term>, there is fleeing and instability; 
> With five piercings, the life-breath barely survives. || 30 || 
> A single piercing brings fear, two bring the destruction of wealth, 
> Three cause a defeat, and death occurs with four piercings. || 29 ||

Regarding the number of planets: the interpretation of anxiety varies. In a piercing where a planet causes **confusion** <term>bhrama</term>, it means a loss of awareness; this is essentially the same as anxiety, as confusion arises from being troubled. It signifies a betrayal of one's surroundings or a loss of one’s work and objectives. In the case of a vowel, it manifests as **disease**. Thus, when four malefic planets "betray" or pierce a point, mental illnesses are mentioned. A piercing by four planets becomes a "disease-bringing" piercing. The analysis here is that through the sequence of one or more piercings, one begins with anxiety. This also occurs with the rising of the "Cruel Sun" <note>original: "nairṛki-samudayena"; likely referring to an inauspicious planetary alignment</note>. When there are four planets, the results range from anxiety to actual disease. Regarding the lunar day <term>tithi</term>, fear and hatred are appropriate. With a double-piercing, fear should be predicted. Here too, anxiety and confusion accompany the fear. In the zodiac sign, a "piercing-strike" <term>vidru-mahā</term> indicates a great obstacle, which leads to death. Later, it is said that a piercing by five planets—which means <gloss>vidrumā</gloss> or the destruction of the person's country—means that with five "piercings," the person does not survive. The meaning here is that the piercing of all five elements (vowel, letter, etc.) is extremely severe. For a person of the Aries <note>original: "meṣa-kārasya"</note> sign, a five-planet piercing results in his end. || 28 ||

**The author explains further details** || Regarding the verse "One piercing...": Such planetary piercings indicate the fear of war and should not be applied elsewhere. A piercing by a "virtuous" or benefic planet causes loss of wealth <note>This is a specific technical exception in this system</note>; a triple piercing results in defeat. || A quadruple piercing results in death; specifically, death in the midst of battle. In this matter as well, the consideration of the number of planets and the number of letters/vowels must be performed as previously described. || 29 ||

<summary>This page details the dire consequences of "piercings" (Vedha) by malefic planets within the Sarvatobhadra Chakra. It provides a progressive scale of misfortune—ranging from mental anxiety to death—depending on how many "cruel" planets (Sun, Saturn, Mars, Rahu, Ketu) are impacting an individual's astrological markers.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the Sarvatobhadra Chakra. Having previously established the negative results (fear, anxiety, and defeat) caused by "cruel" or malefic planets, the text now transitions to the positive effects of "mild" or benefic planets, and then provides a detailed breakdown of the specific misfortune or fortune associated with each individual celestial body. It concludes with a mathematical formula for calculating a planet's strength based on its zodiacal placement.</meta>

Alternatively, the author explains the results of the "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> <note>The astrological "aspect" or line of influence in the grid</note> by auspicious planets. Just as the results of one, two, or three piercings by malefic planets were described as anxiety and so on, one should determine that one, two, or three piercings by benefic planets lead to joy, profit, and similar positive outcomes. Here, a benefic planet joined with a malefic planet acts as a malefic and gives malefic results; otherwise, Venus and others are auspicious. If a benefic is joined with another benefic, it is exceedingly auspicious. Although not explicitly stated here, one should use common sense <note>original: "cittaṃ laṃvadet," literally "let the mind weigh it"</note> to adjust the intensity based on the specific planets involved. If malefic influence is greater, the bad result is greater; if benefic influence is greater, the good result is greater. || 30 ||

As stated in another source regarding "loss" and other effects, the author now describes the specific results for each individual planet's piercing:

->Just as cruel planets are of wicked influence, so the mild planets are providers of auspiciousness. However, mild planets joined with cruel ones should be known as providers of cruel results. || 30 || Mental distress comes from a piercing by the Sun; loss of wealth occurs when Mars pierces. Saturn causes the pain of disease; Rahu and Ketu are the piercers of obstacles. || 31 || The same result is spoken of for the Moon <note>i.e., it varies</note>; gain of pleasure comes from Venus. Great terror arises from a piercing by Mercury; Jupiter is the giver of all [good] fruits. || 32 || Piercings by mild and cruel planets together destroy life; they cause delusion, disease, and the passing away of wealth. A piercing in the "Destructive" <term>Vaināśika</term> <note>The 23rd lunar mansion from the birth star, associated with ruin</note> must be noted; a triple piercing brings the fear of death. || 33 || Full strength in its own sign; three-quarters strength <note>original: "pādena" (minus a quarter)</note> in a friend's sign; half strength in a neutral sign; and one-quarter strength when situated in an enemy's sign. || 34 ||<-

Regarding the piercing by the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note>, there is mental distress—this means the mind becomes agitated. When the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term> is pierced by the Sun, the mind becomes troubled. It was mentioned elsewhere that "loss" occurs; therefore, when Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> pierces, there is a loss of wealth. Similarly, when Saturn <note>original: "Shani"</note> pierces the "Vowel" <note>Svara</note>, one becomes diseased. When the Zodiac Sign <note>Rashi</note> is pierced, great obstacles arise. Here, Rahu and Ketu are the piercers; when the Zodiac Sign is pierced by Rahu or Ketu, there is great obstacle or even death. || 31 ||

Now the results for benefic piercings are explained. For the Moon <note>original: "Chandra"</note>, the result is mixed: if the Moon is joined with malefics or is waning, it gives malefic results; if it is a full Moon, it gives auspicious results. A piercing by Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> brings happiness in the form of love and play. A piercing by Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> causes "great terror"—this refers to the anxiety of weighing what should be abandoned or accepted. A piercing by Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> brings all auspicious results; it means that which was sought is successfully attained. This refers to a single piercing. If there is a piercing of the "Destructive" position, it causes the loss of wealth, loss of status, or even death. || 32 ||

Now for the specific rule: Regarding the phrase **mild and cruel planets together destroy life**, if a malefic planet pierces the 23rd lunar mansion <note>the Vaināśika</note>, then disease and loss of wealth occur. In the collective, communal, or fatal points <note>the Samudāyika, Sāṅghātika, etc.</note>, a triple piercing by such planets results in death. || 33 ||

Now the author explains where a planet gives its maximum or minimum influence: **Full strength in its own sign**. When a planet is in its own zodiac sign <term>Svakshetra</term>, its strength is considered "full." In a friend's sign or a friend's division <term>Navamsha</term>, the strength is "three-quarters" <note>original: "tri-caraṇa," three feet/parts</note>. In a neutral sign, the strength is half. In an enemy's sign, the strength is one-quarter. This is the general rule of the Chakra. || 34 ||

->This strength of mild and cruel planets is equal according to their position. One should understand these results as being inverted for cruel planets. || 35 ||<-

The author elaborates: This chart applies to the mild planets. The strength described (full in own sign, etc.) applies to the calculation of the eight-fold strength for Mercury and others. However, for malefic planets, this strength is understood as "inverted" <note>original: "viparyayāt"</note>. In that inversion, Saturn <note>and other malefics</note> are considered to have full strength when situated in an enemy's sign.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the Sarvatobhadra Chakra, a complex astrological grid. The previous page established how to calculate a planet’s basic strength (Bala) based on whether it resides in a friendly, neutral, or hostile zodiac sign. This page advances the theory by introducing the effects of "retrograde" motion (planets appearing to move backward) and "piercing" (Vedha), which are critical for predicting events like illness, war, and the success of a fort.</meta>

### Now, the detailed explanation is stated:
Regarding the phrase "cruel planets," the word "field" refers to their position. One should state their strength <term>Bala</term> according to the specific time <note>known as Tatkalika Bala</note> based on the position and the aspect <term>Vedha</term>. According to the true meaning, position refers to one’s own house, a friend's house, the seventh house, or even an enemy’s house; even a neutral position is considered, but not in the same way. It is known that in the **retrograde path**, planets "pierce" the positions starting from the first mansion.

There, it is explained: a planet situated in its own sign possesses "positional strength." When a star <term>Nakshatra</term> is pierced by a planet in retrograde motion, the result that was previously stated to be auspicious actually becomes negative. For example, if a star is pierced by a planet in the sign of Leo, or in its own sign, or with a retrograde mind, the result is fully realized. If it is in its own sign and retrograde, the life-force <note>original: "Jiva"</note> possesses full strength. The results are described in terms of agitation, expansion, illness, and death. These results should be declared as either half or full, depending on the specific fears mentioned here.

> Both cruel <note>malefic</note> and gentle <note>benefic</note> planets, when in **retrograde paths**—provided they are not in their sign of debilitation—
> One should state their temporary strength by understanding their position. || 37 ||
>
> For a retrograde planet, the strength is doubled; it is **tripled** when situated in its sign of exaltation.
> In its own sign, the strength is **swift** <note>meaning potent or fast-acting</note>; in its sign of debilitation, the planet gives only half the result. || 38 ||
>
> When the lunar day <term>Tithi</term>, zodiac sign <term>Rashi</term>, divisional sign <term>Amsha</term>, and star mansion <term>Nakshatra</term> are pierced by a cruel planet,
> One should **carefully** avoid starting **any** primary task. || 39 ||
>
> Do not marry on a Nanda lunar day, and do not **return** from a journey then.
> A sick person will not be released from illness if they fall ill during the "Time of Yama" <note>a specific inauspicious period of the day</note>. || 40 ||
>
> In the place of the aspect <term>**Vedha**</term>, there occurs a "shattering of the result." In a fort or a village, destruction occurs. 
> Whether it be the entry of a scholar <note>original: "Kavi"</note> or the killing of a warrior, it happens **there**. || 41 ||
>
> At the time of illness, the doctor appears because of the influence of a cruel planet. 
> Through **retrograde motion**, death may occur; through **swift** motion, one remains afflicted by disease. || 42 ||

In that position in its own sign, the results such as the "piercing of the coral" <note>an idiomatic or technical term for a specific auspicious result</note> should be declared as full. If the planet is in its own sign and **retrograde**, they say the result is complete. With three quarters, it is full; with four quarters, the result is doubled. If it is in a friend’s sign and "piercing the coral," the results like fear should be declared as reduced by one quarter. If it is in a friend’s house, it is called "friendly." Then the results for the planets should be stated. **Swift** motion is the best. In that same way, the positional strength in one's own sign is "swift," while in a friend’s house, it is reduced by a quarter, and so on. In its sign of debilitation, it gives half the result. If it is debilitated in a friend’s sign, for that reason, the movement is read as such. || 37 ||

And so, it is said that "in a **retrograde** planet, the result is doubled." || 38 || Regarding "lunar day, sign, divisional sign, and star mansion"—if these are pierced, the results of the mantra, whether auspicious or inauspicious, must be **carefully** avoided in all cases. This is easy to understand. || 39 || Regarding "not on a Nanda lunar day"—at the time of the **Vedha**, if one’s knowledge is lost on a Nanda day, one does not **return** from a journey. At the time of the Vedha, if a person becomes sick, based on that knowledge, the sickness <unclear>...</unclear> the Vedha arises, and the sick person is not released from the disease. || 40 ||

And so, **he will say**, "at the time of disease," which is easy to understand. || 41 || Regarding "in the place of **Vedha**"—wherever the **Vedha** <note>piercing influence</note> occurs in a place like a village, one should not stay there. If the village's strength is pierced, one should not remain in that village. If there is a **Vedha** in the field, one should not engage in battle there. Because of that, the fort becomes <note>vulnerable</note>. Having established the Sarvatobhadra Chakra for the village or the fort, one should deliberate on the **Vedha**. If the star, letter, sign, or vowel are pierced there, a "shattering" <note>defeat</note> occurs and the fort is broken. One should not enter the fort during such a piercing. Regarding the killing of warriors, if the warrior's star is pierced, and if the strength is situated in that limb, then in the Sarvatobhadra, which possesses "piercing of the coral," a killing will occur. || 42 ||

### Now, the deliberation of the poet is stated:
Where, in the morning, the signs of Taurus, Gemini, and Cancer <note>original: "Vrusha-Mithuna-Kulira"</note> are in the Northeast, those staying in the East are aligned with the proper sounds. Leo, Virgo, and Libra <note>original: "Simha-Kanya-Tula"</note> stay aligned with the South. Scorpio, Sagittarius, and Capricorn <note>original: "Vrushchika-Dhanus-Makara"</note> stay aligned with the East, and there the sounds of the months of Ashvin stay aligned. Aquarius, Pisces, and Aries <note>original: "Kumbha-Meena-Mesha"</note> stay aligned with the North. (43, 44)

The specific details of these directions are stated: Regarding the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term>, in the East there are seven: starting from Krittika, Rohini, and Mrigashira. In these seven eastern mansions... <unclear>ink faded</unclear> ...these are the five colors and four signs. The days of the Sun and Mars, these sounds are established as aligned for a three-month period.

->Where the Sun travels in the first part of the day in a group of three zodiac signs, that direction is known as "aligned" <term>Sammita</term>; the remaining three directions are considered equal. (43) The Northeast signs are in the East; they are known as the Southeast in the movement. The Southwest are the South, and the Northwest are considered the North. (44) The lunar mansions, vowels, letters, zodiac signs, lunar days, and directions—all these should be known as "joined" wherever the Sun resides for three months. (45) In the lunar mansion, one finds victory and gain; in the vowel and the rising sign <term>Lagna</term>, one finds loss and grief. In the zodiac sign, know that there is fear; if five factors are aligned, death is certain. (46) One should avoid travel, war, and disputes when facing the "burning direction" <note>original: "Asha-daha"</note>. Men should not perform any other auspicious deeds while facing this direction. (47) When the first letter of one's name is situated in the aligned direction, then in all works, victory is theirs by the grace of God. (48) In the "poetry" <note>likely referring to the grid's analysis</note> of the fort, in warfare, and in the battle of horses <term>Turangama-hava</term>, the rising sign and the courage of the two sides should be known through victory and defeat. (49)<-

Thus, in the four directions, the lunar mansions, vowels, letters, zodiac signs, lunar days, and directions should be known as "joined." (45)

Now, the result of the health/stability of the direction is stated: "In the lunar mansion..." This is explained as follows: Previously, when combined with agitation or fearlessness, the result is attained. (46) Now, the prohibition of actions in an unaligned direction is stated: "Travel, war..." This means that travel should not be undertaken toward that direction, nor should rituals for a son be performed. "Dispute" refers to scholarly or intellectual debates. One should not do any of these things facing the "burning direction." (47) Now, another result of alignment is stated: "Situated in the direction..." This is easy to understand. (48) Regarding "Poetry and the Fort": When the vowels, letters, and zodiac signs of a warrior are "unjoined" <note>not aligned</note>, those warriors are known as "unjoined." What should be avoided by these unjoined warriors? Siege warfare <term>Kotta-yuddha</term> should be avoided, and hand-to-hand combat should be avoided. Especially the "battle of horses." (49)

Now, the result of the rising of the aligned vowels and other factors is stated: "On the day of the mansion..." On those days, there is gain for the body and health. When the five factors—mansion, letter, vowel, sign, and lunar day—are all risen, one attains a position like that of royalty. (50)

### Now, he explains the result of the "piercing" at the time of an inquiry <term>Prashna</term>:

The phrase "at the time of an inquiry" is easy to understand. **[51]** Regarding the rising sign <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign ascending on the eastern horizon</term> in a particular country, what is said about the result of movable and other types of rising signs should be understood by all astrologers. Through the nature of the rising sign, the result regarding the gain or loss of stolen property, or victory and defeat, should be known. One should declare the result based on the nature of the village, forest, or water-sign <note>Zodiac signs are categorized by their "habitat," such as terrestrial or aquatic, which influences the reading.</note> according to the mind. 

Now, he speaks of the result when a movable or other type of sign is rising: When a movable sign <term>Chara Lagna; signs like Aries or Cancer that indicate change</term> is rising, property that is lost is gone forever, or the sick person meets death; in that case, one should also predict a short lifespan. 

When a fixed sign <term>Sthira Lagna; signs like Taurus or Leo that indicate stability</term> is rising, a lost item may be recovered after a short time. In that case, even if a person is sick for a long time, they may still obtain a long life, though it remains doubtful. If the seeker touches a part of their body <note>This refers to 'Angasparsha,' a technique where the seeker's physical movements provide omens.</note> when a lost item is mentioned, there will be a quick recovery and the sick person will quickly become well. Medium lifespan is predicted when a dual-natured sign <term>Dvisvabhava Lagna; signs like Gemini or Pisces that share movable and fixed traits</term> is rising. The results for night and other times are as previously stated for the movable signs. **[52]** 

Now, another point regarding "piercing from both sides" by cruel planets <term>Krura; malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</term>: this piercing is of three types.

->When a lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term> is sighted, there is profit, vocal happiness, and victory for the king.<-
->There is brilliance and the offering of wealth on the lunar day <term>Tithi</term> when the five-fold combination rises. **[50]** At the time of inquiry, let there be<-
->the two-fold rising sign pierced by cruel planets. If seen by gentle planets <term>Saumya; benefics like Venus or Jupiter</term>, it is auspicious; if by mixed planets, the result<-
->will be mixed. **[51]** The great fruit of the rising sign should be known by the local experts according to the nature of the sign,<-
->whether it be movable or otherwise. **[52]**<-
->If the lunar days and vowels are pierced from both sides by cruel planets, the zodiac sign and the lunar mansion<-
->will surely perish; of this, there is no doubt. **[53]**<-
->A territory, city, village, or a temple of the gods<-
->is destroyed without a doubt if pierced from both sides by cruel planets. **[54]**<- <margin>4</margin>
->In the triad of lunar mansions starting with Krittika, if pierced by cruel planets according to the wheel, the<-
->countries will be destroyed in order according to their state. **[55]**<-

It is said that the piercing can be from the left, the right, or the front. When a planet is situated in both directions, it pierces with a "left sight." Likewise, with a "front sight," it pierces from both directions, or with a "right sight," it pierces from both directions. In whatever direction and in whatever lunar mansion a cruel planet moves, it causes a "great piercing." In that direction, in that mansion, or in the mansion of an equal field, if a cruel planet is written as having a "middle motion," then there is a "front piercing" between the two. 

In this way, the five factors—the lunar day, the vowel, etc.—are "pierced from both sides" by cruel planets. For whoever's name this occurs, death arises; it happens just as death occurs. **[53]** 

The phrase "territory, city, village" describes how these are destroyed when pierced from both sides by cruel planets, as explained previously. This refers to the *Amesha* wheel or the two-fold combination, and also the five-fold combination of letters/sounds. Here too, there is a two-fold consideration. **[54]**

Now he speaks of the result of piercing elsewhere. "Starting with Krittika..." refers to the Tortoise Wheel <term>Kurma Chakra; a diagram mapping the stars onto the geography of the earth</term>. In the triads of lunar mansions, if the mansions in a certain direction are pierced from both sides by cruel planets, the countries in those nine-fold divisions are destroyed. 

Among them, if the triad of mansions starting with Krittika—which represents the Navel of the Tortoise—is pierced from both sides by cruel planets, then the countries in the Navel of the Tortoise <note>In the Kurma Chakra, the 'navel' represents the central heartland of the region.</note> are: **Mithila**, **Kaushambi**, **Kaushiki**, **Ahichhatra**, **Gaya**, the **Vindhya** mountains, **Matsya**, **Paurava**, **Mekhala**, **Kanchi**, **Udra**, and **Prayag**. 

These prominent countries are destroyed. The underlying meaning is this: while it says "all countries are destroyed," it specifically means that among the countries associated with the Krittika triad, those whose five factors (sounds, vowels, etc.) are pierced from both sides by cruel planets will face destruction. This is the deliberation. **[55]**

# Now, he speaks of the consideration regarding other countries:

Regarding the constellation of **Krittika** <note>The Pleiades; the third lunar mansion</note>, this is the meaning: as a whole, it affects everything. When a country is "pierced" <term>Vedha; an astrological phenomenon where a planet's position negatively impacts a specific star or region</term>, the villages within that country are also affected. Whether it involves Krittika and others or those pierced by benefics, even a small or large error in the movement of the planets can result in a defect. However, just as piercing is done by "cruel" <term>Krura; malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</term> planets in the **Twelve-spoked Chart** <note>original: "Saptachadwadashara Chakra"</note>, if one then looks at the birth sign <term>Janma Rashi</term>, the result is determined. This has been specifically stated before. Wherever there is a piercing by a malefic Sun, there is destruction. Wherever there is a "sight" <term>Drishti; a planetary aspect</term> from a benefic planet, prosperity arises. Whether it is the constellation **Shravana** or others, or these three, little grain is proclaimed. Regarding the three "latter" constellations <note>The three Uttara nakshatras: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, one should specify three watches of the day <term>Yama; a three-hour period</term>. **Ashlesha**, **Magha**, and up to **Shravana** are considered special. The Sun's transit through **Ashwini**, **Hasta**, and **Abhijit**, when pierced, leads to the lordship <note>or "affliction," depending on the context of the planetary influence</note> of twice-born classes <term>Dvijati; the upper social classes</term> and artisans. **[56]**

# Now, the consideration of "Cruel Piercing" in other matters:

> Whether in **Krittika**, or likewise in **Revati** and **Punarvasu**,
> if these are pierced, severe hunger <note>famine</note> occurs
> at the start of the year and so on. **[56]**
> Vessels of oil, liquids, grains, elephants, horses, and
> quadrupeds—all of these reach a state of high cost <note>inflation</note>
> wherever a malefic planet is situated. **[57]**
> When a malefic piercing coincides with the appearance of a
> "secondary planet" <term>Upagraha; mathematical points or sensitive omens in the sky</term>,
> there is no doubt that death, disease, or calamity will occur. **[58]**
> The fifth constellation from the Sun's position is known as
> **Vidyunmukha** <note>original: "Vidyunmukham"; literally "Lightning-faced"</note>.
> The eighth is called **Shula** <note>original: "Shulam"; "The Spear"</note>,
> and the fourteenth is **Sannipata** <note>original: "Sannipatam"; "Collision" or "Epidemic"</note>. **[59]**
> The eighteenth is called **Ketu** <note>original: "Ketu"; "The Comet"</note>,
> the twenty-first is **Ulka** <note>original: "Ulka"; "The Meteor"</note>,
> the twenty-second is **Kampa** <note>original: "Kampa"; "The Tremor"</note>,
> and the twentieth is **Vajra** <note>original: "Vajra"; "The Thunderbolt"</note>. **[60]**
> The twenty-fourth is **Nirghata** <note>original: "Nirghata"; "The Impact" or "Gale"</note>.
> These are remembered here as the secondary planets.
> They are said to be the givers of "piercing" in all works, always. **[61]**

Regarding <term>Tailabhandam</term> <note>original: "vessels of oil"</note>: In every country and every region where a malefic piercing occurs, oil, jaggery, ghee, and grains being pierced means that in that country or village, everything becomes very expensive; thus, because of the lack of wealth and resources, a famine occurs. If a benefic planet pierces, things become affordable; for a small amount of money, much is obtained. **[57]** Regarding <term>Kruravedha</term> <note>malefic piercing</note>... **[58]** Now he describes the secondary planets. Regarding <term>the fifth constellation from the Sun</term>... **[59]** Regarding <term>Ketu</term>... **[60]** **Nirghata** is the twenty-fourth. These "pierce" based on their own positions according to the "elephant gaze" <term>Gaja-drishti; a specific type of astrological aspecting</term>. These eight secondary planets—**Vidyunmukha** and the others—are always obstacles in all undertakings. Wherever these secondary planets occur, auspicious works should be avoided. These secondary planets, staying in their own positions or following the "elephant gaze," pierce through a direct "frontal piercing" <term>Sammukha-vedha</term>. **Vidyunmukha** and the others should be known: **Shula** is proclaimed as the best <note>here likely meaning most potent or prominent</note> among them, then **Sannipata**, **Vajra**, then **Vadha** <note>destruction/slaughter</note>, and **Ketu** is proclaimed. **Ulka** is like a meteor, **Chapa** <note>the bow</note> is the part of **Vajra**. **Kampu** <note>tremor</note> is to be known as Saturn, and **Rahu** is indeed **Nirghata**. According to the movement of these, one should perform actions knowing the "piercing." By this greatness, in the twelve-spoked chart, the vision is formed. **[62]**

# Regarding the Functional Constellations

Now he speaks of another matter: **The birth constellation.** The birth constellation is universal, concerning all people. As for what was said regarding the birth constellation and the others (62). Now for the specific details regarding caste and country (63). Now, the names of the constellations: **Janmabha** is the birth constellation. The tenth constellation from it is named **Karma** <term>Karma Nakshatra; the star governing one's actions, profession, and public life</term> (64). The eighteenth constellation (65). The seventh constellation is **Adribha** <note>literally "Mountain Star"</note> (66). Now, through the power of "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> by malefic planets, death may occur if the birth star is afflicted (70). Regarding the country and others: through the conjunction of secondary planets <term>Upagraha; shadow planets or mathematical points that intensify malefic effects</term>, if the birth star is pierced, death occurs; if the "action" star is pierced, distress occurs, and so on. All of this must necessarily happen due to the conjunction of the secondary planets.

One should not perform any auspicious work when a Pulse Star is pierced (69). Now another point: **Fear and Breaking.** Regarding the birth and other stars, when pierced by "cruel" <term>Krura; malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</term> planets numbering from one to five, one should announce the result. the results are explained. Regarding the date and constellation: if the Moon pierces five stars, one should call it auspicious on that day. But if the Moon's piercing occurs when the stars are already afflicted by malefic planets, one should call that day inauspicious (71).

# Regarding the Valuation of Prices

Now he says, "**I shall explain the determination of prices**," which is clear (72). Now, based on the power of location and time, he explains the decision regarding the "piercing" of subjects. For merchants, this is the **determination of location** <note>original: "desha-nirnaya"</note>—to know in which country goods will be cheap or expensive. It is also the **determination of time** for selling commodities—in which year, or in which season or month, the nature of the commodity will be affected. This allows for the decision regarding the journey and profit of merchants (73).

Now he explains the division of countries, saying "**The circle of country-regions**," which is easy to understand (74). Regarding "**Metals, Roots, and Living Beings**": **Metals** <term>Dhatu; refers here to mineral wealth including gold and silver</term> refer to gold, silver, and the like. **Roots** <term>Mula; refers here to botanical products</term> include sandalwood, aloeswood, aromatic grasses, musk, and so forth. **Living Beings** <term>Jiva; biological commodities</term> include elephants, humans, parrots, musk deer, bulls, and others.

Because merchants do not sell their various commodities separately for the sake of profit, there is a unified decision: in which country or at what time will commodities like **metals** and others be cheap <note>original: "samargha"</note> or expensive <note>original: "mahargha"</note>? After considering this, one may speak. Or, if kings and others desire to know: "In which place and at what time will fortresses and such become very valuable?" And others may ask: "To which country and at what time should I go for the sake of my livelihood?" Thus, there are many variations (75). 

Now he names the lords of the triads of countries and the like: "**The lords of the country are the Ascending Node and Saturn...**" The Ascending Node <term>Rahu</term>, Saturn, and Jupiter are the lords of countries. For example, a "country" is a broad region; a "circle" <note>original: "mandala"</note> is a specific area containing constellations; and a "site" <note>original: "sthana"</note> is even more specific, like a treasury of jewels. Now the countries are spoken of generally—the lords of all countries, the lords of the country’s zodiac signs, and the lords of the local sites. The lords of the regional circles are the Descending Node <term>Ketu</term>, the Sun, and Venus. The lords of the sites are Saturn <note>The commentator here lists Saturn, Mars, and Mercury, slightly varying from the verse above</note>, Mars, and Mercury (76).

Now he names the three-fold lords of time: "**The lords of the year...**" (77). Regarding "**The lords of metals are Saturn and others...**"—here the lords are assigned according to their inherent nature <note>original: "svabhava"</note>, not according to the influence of the rising sign or zodiac sign (78). Regarding "**Commodity-takers**"—this is used merely as a technical name, not according to their actual nature (79).

# Regarding the Great Periods of the Planets

Now he presents the **Great Periods** <note>original: "Mahadasha"</note>, beginning with the verse: "In the white [fortnight]... Saturn and Jupiter..." (76). The Lords of the regions are stated separately. For some regions, there are three [Lords]; for others, two; and for some, four. A doubt arises: does this lordship happen collectively, or by the power of time, or by the power of strength? To consider this, he says:

->The Lords of the Year are Rahu, Ketu, the Sun, and Jupiter.<-
->Then Mars, the Sun, and Venus are the Lords of the Month.<-
->The Moon is known as the Lord of the Day. || 77 ||<-
->The Lords of Metals are Saturn and Mars.<-
->The Lords of Living Beings are Jupiter and the Moon.<-

## On Planetary Exaltation and Strength

->A retrograde planet <term>Vakri; appearing to move backward</term> or an exalted planet<-
->is full of vigor and becomes "Great."<-
->Exaltation is in Aries, Taurus, Virgo, Cancer,<-
->Pisces, and Libra. || 82 ||<-
->The Sun and other planets are exalted there;<-
->their debilitation is in the seventh sign from that point.<-
->Deep exaltation <note>original: "paramocca"</note> occurs at these degrees:<-
->10, 3, 20, 24, 28, 15. || 83 ||<-
->Twenty-seven and twenty are also mentioned.<-
->If the planet is at the deep exaltation or deep debilitation,<-
->the calculation of its power is analyzed. || 84 ||<-
->When a planet is in exaltation or debilitation,<-
->its position is visible in the arrangement.<-
->The strength of the planet is determined by the Rule of Three. || 85 ||<-
->In exaltation, the strength is full; in debilitation, it is minimal.<-
->In its own sign, strength is full; it is three-quarters in a friend's sign. || 86 ||<-

***

Regarding "Saturn, Jupiter," and so on: whichever planet is **retrograde** <note>original: "Vakri"</note>, or has **risen** <note>original: "Udita"</note>, or is situated in its own division, its own house, or its exaltation—by these characteristics, and by its power, that planet should be considered the **Lord of the Time** <note>original: "Kaladhipati"</note> for that region. If another planet becomes stronger later on, the lordship shifts to that one. This is the method of determining the periods (81).

Now he considers the determination of strength: a retrograde or exalted planet is of **full vigor** <note>original: "purna-virya"</note>. Among these, a retrograde planet has even more strength. The strength should be calculated using the **Rule of Three** <term>Nairashika; a mathematical proportion used to find an unknown value</term> based on its distance from the exaltation point (82-85).

Now he speaks of the primary strength: a planet in its exaltation has full strength; in its sign of debilitation or a debilitated division, it has half strength. What is the use of an extensive explanation? One should know the strength through the methods taught in birth horoscopy <note>original: "Jataka"</note>. By considering the six-fold strength <term>Shadbala; a system assessing planetary strength based on position, direction, time, etc.</term>—including strength from exaltation, own house, divisional charts, or friendly groups—whichever planet among the three is strongest, that one is considered the Lord. If two are equally strong, then both are Lords, and their effects should be explained according to their relative power. Based on the strength of the **Lord of the Region**, one should predict the results of the period (86).

It emphasizes that a planet's influence on market prices depends on its mathematical strength, particularly if it is retrograde or in its sign of exaltation.</summary>

# Now, the Consideration of Results
## Thus, the Lords of the Regions, etc.

<margin>34</margin>

|| 87 || Now, the consideration of results. [Regarding the phrase] "Thus, the lords of the regions," and so on. "Thus" refers to the previously mentioned calculation of planetary strength. Those who become the lords of regions and other divisions at the present time are situated in <term>Vedha; an astrological influence or "piercing" aspect</term>. This means they are positioned to exert influence through left-side, front-facing, or right-side glances. These regional lords influence the "five-fold group" consisting of the region, the self, the <term>Varna; the social or symbolic class</term>, and so on. They also influence the lord of the region and the <term>Rashi; the zodiac sign</term> associated with the region's name. When such an influence occurs, one must diligently investigate whether the planet causing the influence is a friend, an enemy, or neutral <note>original: "madhya" or "udasina"</note> to the regional lord. || 88 || Regarding this matter, he speaks the following verses:

> When a planet is in a neutral sign, it should be known as having half strength; it has a quarter strength when situated in an enemy’s sign. Through the influence of the signs, the wise should determine the strength accordingly. || 87 || Thus, those who are the lords of the regions are situated here in an influential position. One must diligently [observe] whether they are friends, enemies, neutrals, or of mixed nature. || 88 || Friendship, neutrality, and enmity [are considered] in order during the influence upon regions and the rest. With auspicious planets <note>original: "shubha-graha"</note>, there is benefit in the hand <note>meaning: "in the possession of"</note> by four, three, two, or one quarters. || 89 || Friendship, neutrality, and enmity [are considered] in order during the influence upon regions and the rest. With malefic planets <note>original: "dushta-graha"</note>, one should produce [harm] by one, two, three, or four quarters. || 90 || Seeing the afflicted point with a full glance results in great fruit, even with a small quarter. Otherwise, the result should be known by inferring the strength of the glance. || 91 ||

Then, by investigating the strength of auspicious planets regarding years, months, and days, the benefit is determined in four, three, two, or one <term>Padas; quarters or one-fourth parts</term>. Specifically, if the lord of the region is an auspicious planet:
* In its **own house** or exaltation: it gives 4 quarters (Full) of benefit.
* In a **friend's house**: it gives 3 quarters of benefit.
* In a **neutral house**: it gives 2 quarters of benefit.
* In an **enemy's house**: it gives 1 quarter of benefit.
Similarly, one should calculate the benefit for the lords of Metals, Roots, and Living Beings based on whether they are in friendly or other types of signs. This covers the influence of auspicious planets. || 89 ||

Next, he discusses the influence of "cruel" or malefic planets. [Regarding the phrase] "In friendship, neutrality," etc., the consideration for a malefic planet is as follows:
* In its **own house**: it causes 1 quarter of harm <note>malefics are less harmful in their own territory</note>.
* In a **friend's house**: it causes 2 quarters of harm.
* In a **neutral house**: it causes 3 quarters of harm.
* In an **enemy's house**: it causes 4 quarters (Full) of harm through its influence. || 90 ||

Now, he validates the result of the influence through the power of the "glance" or aspect <note>original: "drishti"</note>. [Regarding the phrase] "Afflicted with a full [glance]," etc. If the regional lord causing the influence is capable of giving results based on the quarters (friendship, etc.), and it also "sees" (aspects) that point, then the result known by that quarter definitely comes to pass. To elaborate: if there is an influence of three quarters because a planet is in a neutral sign, and the planet gazes upon the "five-fold group" (Region, Varna, etc.) with a **Full Glance**, then the result is fully realized according to its nature. 
If a planet is in a friend’s house (giving 3 quarters of result) and sees with a **three-quarter glance**, then the result produced by that nature is experienced as three-quarters. 
If a neutral planet influences with 2 quarters of result and sees with a **half-glance** (two quarters), the result naturally becomes two quarters.
If an enemy planet is the influencer, it gives 1 quarter of auspiciousness <note>meaning the maleficence is high, and benefit is low</note>; if it sees with a **one-quarter glance**, the result is specifically explained as being exactly that. 
In summary, if there is a 1-quarter influence and the planet sees with a full glance, the 1-quarter result is fully realized. There is a requirement for equality between the strength of the influence and the strength of the glance. || 91 ||

# The Influence of Planetary Aspects
## On the Five-fold Group Beginning with the Phoneme

Now he speaks of the planetary aspect <note>original: "drishti," meaning the gaze or influence of a planet across the zodiac</note> upon the five-fold group starting with the phoneme <note>original: "varna," referring to the initial letter of a name</note>. Regarding the phrase "phoneme, etc.": either in the circle of the zodiac signs or within the twelve-spoked wheel <note>original: "dashara-chakra," a specific diagram used for calculation</note>, when the great planets gaze upon a zodiac sign or a division of a sign through an aspect, then whatever phonemes and other elements are situated in that particular sign become "pierced" <term>Vedha; an astrological affliction or "piercing" caused by a planet’s position or aspect</term> by that influence. That is how the state of being pierced occurs. || 2 ||

Demonstrating the five-fold group within the zodiac signs, he says: regarding the "vowels, phonemes, etc." The vowels and phonemes are described in the "Vowel Wheel" <term>Svara-chakra; a specialized astrological chart used to analyze the sounds in a person's or place's name</term>. Those vowels and phonemes stated in the chapter on the five vowels in the Vowel Wheel become afflicted through the "piercing" of the lunar day <term>Tithi; a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>. 

For example, consider the analysis of the Magadha country: 
1. The **Phoneme** <note>varna</note> is 'Ma' (म).
2. The **Vowel** <note>svara</note> is 'i' (इ) <note>likely referring to the vowel inherent or associated in the calculation</note>.
3. The **Constellation** <note>nakshatra</note> is Magha.
4. The **Zodiac Sign** <note>rashi</note> is Leo.
5. The **Lunar Day** <note>tithi</note> is the twelfth <note>Dvadashi</note>.

If one asks how this occurs: it is according to the method of the phonemes. In this way, this five-fold group of phoneme, etc., which is stated in the Vowel Wheel, becomes afflicted by the pain caused by the "piercing" of its own lunar day. Now, regarding how the aspect falls on these five, he gives the reason: whichever zodiac sign is seen among the lunar days and phonemes, that sign receives the aspect. For example, on the twelfth lunar day, the Sun...
