The phoneme 'Ma' (म) becomes afflicted by the piercing of the twelfth lunar day. The vowel of the phoneme 'Ma' is 'i' (इ). This is what is meant by the "Five-fold Group beginning with the Phoneme." Now, when the phonemes are produced, the phonemes and vowels become afflicted by the pain of the lunar day. In this five-fold group, a distinct "piercing" is possible. Through the influence of the single Vowel Wheel, the vowels and signs are described. 

By the planets causing the "piercing," when the sign of Leo is seen in the twelve-spoked wheel, the "five-fold group beginning with the phoneme" is seen to be pierced. It is not merely that the signs situated in the place of the phoneme and vowel are seen, but that the entire five-fold group is seen as pierced when any one of them is aspected. || 3 ||

Now, he determines the result based on the distinction between the bright <note>Shukla Paksha; the waxing moon</note> and dark <note>Krishna Paksha; the waning moon</note> fortnights. Regarding the phrase "Now, the beauty...": when it is said they are afflicted by the distinction of night and day, that itself is three-fold. In the bright fortnight, one receives the result in three ways, <insert>and</insert> in the dark fortnight, this is the result. || 4 ||

He speaks of the characteristics of the planets and vowels: "Of the planet..." and so on. "In this way..." and so on. By this "piercing by aspect," the entire result is indicated. An aspect on one's own planet or one's own division is a "full aspect" <note>purna-drishti</note>. Now, he calculates the results when an aspect exists regarding the five-fold group. || 5 ||

A benefic <note>Saumya</note> planet takes away [the affliction] fully. Four benefics are mentioned: Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and the full Moon. The malefics <note>Papa</note> are the Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Rahu. For these auspicious and inauspicious planets, one should calculate the result from a twenty-point scale <term>Vimshopaka; a traditional 20-point system for measuring planetary strength</term> when an auspicious aspect is present. In that regard, for the auspicious ones...

# Calculating the Strength of Influence
## The Numerical Values of Planetary Gazes

For the full and other types of aspects <note>original: "drishti," the "gaze" of a planet</note>, the numbers five and four should be set down separately. If there are four full-strength aspects, then by multiplying five by four, the number becomes twenty.

<margin>35</margin> Likewise, regarding the quarter-aspect... <term>97</term> The "piercing" <note>original: "vedha"</note> occurs in the five-fold group beginning with the phoneme <note>the Varnadi-panchaka: phoneme, vowel, constellation, zodiac sign, and lunar day</note>. In whatever place the piercing occurs, if it is from one's own planet with a full gaze, or a friendly planet with a three-quarter gaze, or a neutral planet with a half-gaze, or an enemy planet with a quarter-gaze <note>original: "pada-drishti"</note>, the strength of that piercing should be calculated accordingly. Depending on the number of aspects, one should multiply the value... <note>The text here describes the mathematical proportions of planetary strength</note> in the fifth, tenth, third, first, and eighth positions. One should look at the calculation device <note>original: "kala-yantra"</note>; the aspects provide results in this manner. Here, the piercing must be calculated by the wise using "units of strength" <term>Vishoshaka; a unit of measurement used in Vedic astrology to quantify the power of a planet or aspect, often on a scale of 20 or 40</term>. However many piercings occur in auspicious places...

In this way, all the results are specified according to the different types of aspects. When the five-fold group beginning with the phoneme is pierced, the planet gives its good or bad fruit. || <term>96</term> || 

A benefic planet <note>original: "saumya," literally 'gentle' or 'lunar'</note> gazing upon and piercing the five-fold group provides five units of strength; a malefic planet <note>original: "krura," literally 'cruel'</note>, however, provides four units. || <term>97</term> || 

The wise should calculate the units of strength based on the type of aspect and the location where the piercing occurs in the five-fold group. || <term>98</term> || 

In this way, where good and bad units of strength occur, one should subtract them from each other <note>to find the net influence</note>; there is no doubt regarding the resulting good or bad fortune. || <term>99</term> || 

By using the current units, the calculation of the twentieth-parts <note>original: "vimshansha"</note> should be written in order. One should apply the current values...
->for the sake of determining the great good or evil. || <term>100</term> <-

---

Whatever the strength of the aspect is, it should be calculated separately. The units of strength <note>vishoshakas</note> for a piercing by a full, auspicious aspect are forty (40). For a piercing from an aspect missing one quarter <note>a 3/4 aspect</note>, the units are thirty (30). For a half-aspect, twenty (20). For a quarter-aspect, ten (10). Having calculated these separately for the benefic planets, they should be combined into one total. One should also calculate the units for the malefic planets separately and combine them into one total. || <term>98</term> || 

Then, he asks: "What should be done?" The answer regarding the units of strength is easy to understand. || <term>99</term> || This refers to the current units. || <term>100</term> || <sig>s</sig>

# The Appendix of Results
## Consequences of Malefic and Benefic Piercings

Now begins the appendix. If there is a malefic gaze <note>original: "papa-drishti"</note> and a piercing <note>original: "vedha," the intersection of planetary lines on the grid</note> totaling twenty units of strength <term>Vimshopaka; a numerical system where 20 represents full strength</term>, the result is the "destruction of the country," which is easily understood. || २०१ || Regarding "at the wrong time" and so on... || २०२ || Regarding "foreign armies," this implies internal conflict within the royal family in a state without a strong leader. || २०३ || Regarding "earthquakes" and so on... || २०४ ||

Now, if the piercing is auspicious and the units of strength are benefic, the results are described as follows: "growth occurs" and so on... || २०५ || Regarding "one’s own army/circle"... || २०६ ||

---

> The destruction of the country, the suffering of the people, and widespread slaughter occur where this malefic gaze falls; famine clearly manifests in that region. || २०१ ||

>
> Growth and prosperity arise; rain falls at the proper time. Abundance, safety, and health prevail among the people. || २०५ ||

> Neither internal nor external wars ever occur. Relatives and friends thrive when an auspicious piercing is present. || २०६ ||

> Without making offerings, without fire rituals, without the worship of the Maiden <note>original: "Kumari-pujana," a ritual honoring young girls as embodiments of the Goddess</note>, and without a clear planetary alignment, one should not gaze upon this "King of Charts." || २०८ ||

# The Assembly of the King
## A Scene from the Sacred Histories

<unclear>
[The top of the page contains several lines of faded and blurred script in the Devanagari character. Due to physical damage and ink bleeding from the reverse side, these lines—likely an invocation or a transitional verse from the astrological text to the narrative—are currently illegible.]

->THE COURT OF KING PARIKSHIT<-
<note>original: "Raja Parikshat Sabha"</note>

<unclear>
[Beneath the central illustration, several more lines of script are obscured by fading. They likely identify the figures in the painting or describe the King’s decision to seek spiritual counsel after learning of his destined end.]
</unclear>

***

**Commentary on the Illustration:**

The vibrant painting in the center of this page depicts the royal assembly <term>Sabha; a formal gathering or court</term> of **King Parikshit**. In the upper portion, the King is shown seated under a magnificent golden canopy, a symbol of his high status as the ruler of the Kuru dynasty. He is accompanied by attendants holding fly-whisks <term>chamara; whisks made from yak-tail hair used as a royal insignia</term>. 

The King is engaged in a solemn dialogue with a seated sage, identified by tradition as **Shukadeva**. This scene represents a foundational moment in the *Bhagavata Purana* <note>one of the most important sacred texts in Hinduism, focusing on devotion to Vishnu</note>. Having been cursed to die in seven days, King Parikshit abandoned his throne to sit by the Ganges River, asking the gathered sages: "What is the duty of a man who is about to die?"

The lower portion of the painting shows a gathering of noblemen and fellow sages, reflecting the King's gathering of the wisest minds to witness the sacred recitation. The presence of this illustration in a manuscript otherwise focused on astrology suggests a connection between the "piercings" of fate described previously and the spiritual liberation sought by the King.

# The Construction of the Hundred-Step Wheel
## Technical Instructions for the Astrological Grid

Regarding the **Hundred-Step Wheel of the Lord** <note>original: "Chakreshatapada"; a specialized astrological chart</note>: For those lunar mansions and their quarters, which number one hundred and twelve (112) <note>There are 28 Nakshatras including the intercalary Abhijit; each has 4 quarters (padas), totaling 112</note>, I shall now describe the resulting **Hundred-Step Wheel**. Regarding the "Beginning of the Name" and so on:

**The Placement of the Wheel**

Now, the author explains the layout of the chart. Regarding the "Horizontal and Vertical" lines: In the four directions, beginning with the **Northeast** <term>Ishana; the direction associated with Shiva and spiritual clarity</term> and its surrounding cells <term>kostha; the individual squares or "rooms" in a grid</term>, one should place the phonetic groups **A-Va-Ka-Ha-Da** <note>These are specific seed-syllables used to categorize the lunar mansions (Nakshatras)</note>. 

Place the **A-Va-Ka-Ha-Da** group in the Northeast. Then, place the **Ma-Ta-Pa-Ra-Ta** group in the five cells of the **Southeast** <term>Agneya; the direction of the fire god, Agni</term>.

***

->|| I shall describe the Hundred-Step Wheel of the Lord, which relies on the letters of a name. ||<-
->|| From the letters of the name, one may know the stars and their indicators. || 1 ||<-
->|| The wise practitioner should draw six horizontal and six vertical lines. ||<-
->|| From these, one hundred cells are undoubtedly created. || 2 ||<-
->|| One should place the letters, such as Ka, Ha, Da, and the others, ||<-
->|| starting from the prescribed directions of the wheel. ||<-
->|| In groups of five, one should thus arrange the letters. || 3 ||<-
->|| By combining them with the **five vowels**, make each letter fivefold. ||<-
->|| In the base positions, place three letters each. || 4 ||<-
->|| Let the letters **Ku, Gha, Nga** be placed in the realm of the Northeast; ||<-
->|| let the letters **Du, Kha, Tha** be placed in the center of the Southeast. || 5 || <insert>|| 100 ||</insert><-

***

**The Method of Phonetic Combination**

Through the combination with the **five vowels** <term>panchasvara; the primary vowels A, I, U, E, O</term>, the letter **Va** becomes fivefold: *Va, Vi, Vu, Ve, Vo*. Likewise, by combining the letter **Ka** with the five vowels, five versions are produced: *Ka, Ki, Ku, Ke, Ko*. The same applies to the letter **Ha**, which becomes *Ha, Hi, Hu, He, Ho*, and the letter **Da**, which becomes *Da, Di, Du, De, Do*. By doing this for the **A-Va-Ka-Ha-Da** group, twenty-five variations are generated (5 consonants × 5 vowels = 25).

In the same manner, for the **Ma-Ta-Pa-Ra-Ta** group and the others located in the Southeast and further directions, twenty-five variations are generated for each. By combining the vowels and consonants in this way, the total count of letters reaches **one hundred** (100).

In the position of **Ku-Gha-Nga**, these three letters are to be placed. In the position of **Pu**, one should place **Kha, La, Tha**. In the cell for the letter **Bha**, place **Dha, Ma, Dha**. In the position of the letter **Ngu**, place **Tha, Jña**. By arranging them thus, the letters of the third step of the wheel are produced.

As stated in the verse: "Ku, Gha, Nga shall be..." (5). "Dha, Ku, La in the first..." (6). "In the hand of the letter I..." Thus, the fourfold symmetry is completed. Just as before, the following five stars belonging to the deity **Bhairava** <note>a fierce manifestation of Shiva associated with protection and time</note> are drawn... the letters for the constellation **Uttarashadha** <note>one of the 27/28 lunar mansions</note> form that specific group of four.

<summary>This page provides the mathematical and phonetic instructions for constructing the Shatapada Chakra (Hundred-Step Wheel), a 100-cell grid used to map names to astrological influences. It explains how to expand basic consonants into 100 variations by combining them with five primary vowels and placing them in specific cardinal directions.</summary>

<keywords>Shatapada Chakra, Jyotisha, Vedic Astrology, Phonetic Grid, Nakshatra, Vowels, Ishana, Agneya, Grid Construction</keywords>

# The Hundred-Step Wheel
## Table of Phonetic Correspondence

| A | Va | Ka | Ha | Da | Ma | Ta | Pa | Ra | Ta |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | Vi | Ki | Hi | Di | Mi | Ti | Pi | Ri | Ti |
| U | Vu | Ku <gloss>56</gloss> | Hu | Du | Mu | Tu | Pu <gloss>54</gloss> | Ru | Tu |
| E | Ve | Ke | He | De | Me | Te | Pe | Re | Te |
| O | Vo | Ko | Ho | Do | Mo | To | Po | Ro | To |
| Na | Ya | Bha | Ja | Kha | Ga | Sa | Da | Cha | La |
| Ni | Yi | Bhi | Ji | Khi | Gi | Si | Di | Chi | Li |
| Nu | Yu | Bhu <gloss>58</gloss> | Ju | Khu | Gu | Su | Du <gloss>53</gloss> | Chu | Lu |
| Ne | Ye | Bhe | Je | Khe | Ge | Se | De | Che | Le |
| No | Yo | Bho | Jo | Kho | Go | So | Do | Cho | Lo |

The lunar mansions <term>Dhisnya; also known as Nakshatras</term> starting from the first **Ashadha** are placed in the **Southwest** <term>Nairrtya; the direction ruled by the goddess of destruction</term> range of the wheel. Regarding the visual path <note>Drikpata; the line of sight or aspect in astrology</note>, three should be placed outside the grid in **Uttara Bhadrapada**. || 6 || 

**Ashlesha**, the "Light" **Ashadha** <note>Likely referring to Purva Ashadha</note>, and those named **Purva** and **Uttara Bhadrapada**: these four are known as the "White Steps" by those who know the science of sounds <term>Swaravedi; practitioners who divine outcomes through phonetics and breath</term>. || 7 ||

The lunar mansions begin with **Kruttika**, each separated by four intervals. Including the period of the intercalary mansion **Abhijit**, the total count reaches one hundred and twelve <note>original: "ko dvasadhika"; 112 refers to the 28 Nakshatras each having 4 quarters (padas)</note>. || 8 ||

Whichever cell <term>kostha; a square within the grid</term> a planet occupies—whether it be malefic <term>Krura; "cruel" planets like Saturn or Mars</term> or benefic <term>Saumya; "gentle" planets like Jupiter or Mercury</term>—one must always calculate if it "pierces" <term>Vedhayet; a technical term for a specific astrological aspect or connection</term> the first letter of a man's name. || 9 ||

A piercing by a benefic planet is known to be auspicious; a piercing by a malefic planet is middling. Mixed planets yield mixed results. If there is no piercing at all, the result is mediocre or unfavorable. || 10 ||

Whatever has been said regarding the "piercing" of planets in the **Sarvatobhadra** <note>A famous 81-square or 100-square astrological chart</note> chart, all those auspicious and inauspicious results should be considered here as well. || 11 || 
This is the **Hundred-Step Wheel**. || 12 ||

<margin>...the lunar mansion is everywhere in the positions of the grid... || 7 || Now regarding the calculation of the one hundred and twelve...</margin>

Regarding the syllables: the lunar mansions begin with **Kruttika** and follow the sequence A, I, U, E, O. The twenty-five letters are divided into four parts. By assigning these letters to the quarters <term>Charana; a fourth part of a Nakshatra</term> of **Kruttika** and the others, one determines the phonetic power. 

(The commentary continues to list the specific syllable mappings):
The letters A, Aa, I, U, E, O are used one by one. For **Kruttika**, the quarters are A, A, Vi, Vu. For **Rohini**, the quarters are Ve, Vo, Ko, Ki. For **Mrigashira**, the quarters are Ve, Vo, Pu, Va. For **Ardra**, the quarters are Ku, Kha, Nga, Chha <note>The text details how specific phonemes like 'He', 'Ho', 'Tha', 'Da' are assigned to the mansion **Pushya**, and 'Di', 'Du', 'De', 'Do' are assigned to **Ashlesha**</note>. 

The ninth-parts <term>Navamsha; a 1/9th division of a zodiac sign</term> of the Nakshatras, including **Abhijit**, are situated in their respective cells within the Hundred-Step Wheel according to their assigned letters. Depending on the influence of the planets on these cells, the results are determined. Regarding the cell containing the first letter of a man's name: if it is "pierced," the result is seen. If it is not pierced, it means that the auspicious or middling result will not manifest in that specific way. || 20 || This is what was stated in the **Sarvatobhadra** teachings. || 22 ||

->|| Thus ends the Phonetic Hundred-Step Wheel from the *Victory of the King* <note>Narapati Jayacharya</note> regarding the science of sounds in the *Yamala* tradition. || 23 ||<-

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Swarodaya system, moving from the Hundred-Step Wheel to the Ashtamsha Chakra (Eight-Part Wheel). It provides the mathematical and geometric instructions for drawing the grid and interpreting the "piercing" (vedha) or astrological aspects of malefic planets on specific letters and quarters of the lunar mansions.</meta>

# The Eight-Part Wheel
## Page 35

Twenty-eight vertical lines and twenty-eight horizontal lines are drawn to form a grid of squares. Just as there are one hundred and twelve steps <note>original: "द्वादशाधिकशतं" (one hundred and twelve)</note> for the lunar mansions starting from **Krittika**, one hundred and eight are used when writing the triplets and other divisions. || 1 || 

Regarding this, he says: "Starting from **Krittika**..." || 2 || "The worthy planet..." || 3 || 

Now, concerning the "piercing" <term>Vedha; an astrological "aspect" where a planet's position aligns with and "pierces" or afflicts a specific letter, sound, or star</term>, he says: "In this piercing..." || 4 || "Which has another name..." || 5 || "Situated between two cruel influences..." || 6 || 

"It shall pierce..." means that where there is an entry from both sides, in that quarter <term>charana; one-fourth of a lunar mansion</term>, the "piercing" becomes <unclear>heart-shattering</unclear>.

---

Twenty-eight vertical lines and twenty-eight horizontal lines form the grid. This is the **Amsha Chakra** <term>Amsha Chakra; the "Division Wheel," a specialized grid used for fine-tuned predictions based on the quarters of lunar mansions</term>, as was previously stated in the *Yamala* texts. || 1 || 

By placing the lunar mansions starting from **Krittika** in a sequence of three quarters <note>original: "पादत्रय" (three quarters/feet)</note> and combining them to reach the number twenty-eight: || 2 || 

Where a planet is situated in a specific quarter of a lunar mansion <term>Rikshansha; the degree or quarter of a star</term>, a wise person should contemplate that. A malefic planet <term>Krura; literally "cruel," referring to planets like Mars, Saturn, or the Sun</term> pierces the letter directly in front of it, whether by day or night. || 3 || 

The first quarter pierces the fourth; the fourth does not pierce the first. The second pierces the third, but the third does not pierce the second. || 4 || 

If a person's name-letter <note>original: "विठ्ठमंश" (pierced division/letter)</note> is caught in this wheel, then malefic planets will undoubtedly cause loss or death. || 5 || 

This is known by the masters as "situated between two cruels." When "pierced" by two malefic influences, it results in disease, pain, or even death. || 6 || 

One should check for this piercing during marriage or at the start of a journey; if present, one should not proceed. In the case of illness, malefic piercing undoubtedly signifies death or total collapse. || 7 || 

The "piercing of the four oceans" <note>A metaphor for total or widespread affliction</note> caused by malefic planets leads to destruction. In this matter, there is no need for further hesitation. || 8 || 

If the Moon is in a quarter of a lunar mansion that is being "pierced" by a planet, that day and that measure of time must be avoided for all auspicious activities. || 9 || 

**Thus ends the chapter on the Amsha Chakra in the "Victory-Conduct of the King" <note>Narapati-jayacharya</note> regarding the science of sounds.** || 3 ||

---

### Commentary on the Power of Piercing in the Amsha Chakra

He explains the power of the "piercing" in the **Amsha Chakra**. Regarding the "four oceans": where there is a malefic piercing in the wheel, things are destroyed as previously mentioned. In such a case, what hope is there for recovery from illness? In grids like the *Sarvatobhadra* <term>Sarvatobhadra; a "square-on-all-sides" auspicious grid used for complex divination</term> or the Hundred-Step Wheel, if the five-fold name-elements are pierced, that fated result must surely come to pass. 

Regarding the "Moon's quarter": the Moon moves through the quarters of the lunar mansions. If, at the time of a specific action, the Moon pierces a letter, and if that same lunar mansion is also being pierced by another planet, then one must avoid all auspicious actions on that day and for that specific duration. || 9 || 

**Thus ends the Amsha Chakra section in the "Victory-Lakshmi" <note>Jayalakshmi</note> commentary on the Science of Sounds, composed by Sri Harivansha Mahadeva.** || 3 ||

It emphasizes avoiding travel, marriage, or important work when malefic "piercings" (vedha) are present.</summary>

<page-num>38</page-num>
<header>The Illustrious Shalihotra</header>
<meta>rubricated manuscript; Sanskrit text in Devanagari script; possible bleed-through from verso</meta>

# **The Horse Wheel** || 1 ||

**Regarding the Ashvini System:** The Ashvini system <note>original: "अश्वि न्यायमिति" (Ashvi-nyayam)</note> consists of a wheel of nine constellations <term>Nakshatras; lunar mansions or star clusters</term> situated in the Northern direction. The group beginning with **Magha** consists of nine constellations situated in the Eastern direction. The group beginning with **Mula** consists of nine constellations situated in the Southern direction. **This is the division of the three royal fields:** In these fields, kings named the "Three Vala" <note>original: "त्रिवलनामका" (Three Powers/Valas)</note> are situated. Their names are explained as **the Avachaka Vala names.** Their "Valas" <term>Vala; here referring to military or sovereign power/force</term> are known as: the Horse-force <note>original: "आश्ववल" (Ashva-vala)</note>, followed by the [Human] progeny, and then the Elephant-force <note>original: "दंतयः" (Dantayah; literally "the tusked ones")</note>. 

How is the primary force of these constellation names determined? Thus it is said in the **Shalihotra composed by Nakula** <note>Nakula was one of the five Pandava brothers, traditionally regarded as an expert in equine science</note> in the dialogue concerning the King’s fame, fortune, righteousness, desire, and wealth: "For one whose horses remain endowed with all the auspicious marks <note>original: "सर्वलक्षण लक्षिताः"</note>..." 

And likewise, the **Words of Lakshmi** <term>Lakshmi; the goddess of wealth and prosperity</term>: "Even if a single horse resides in the courtyard of his home, Lakshmi, having abandoned the site of the Vedas <note>original: "विशो वेद स्थले" (likely referring to a place of ritual/learning)</note>, remains in his house for a long time."

---

-> **Now, the Fly-whisk Wheels.** <-
<note>Fly-whisks (chamara) were ancient symbols of royalty and divinity</note>
-> **First, having drawn the wheel,** <-
-> **it is beautified by the three royal fields.** <-
-> **One should place the Ashvini system there,** <-
-> **and the twenty-seven stars.** || 1 ||
-> **Starting with Ashvini, then Magha,** <-
-> **and then the Mula-group are joined in the wheel.** <-
-> **In the North, East, and South,** <-
-> **is the thought of the three star-groups.** || 2 ||
-> **In the West and the middle line,** <-
-> **up to ten is the Lord of Horses.** <-
-> **In the Southeast is the Lord of Men,** <-
-> **and in the center is the Lord of Elephants.** || 3 ||

---

Likewise, regarding how the force of elephants is inferior to these, he says in support: "Indeed, elephants as dark as split blue collyrium <note>original: "नीलेंद संनिभाः" (like blue-black ointment)</note> are joined; they are difficult to conquer and difficult to restrain, yet they are easily defeated by those [horses]." Men are considered even lower in force than those elephants. <margin>And</margin> In that system, the horses are anchored to the constellations starting with Ashvini, the elephants are situated in the field of Magha, and the men are situated in the field of Mula. This division of duties applies everywhere in the *Yamala* texts and others. || 4 || 

He describes the division of duties in the four directions: **The middle line of the West.** In this example, there is the division of the land. Thus in the **Rajavijaya** <note>original: "राजविजये" (The Royal Victory, a text on statecraft or omens)</note>: "The Overseer of Horses, the Overseer of Elephants, and the Overseer of Men, in that order. The arrangement of these three is the secret of the three kings." Regarding this matter, in the **Vijaya Kalpalata** <note>The "Creeper of Desire for Victory," likely a technical manual</note>, there are the wheels: "Horses and waves, the human pair, the master of elephants... [corrupt text]... The elephants of the Vindhya mountains, the lords of the East, and the humans of the Southern direction." The lords of the West, the lords of elephants, and the lords of men; these kings shall provide. 

This is merely the naming of the three star-groups. Here, the rule of the middle line of the West determines the attainment of the country of those who are the lords; therein lies the division of countries in the wheel itself: **Ayodhya, Mithula, Champa, Koshambi, Koshiki, Ahikshetra, Gaya, the Vindhya Forest, Vedi, and Mekhala.** In **Kanyakubja**, the middle country <term>Madhyadesha; the central heartland of ancient India</term> perishes. In these countries, wherever the **Koshiki** is, between that and the West, that line is situated; in the upper part of that line, up to the Northeast, is the Lord of Horses. Koshiki is in the Southeast...

<vocab>Horse Wheel (Ashva-chakra), Shalihotra (Ancient horse science), Nakula (Pandava horse expert), Fly-whisk Wheel (Chamara-chakra), Lord of Horses (Hayadhipa), Lord of Elephants (Gajadhipa), Lord of Men (Naradhipa), Lunar Mansion (Nakshatra), Royal Victory (Rajavijaya), Creeper of Victory (Vijaya Kalpalata)</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces the Ashva Chakra (Horse Wheel), a system that maps 27 lunar mansions to geographical regions and three types of sovereign power: Horse, Elephant, and Human. It cites the Shalihotra of Nakula to emphasize the supreme spiritual and political value of horses, suggesting that their presence ensures the long-term residence of the goddess Lakshmi.</summary>
<keywords>Ashva Chakra, Shalihotra, Nakula, Lakshmi, Hayadhipa, Gajadhipa, Naradhipa, Equine Astrology, Indian Geography, Ancient Statecraft</keywords>

--- Page 114 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of the Ashva Chakra (Horse Wheel) by moving from the celestial constellations to their terrestrial counterparts. It maps the geography of the known world—from the Himalayas to the southern oceans—categorizing various kingdoms under the dominion of three great symbolic archetypes of power: the Lord of Horses, the Lord of Men, and the Lord of Elephants. This reflects a traditional Indian geopolitical system where different regions were famed for their specific military strengths.</meta>

Moving from the central line toward the South-East <note>original: "आग्नेयांतं" (Agneyantam)</note>, there are the **Lords of Men**. Between them is the Sage Kaushika <note>A name for the Sage Vishvamitra, often associated with the boundaries of cosmic and terrestrial order</note>. The region between the North-East and the South-East includes the territory of Magadha and others, known as the field of the wind-travelers. In the center of this resides the **Lord of Elephants**. Now, moving from the central line of the West toward the North and the North-East, we find the kings of the northern lands. As stated by <term>Varāha</term> <note>Referring to Varahamihira, the famous 6th-century astronomer and polymath whose "Brihat Samhita" is a primary source for this type of geographical astrology</note>:

<margin>1</margin> The Mlechhas <note>A term used for foreign or non-Vedic peoples</note>, the Hehayas, and the Shatakas reside in the North-West direction, along with those who are "red-eyed." The kingdom of women <note>original: "स्त्रीराज्य" (Stri-rajya); a legendary land often placed in the Himalayas or further North</note>, the long-maned ones, and those of the Hermitage of Manjika. Then follow the Gandharas, the Ayodheyas, and those who live in the valleys of the mountains. In the North-East <note>original: "ऐशान्यां" (Aishanyam)</note>:

<margin>13</margin> The Kira, <term>Kashmīr</term>, Ganjana <note>Possibly Ghazni in modern Afghanistan</note>, the Khurasans, the Khasas, the Simhapura, and the Kantas. Whoever is the sovereign of these regions is known as the **Lord of Horses** <note>original: "अश्वपति" (Ashvapati)</note>. His royal umbrella is the **Umbrella of the Lord of Horses**.

Now, moving toward the South-West <note>original: "नैऋत्य" (Nairitya)</note> and the South-East, we find the following kings. In the South-West direction:

---

-> ॥ The position of the royal umbrella must be understood for other kings as well. ॥ <-
-> ॥ The radiance of that umbrella brings auspicious results for that king. || 4 || <-

---

In that direction are the Kamboja, the Dravidians, the Vanitas, the people of Surashtra <note>Modern-day Gujarat</note>, the Revatakas, and the Dhargharas. Then, regarding the South and other directions, there is the capital of the night-roamers <note>original: "रजनी चर राज धानी" (Rajani-chara-rajadhani); a poetic reference to Lanka, the kingdom of the Rakshasas or demons</note>, the Shri Mountain, the Malaya mountains, Mount Mahendra, the Kalatakas, Kerala, Rishyamuka <note>The mountain associated with the monkey-kingdom in the Ramayana</note>, the Abhiras, the Cholas, the people of Vanavasi, and the <term>Mahārāṣṭra</term> people. In the South-East: the Keke, the Bhajya, the Bhoja, Kalinga <note>Ancient Odisha</note>, Vanga <note>Bengal</note>, the Upamadhya, Vidarbha, and the Vatsas. These lands are populated by those along the <term>Vindhyā</term> range.

The sovereign of these South-Eastern lands is the <term>Narādhipa</term> (Lord of Men). His umbrella is the **Umbrella of the Lord of Men**, which distinguishes all the Southern regions. Furthermore, in the area toward the South-East, there is the Kaushiki River. In the Eastern direction are the Tanges, the people of Dehalli <note>Likely an early reference to Delhi</note>, the Pancharatras, and those living toward the Lohitya <note>The Brahmaputra River</note>. These include Udayagiri, Bhadrashva, Purushottama <note>Puri in Odisha</note>, Kashi <note>Varanasi</note>, and the Mekala people. The sovereign of these is the <term>Gajādhipati</term> (Lord of Elephants).

Some scholars state that the <term>Hayādhipa</term> (Lord of Horses) rules the North-East; the <term>Narādhipa</term> (Lord of Men) rules the South-East; and the <term>Gajādhipati</term> (Lord of Elephants) rules the center. This is how the masters have explained it. Wherever a king may be situated, his title—whether of the Horse, the Elephant, or the Man—is determined by the marks and characteristics of his territory.

As it was said: "Regarding other kings..."—wherever a king is situated, in whatever field or territory he resides, his royal umbrella is fashioned according to the nature of that land.

-> ॥ 4 ॥ <-

<summary>This page maps the traditional Indian "three-fold sovereignty," dividing the Indian subcontinent into regions ruled by the Lord of Horses (North/West), the Lord of Men (South), and the Lord of Elephants (East), while citing Varahamihira.</summary>

<header>The Identification of the Spear and the Water-Pot 2</header>

# || Now, the knowledge of the nine-fold names of the constellations is told ||

The Chowrie, the Water-pot, and so on. In that context, the <term>Ashvinī</term> <note>The first lunar mansion/constellation in Hindu astrology</note> constellation is designated as the Royal Umbrella and the Chowrie. <term>Krittikā</term> is the Spear <note>original: "वील्लं" (Villam); likely a variant of 'bhalla' meaning spear or arrow-head</note>, and it is said that the constellation <unclear>Kunjamaga</unclear> is the climbing-ascent. <term>Punarvasu</term> is known as the Throne, and the Stake is also associated with Punarvasu. Thus the wise understand the **5** types; nine constellations create the nine distinct divisions. Similarly, for those starting with <term>Maga</term> <note>Maghā constellation</note>, the nine divisions from the Chowrie onwards should be understood in the Royal Umbrella, and for the nine starting with <term>Mūla</term>, the nine divisions of Chowrie etc. must be known. **5** || Now, the result of the eight-fold and six-fold transits of Saturn is told. Regarding the three constellations of the Umbrella: if Saturn <note>original: "सौरि" (Sauri); a name for Saturn as the son of the Sun</note> wanders through these three, then one should predict the "breaking of the Umbrella" <note>An astrological metaphor for the downfall of a king or the loss of sovereignty</note>. **6** || For the others, the results of the Water-pot and so on are told according to the movement of Saturn. For the Chowrie: **7** || For the Water-pot: **8** || For the Spear: **9** || For the three constellations: **10** || For the Throne: one Throne, the Umbrella.

<margin>Destruction of the seat/throne in that constellation</margin>
What is the meaning? One should abandon the seat/throne. **12** By the wheels etc., if Saturn is in the Stake constellation, there is the destruction of the Crown Prince; may he live if the King is the father and the son is the Crown Prince—so say the virtuous. In the ninth constellation, the Rope, if Saturn is there, there is binding; this occurs in the <unclear>Varachakra</unclear> **9**. If Saturn is in the Umbrella or other constellations, whatever the result is, it will surely happen if the constellation is afflicted. This is the instruction. **12** || Now, the specific details regarding Saturn's auspicious or inauspicious results are told. "Joined with a benefic": when Saturn is in the three constellations of the Umbrella, the "breaking of the umbrella" occurs. In the Chowrie, there is fierce wind, etc. Here, the result for the ninth constellation is given according to the direction. If it is joined by a benefic planet, it is utilized as such. "Accelerated motion" <note>original: "अतिचार" (Atichara); when a planet moves faster than its average speed into the next zodiac sign</note> refers to passing the normal duration of a sign's transit to enter the next sign—as the Sage Vashistha says, this is how the results of Saturn's constellations are calculated. Thus, when Saturn is in the constellation named Chowrie, it destroys... <note>The text here becomes a bit fragmented with abbreviations</note> there is no rain and suffering for the people. When Saturn is in the Water-pot, there is war and death, as mentioned. If that same Saturn is retrograde <note>original: "वक्री" (Vakri)</note>, then the results for the Chowrie etc. become extremely cruel. Or, if joined with malefic planets—the Sun, Mars, or Rahu—it gives the aforementioned results. If joined with Mercury, it gives auspicious results, because Mercury is considered a malefic only when joined with a malefic. **39**

<meta>This page continues the technical astrological instructions for predicting the downfall of rulers based on planetary transits through the three divisions of the lunar mansions (Nakshatras). It specifically addresses the "Breaking of the Umbrella," a metaphor for the loss of sovereignty or the death of a king.</meta>

Now, the specific results regarding the conflict-positions in the three groups of constellations are told, starting with the phrase **"Saturn, Rahu, and Mars."** Now, if the Lords of the regions are joined by Jupiter and the Moon, they cause the **breaking of the umbrella** <note>original: "छत्रभंगं" (chatrabhangam); a technical term in Jyotisha referring to the collapse of royal power or the death of a king</note>. Without Jupiter and the Moon, the umbrella of the other Lord is not destroyed. However, regarding those who are the Lords of the Regions situated in the constellations: if the "Eye" <note>An astrological sensitivity point</note> known as the *Akshapa* is situated in another Lord’s umbrella, specifically in a constellation that is afflicted, then a defect occurs there. **13**

Now, the author confirms the effect of the four malefic planets on the "Elephant Lord's" umbrella through Mercury and the Moon. If the four malefic planets are joined with Mercury and the Moon, it is said by the ancient sages to cause the destruction of the entire umbrella. Even through the order of writing, the "Elephant Lord’s" umbrella is determined for all directions. **14** Thus, concerning the four malefics: The destruction is stated from the beginning. One should combine the cycles starting with *Ashvinī*, starting with *Maghā*, and starting with *Mūla*. Here, if all are seen as afflicted: the Lord of the North, the Lord of the East, and the Lord of the South are joined according to the three divisions starting with *Ashvinī*. **15** Now, regarding the positions of the other kings...

<meta>catchword: Saturn (Shani)</meta>

---

> **When Saturn, Rahu, Mars, and the Sun are joined by Jupiter and the Moon, they cause the breaking of the umbrella of the King who is the Lord of the North. || 13 || The four malefic planets joined with Mercury and the Moon were said by ancient sages to cause the destruction of the Eastern umbrella. || 14 || In the same way, the four malefics joined with Mercury and the Moon are declared by the wise to cause the destruction of the Southern Lord's umbrella. || 15 || Thus, in other kingdoms, wherever such a conjunction occurs, one should predict the breaking of the umbrella due to the union with malefic planets. || 16 || Just as the malefic influences are cruel, so the benefics give auspicious results. Mercury is malefic if joined with a malefic, and auspicious if joined with an auspicious planet. || 17 || Just as Saturn produces malefic results, so Jupiter produces auspicious results. Mars, the Moon, Rahu, and the Sun—**
> ->**|| the Sun and Moon are equal in their results. || 18 ||**<-

---

Regarding other lands, as stated before, one should predict the **breaking of the umbrella**. In other kingdoms, wherever there is even a small portion of land, or in the case of kings facing loss of majesty or honor, or even regarding the king's son, one should predict the breaking of the umbrella as before. He gives the reason with the word **"Wherever."** "Wherever and for whomever there is a possibility"—this refers to the three groups of constellations. The "Lord of Horses" <term>Ashvapati</term>, "Lord of Elephants" <term>Gajapati</term>, and "Lord of Men" <term>Narapati</term>. The "possibility" of the three umbrellas refers to the birth constellation or the name constellation. The poet's intention is that whichever of the three umbrellas a constellation is situated in, that becomes the umbrella for that person.

What does this mean? If that constellation is in the "Lord of Horses" umbrella when "Saturn, Rahu, Mars, and the Sun are joined by Jupiter and the Moon," then there is a **breaking of the umbrella** for him. Alternatively, if the constellation is in the "Elephant Lord’s" umbrella when the four malefics are with Mercury and the Moon, then there is a **breaking of the umbrella** for him as well. Or, if the birth or name constellation is in the "Lord of Men" umbrella when the cycle of malefic planets is joined with Mercury and the Moon, then his **umbrella breaks** as well. In this way, if the kings of the constellations starting with *Ashvinī* are the masters of the "Elephant Lord's" umbrella region, then the union of the four malefics with Mercury and the Moon destroys them. One should calculate similarly for the group starting with *Mūla*. Therefore, the three umbrellas were explained for the sake of knowing the signs of all "illnesses" <note>metaphorical for political or natural calamities</note> of the state. **16** Now they state the power of auspicious results: **"Just as the malefic results..."** this is easy to understand. **17** He states the specific detail: **"Just as the malefic results..."** this is also easy to understand. **18**

It categorizes these omens across three traditional types of sovereignty: the Lord of Horses, the Lord of Elephants, and the Lord of Men.</summary>

|| 19 || 20 || It is situated in the kingdoms of the **Horse-Lord**, the **Elephant-Lord**, and the **Human-Lord** <note>These are traditional titles for kings categorized by their primary military strength or regional dominance</note>. The name of that constellation is determined by designations such as the "Whisk" <note>original: "chāmara"; a fly-whisk is a symbol of royalty</note> and so on. || 21 || Then, what should be done? Regarding that, whatever constellation is designated as the "Whisk"—for instance, if Saturn is in the Ashvini constellation in the kingdom of the **Horse-Lord**—then if the name of that king or his birth constellation is also Ashvini, those who know the sacred traditions <note>original: "āgamavidah"</note> say that his kingdom will suffer pain or distress. Here, if Saturn is in the Bharani constellation...

### Just as malefic planets cause harm, so do benefics bring good fortune.

A malefic planet in a malefic house produces malefic results; joined with a benefic, it becomes auspicious. || 19 || When benefics and malefics are situated together in the Ashvini division, the **breaking of the umbrella** <note>loss of sovereignty or death</note> will occur there due to the arrival of an enemy army. || 20 || Among other kings, wherever the three [constellations] are located, his name is generated based on the designation of the "Whisk" and so on. || 21 || Then, the specific result for the ruler of that other country should be declared. One should not declare the results regarding the three kings' umbrellas without such [specific names]. || 22 || If Rahu or Ketu <note>the north and south nodes of the moon, viewed as shadowy planets</note> are situated in the king's sensitive points <term>bhūpānām arta</term>, the breaking of his umbrella occurs through the **administration of poison** to the king. || 23 || The king should avoid hunting, bold adventures, travel, and riding unruly horses or elephants; he should also avoid conflict when a malefic planet is moving through the "Umbrella" [house]. || 24 || If the king performs the pacification rituals of the house in this way, according to what is stated in the **Yamala** <note>A category of ancient Tantric texts providing ritual instructions</note>, then there shall be peace for the ruler. || 25 ||

->Thus ends the "Umbrella Cycles" in the *Svarodaya* section of the *Narapati Jayacarya*. 25<-

---

For a king of a hostile country, if his birth constellation is Bharani, one who knows this should predict war. This is why it was said: "One should not declare the results regarding the three kings' umbrellas without such specific names." || 22 || Now, the author explains the result of the constellation cycle: <term>bhūpānām arta-ga</term> <note>referring to a planet entering a king's vulnerable or "afflicted" degree</note>. If the birth constellation of a king is situated in the "Staff of the Umbrella" <note>a specific structural part of the astrological diagram</note>, and Rahu or Ketu stays there and is not joined by a benefic planet, then one should say that the **breaking of the umbrella** for that king happens through **poisoning**. By "breaking of the umbrella," death is meant. || 23 || Regarding the king's proper conduct, the author says: "avoid hunting, bold adventures," etc. || 24 || And regarding "performing pacification rituals" || 25 ||

->Thus in the *Victory Guide of the Lord of Men* (*Narapati Jayacarya*)<-

<meta>Continuity: Following the discussion of the "Umbrella Cycle" (Chhatra Chakra) on the previous pages, which dealt with the physical safety and regime stability of the ruler, this page introduces the "Three-Throne Cycle" (Simhasana Chakra). While the previous text was heavily damaged and written in Sharada script, this section is preserved in Devanagari and focuses on the astrological timing for a king's coronation and his subsequent independence or dependence.</meta>

# **"Now, therefore, I shall fully declare..." || 1 ||**

**"The twenty-seven constellations..." || 2 ||** "In the northern part of Ashvini..." || 3 || **"The Throne is also to be known..." || 3 || "And in the others..." || 4 ||** **"By the five-fold <term>nadika</term> piercing..."** <note>A 'nadika' is a measure of time, approximately 24 minutes. In this context, 'piercing' (vedha) refers to an astrological aspect or intersection where a planet's position affects a specific point in the diagram.</note> Regarding the Throne-constellation and its separation, there is a piercing of the *nadikas*. The term "Group of Five" is the specific name for the five *nadikas*. When the "Throne" is constructed as a five-stepped square, one should place two constellations on each line of the steps for the purpose of calculating the "piercing" (*vedha*); thus, the layout of the piercing is established. There, the bottom step is designated as the **Foundation** (*Adhara*). Above that is the **Self**. Above that is the **Vehicle** (*Vahana*). Above that is the **Throne**. And at the very top is the **Throne**. <note>The repetition of 'Throne' emphasizes the pinnacle of the diagram.</note> Where the King sits, he acts like a lion. Although the "piercing" is not explicitly stated here, it is described because of the instructions for the layout. The Foundation <term>nadikas</term>...

---

> **Now, therefore, I shall fully declare the Cycle of the Three Thrones || By the mere knowledge of which, the King’s residence is established. || 1 ||**
> 
> **With the twenty-seven constellations, each [throne] is made of [nine] constellations || Starting with Ashvini, Magha, and Mula, divided by five <term>nadikas</term>. || 2 ||** <note>original: "Ashvini-Magha-Mula-aryam". The text organizes the 27 lunar mansions into three groups of nine, starting with these three specific constellations.</note>
> 
> **Ashvini is situated in the Northern part; Magha in the East; || Mula in the Southern part; and the other in the West. || 3 ||**
> 
> **In these and other kingdoms, one should speak of the crushing of the King || All of this—good or bad—depends on where Saturn is situated. || 4 ||** <note>Saturn (Shani) is historically viewed as the 'Great Taskmaster' or a planet of misfortune in Vedic astrology; its position determines the king's fate.</note>
> 
> **By the piercing of the five <term>nadikas</term>, each sits upon a seat || The Foundation, the Seat, the Fire [Vehicle], and likewise the Throne. || 5 ||**
> 
> **I shall describe the entirety of the Foundation and the rest for each one | Based on the piercing of the planets—auspicious or inauspicious—by gentle or cruel ones. || 6 ||** <note>'Gentle' (saumya) planets include Jupiter and Venus, while 'cruel' (krura) planets include Mars and Saturn.</note>
> 
> **When the King's 'Foundation' constellation is pierced by 'Fire' [a malefic] || His kingdom falls under the control of others; of this, there is no doubt. || 7 ||**

---

...where there is one of the ten "Serpents" <note>likely referring to a specific astrological division or yoga</note>, the constellation situated at the head of the Throne-constellation falls downward. A piercing in the Foundation direction... a piercing in the ninth from the middle... a piercing in the ninth from Mula is to be known by the "Fixed Day." || 5 || 

Now, **he declares the results regarding the directions of the Foundation and others. "The Foundation direction..." || 6 || "One's own Foundation..." ||** If a King's coronation (*abhisheka*) is performed on a day when the constellation designated as the "Foundation" is active, then the King consecrated at that time enjoys his kingdom only in a state of dependence on others. What does this mean? It means through service to others. || 7 || 

**He declares the result of the "Seat." "Of the Seat..." ||** If the King is consecrated on the "Seat" via either <term>Bharani</term> or <term>Pushya</term> constellations, and the "Five-fold Piercing Coronation" is performed, he becomes agitated. The "Principal Man" <note>likely the Prime Minister or Chief Advisor</note> or the remaining deities/fires will have their tasks destroyed. **"By peaceful speech..." || He protects the subjects with kind words. || 8 ||**

<summary>This page describes the construction and interpretation of the "Throne Cycle" (Simhasana Chakra), an astrological tool used to predict a king's success. It explains how a coronation timed to the "Foundation" step of the diagram leads to a puppet-king serving others, while the "Seat" step requires ruling through diplomacy and kind speech.</summary>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues the technical instructions for the Three-Throne Cycle (Simhasana Chakra). While the previous page established the physical layout of the "Throne" diagram (the Foundation, Self, Vehicle, and Throne steps), this page details the specific omens and outcomes (Phala) based on which section of the diagram the King’s birth star or the current Moon falls into during his coronation.</meta>

### **Now, he explains the results of the Fire-constellation. || 9 || [Starting with] "Fire, constellation..." || 9 || Now, he explains the results of the Lion-Throne-Navel-Movement constellation. || "Lion..." || 10 || "This Throne..." || 11 ||**

Now, he explains the manner in which the movement of the Throne's navel makes one radiant, a protector of the people, and a conqueror of all directions. **"At that time, in the 6th constellation..."** Regarding the **Coronation** <term>Rajabhisheka</term> <note>The ritual consecration of a monarch</note>, one should first determine the exact constellation of the Moon at that moment, then establish the "Seat" <note>Asana</note> and other relevant constellations. Having examined the "piercing" of the <term>nadikas</term> <note>Time-units of 24 minutes used here to calculate astrological interference</note>, the **Coronation** <term>Pattabhisheka</term> should be performed for the King at a time free from the affliction of malefic planets. **Now, regarding the one named "Foundation," if it is occupied by Saturn...** <note>The original text uses "Sori," a name for Saturn</note> these should generally be avoided. And so it is said in the *Vijayakalpataru* <note>A classical Sanskrit work on law and ritual</note>: Having excluded the "piercing" of the constellations and the lunar day...

> By being situated in the **Seat** constellation, a man becomes righteous. He becomes a leading figure among men, ruling over countries and bringing peace through his commands. || 8 ||
> When a King enters the throne while in the **Fire** <term>Vahni</term> constellation, he, like the king who stood there before him, protects the earth for a long time. || 9 ||
> If seated on the **Lion** constellation, the King becomes like a lion. He is forever fond of battle and always impossible for his ministers to manage. || 10 ||
> On the **Throne** constellation, he is radiant but of terrifying appearance. He is prone to sudden outbursts, angry, and a king who causes suffering to his subjects. || 11 ||
> At that time, when the Moon is in an unfavorable constellation and the malefic <term>nadikas</term> are pierced, if the King is placed on the throne during an auspicious state and an auspicious **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>... || 12 ||
> ...Then, sitting upon the throne in such a combination, he destroys the multitude of his enemies and brings the world under a **Single Umbrella**. <note>The "Single Umbrella" (Eka-chhatra) is a traditional Indian metaphor for undisputed universal sovereignty.</note> || 13 ||
> If he sits during the <term>nadika</term> of a malefic planet, imprisonment, loss of land, and injury to the ruler will surely occur. || 14 ||
> If Saturn is in the **Foundation** constellation, it grants defeat in war. Furthermore, disease, pain, and injury will arise. || 15 ||

When the Moon is at the Ascendant and situated in an exalted position, even if seen by the "Awakened Ones" <note>Likely referring to specific planetary aspects</note> along with the "Fire-dwellers"... Elsewhere, the King's own name-constellation in the **Fire** constellation... || 17 || **"The Lion-Throne..."** Regarding the other names... in the three Throne-constellations, there is one Lion-Throne, and such is the result... || 18 ||

### The Explanation of the Tortoise Diagram

Now I shall explain the **Tortoise Diagram** <note>original: "Kurma Chakra"; a symbolic representation of the earth as a tortoise used to map astrological influences onto specific directions and countries</note>. The statement "I will speak of the Tortoise Diagram" is easily understood. || **1** || Now, to describe the greatness of the Tortoise Diagram, the author speaks of the "Preservation of the Earth." This begins with the words "Upon whose..." || **1** ||
|| **2** || The phrase "Such mountains..." refers to verse || **3** ||. The phrase "By the tusk..." refers to verse || **4** ||. The phrase "The Lord in the month..." refers to verse || **5** ||. The phrase "Thus named the Awakened One..." refers to verse || **6** ||. The phrase "The body and the joints..." refers to verse || **7** ||. The phrase "By half of that..." refers to verse || **8** ||. The phrase "Regarding the eyes..." refers to verse || **9** ||.

---

I shall now describe the **Tortoise Diagram** as found in the *Scripture on Skill in War* <note>original: "Yuddhakoshala Agama"</note>. Merely by knowing this, one understands the cause of upheaval in a country. || **1** ||
Upon whose body are established the thirty-crore <note>A "crore" is ten million; thus, 300 million</note> gods. **Mount Sumeru** <note>The central world-mountain in Indian cosmology</note>, situated in the middle of the earth, rests upon your tusk. || **2** ||
The mountains, the oceans, the islands, and the **Elephants of the Directions** <note>Diggajas; the mythical elephants who support the world at the cardinal points</note> are all held by the Earth; I worship Him by whom that Earth is held. || **3** ||
Between the tusks and the ocean, the Earth is thus supported. From the digging of His mouth, the Earth shines like a mother's wisdom. || **4** ||
That Great-Bodied Lord is **Varaha** <note>The Boar avatar of Vishnu</note> resting upon the head of the serpent **Shesha** <note>The infinite cosmic serpent</note>. Upon his crest-jewel, the universe is situated like a tiny mosquito. || **5** ||
Such is the one known as the coiled one <note>Likely referring to the serpent Shesha supporting the tortoise</note> situated upon the ground. Upon the back of the **Tortoise**, the nine divisions of the world appear as distinct systems. || **6** ||
The joints of the body, the head, the tail, the claws, and the natural parts—I shall describe the measurements of that Tortoise with great care. || **7** ||
The Lord’s body extends for one hundred thousand **yojanas** <note>A yojana is an ancient unit of distance, roughly 8 to 9 miles</note>. Half of that measure constitutes the tail and the two sides of the belly. || **8** ||
The tongue is ten million units long; the head is seventy million. The distance between the eyes is measured as ten million. || **9** ||
<margin>The mouth</margin> is twenty million, and the feet are twice that. The measurement extends to ten thousand yojanas at the tips of the claws. || **10** ||
Thus is the measurement of the Tortoise described in the *Adi Vamala* <note>An ancient, likely Tantric, source text</note>. Upon him sits the Earth, adorned with the seven islands. || **11** ||
One should draw the diagram in the **shape of a tortoise**, complete with all its limbs. Its mouth should be in the **Eastern section**, and its tail in the **Western circle**. || **12** ||
Draw lines from East to West, and likewise from North to South. Draw diagonal lines from the Northeast to the Southwest, and from the Southeast to the Northwest. || **13** ||
The navel and the head are well-situated upon the back. One should contemplate the influence of the **Sun** as it falls upon these positions. || **14** ||

## The Earth-Tortoise has been fully described, spanning from the constellation Krittika to the end of Bharani. Next are the cycles of the country and the rest—the four-fold Tortoise system. || 37 ||
## These are the Tortoise-Cycles consisting of the Country <note>Deśakūrma</note>, City <note>Nagarakūrma</note>, Village <note>Grāmakūrma</note>, and House <note>Gṛhakūrma</note>. Drawing the cycle as before, one should show the names of the countries. ||
## In the Country-Tortoise, wherever Saturn <note>original: "Sori"</note> is situated, there will be destruction <note>original: "kshaya"</note>. || 38 || Thus ends the Country-Tortoise.
## In the City-Tortoise, after first placing the city's name, the wise man should write the layout. In the place where Saturn stays, it is harmful; in the place of "piercing" <note>vedha</note>, it is the same. || 39 ||
## Having drawn the Village-Tortoise and showing the names of the villages, wherever Saturn is located as before, one should predict a breach or ruin <note>bhanga</note> in the beginning of the village or its gate. || 40 || Thus ends the City-Village-Tortoise Cycle.
## In the Field-Tortoise, having placed the honors of the field and its measurements in the tortoise as they stand, destruction and great fear will arise in the place where Saturn is situated. || 41 || Thus ends the Field-Tortoise Cycle.
## Having drawn the House-Tortoise, situated at the mouth of the house-door, one must first determine the auspicious or inauspicious qualities based on the house's name. || 42 ||
## Saturn situated in the middle of the house causes grief and anguish. If he is found at the door, there is a defect; if in the kitchen <note>original: "pavake," the place of fire</note>, he brings fire. || 43 ||
## In the South-West, he grants death; in the South, he brings enemies and demons. In the West, he is the giver of auspiciousness; in the North-West, he brings emptiness or lack of profit. || 44 ||
## In the North, he is the giver of wealth and profit; in the North-East <note>original: "shambhave," the direction of Shiva</note>, he is the giver of all success. Saturn is harmful when associated with disease, but when his strength is small, he can be auspicious. || 45 ||

<meta>This page transitions from the completion of the Five-fold Tortoise (Kurmapanchaka) system into the description of two new astrological diagrams: the Lotus Cycle (Padma-chakra) and the Seven-continent "Lord of the Tribes" Cycle (Kuli-ishwara-chakra). These systems continue the theme of mapping celestial movements, particularly those of Saturn, onto terrestrial geography to predict regional disasters.</meta>

# || Now begins the Lotus Cycle ||

|| One should draw the Lotus Cycle in a five-fold shape ||
|| With beautiful petals; the ninth cell is the center of the cycle ||
|| It is created. || 1 || Place the lunar mansions <note>term: nakshatras</note> starting with Krittika, three ||
|| by three in sequence. From the center cell to the end... ||
|| one should place the twenty-seven <note>referring to the lunar mansions</note>. || 2 || By this, the earth with its nine divisions ||
|| is well-established in its sections. In whichever division Saturn <note>original: Sauri, "the son of the Sun"</note> is located, ||
|| that specific division is destroyed. || 3 || Bhadrashva, Kim-purusha, ||
|| and Shiva <note>likely referring to the Harivarsha region</note>, just so. In Meru <note>the sacred mountain at the center of the world</note>, the land of Bhadratila, the segment ||
|| is struck by the three. || 4 || Ramyaka, the "Land of Gold" <note>original: Hiranmaya</note>, and the Kuru ||
|| segment likewise. || Meru and the North, these are declared ||
|| in the pure directions as three. || 5 || The name of the one called Ketumala is the segment ||
|| in the West. In Bharata <note>India</note>, it is famous; in the East, the Winds <note>Maruts</note> are ||
|| situated. || 6 || Ilavrita is the same as the segment situated at Meru. ||
|| These are the nine divisions <note>term: Navakhanda; traditional Puranic geography</note> declared and spoken of in the *Adi-Yamala* <note>an ancient Tantric scripture</note>. || 7 ||
|| Thus ends the Lotus Cycle. ||

# || Now begins the Cycle of the Seven Continents ||

|| One should write the "Lord of the Tribes" <note>term: Kuli-ishwara</note> cycle in a serpent-shape, starting with Krittika. ||
|| It is joined with seven channels <note>term: nadi; astrological or energetic pathways</note>; one should observe the "piercing" <note>term: vedha; an astrological affliction</note> there. || 1 ||
|| Assign the conjunction of each channel to the Rose-Apple Continent <note>Jambu-dwipa</note> and the others. || The seven ||
|| continents follow in order; one should contemplate Saturn’s position there. || 2 || The Rose-Apple Continent, ||
|| then Shaka, Kusha, Krauncha, and Shalmali. Gomeda and Pushkara ||
|| are the continents; Saturn's play ends there. || 3 || <insert>end end</insert>

## ।। The Rahu-Kalanala Cycle ।।
<note>Literally: "The Cycle of the Fire of Death/Time of Rahu." This is a specialized astrological chart used to predict the success of journeys or the outcome of conflicts.</note>

Now, therefore, I shall fully explain that which was told by you in the *Yamala* <note>A class of Tantric scriptures; likely the *Brahma-Yamala* or *Rudra-Yamala*</note>. The "Fire of Time" Cycle of Rahu gives immediate results for one's travels. **1** || 

That which is spoken in the Science of Light <term>Jyotish Shastra: traditional Indian astrology and astronomy</term> regarding the alignment of planets; that which provides auspicious results in peace and success in battle. **2** || 

By the power of whose body the Moon and Sun <note>The "Sun and Moon" refers to the two luminaries eclipsed by Rahu</note> are affected; I shall speak of the power of that Rahu in the sequence of his limbs. **3** || 

Some, however, speak of him in this way: that Rahu is "causeless" <note>meaning he has no physical body or "lordship" over a day</note>, because no lordship is seen for him over any day or rising sign <term>Lagna: the ascendant sign</term>. **4** || 

Those planets that stand in lordship over the year, the month, and the day—Rahu is not among them; yet he is known as the King in the *Yamala* scriptures. **5** || 

In the Vedas, Agamas, and Puranas, Rahu is explained as a moving celestial body <term>Khechara: "sky-goer" or planet</term>. When he is present, the one who speaks of him as "unreal" or "false" is merely a babbler of lies. **6** || 

In all evil omens and in the calculation of birth horoscopes, Rahu gives immediate proof; therefore, it is wrong to call him "causeless." **7** || 

He who roams as the very Form of Time <note>Kalarupa</note> in the destruction of this world; in all planetary alignments, Rahu's alignment is the most certain. **8** || 

Having churned the middle of the planets, he stands at the forefront; I will describe his general destructive nature through the sequence of his head and his tail. **9** || 

He is known as Rahu, Darkness <note>Tamas</note>, the Fall <note>Pata</note>, Svarbhanu, and the Son of Simhika <note>A reference to the myth where the demon Rahu's mother is Simhika</note>. He is the Daitya's body, the severed head, and is famous among the kings of planets. **10** || 

In the tail is Ketu, the Crested One <note>Shikhi</note>, the source of all disease and distress; he is a portent, a bringer of power, the smoke-bodied one, and the Great Darkness. **11** || 

Thus, Rahu sustains himself, existing in every form. I shall now explain his cycle, which consists of the "Fire of Time." **12** || 

Draw a cycle of seven lines <term>Shalaka: stakes or lines used to create the grid of a chakra</term>. Start from the Northeast <note>Isha</note> with the constellation Krittika. One should draw the twenty-eight constellations, including Abhijit, in a counter-clockwise direction. **13** || 

The constellation where Rahu is situated is designated as the "mouth." From that mouth, the tenth constellation is where his middle is located. **14** || 

The number of Ketus <note>comets or secondary nodes</note> becomes one hundred and nineteen; they pervade the entire world, gathered around the Great Sun. **15** ||

---

"Thus he sustains..." **12** || Regarding the "seven lines," one should write them in order starting from the Northeast to the Southeast. **13** **14** || "One hundred..." **15** || Now he speaks of Rahu's two-fold nature: 

Rahu and Ketu... <note>The commentary becomes more technical here</note> This "Fire of Time" Rahu is established in the cycle of constellations. The rule is that from the starting point, whichever constellation he arrives at, that becomes the "Mouth Constellation" <note>Mukha-Nakshatra</note>...

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of the Rahu-Kalanala-Chakra (The Cycle of the Fire of Time). It specifically defines how the 27 or 28 lunar mansions (Nakshatras) are divided into two "sides" or zones: the Side of Life (Jiva-paksha) and the Side of Death (Mritya-paksha), determined by the current position of the shadow planet Rahu.</meta>

Five lunar mansions starting from **Revati** <note>The 27th and final lunar mansion</note> have been consumed (passed through); **Ashvini** <note>The 1st lunar mansion</note> and the subsequent ones are yet to be experienced. 

The current lunar mansion of **Rahu** is **Mrigashira** <note>The 5th lunar mansion</note>. Sixteen constellations constitute the **Side of Life** <term>Jiva-paksha: the auspicious zone of the chart</term>, while fourteen belong to **Ketu** <note>The "tail" of the celestial serpent</note>. That is to say, from the constellation occupied by Rahu, fifteen constellations comprise the **Side of Death** <term>Mritya-paksha: the inauspicious or "dead" zone</term>. 

When Rahu is in **Revati**, the **Side of Life** begins from the "foot" of **Ashvini** and extends to **Hasta**. Beyond that, starting from **Chitra**, where **Ketu** is located, the fifteenth constellation, up to **Swati** and others, thirteen constellations constitute the **Side of Death**. In this way, whichever constellation Rahu currently occupies, thirteen constellations from it are those yet to be experienced, and thus belong to that "side." Similarly, for the **Side of Death**, it begins where Rahu has entered; where **Ketu** resides, there are 7 constellations in the **Side of Life**. Including the intercalary constellation **Abhijit**, fourteen constellations are in the **Side of Life**, and fourteen are to be experienced in the **Side of Death**. || 26 || Thus, "His face is in the Side of Death..." || 27 ||

---

-> The constellations released by Rahu are thirteen in the **Side of Life**. <-
-> Thirteen are yet to be experienced; this is declared the **Side of Death**. || 26 || <-
-> His face is in the **Side of Death**; his back is in the **Side of Life**—the middle. <-
-> Thus these two, Rahu and Saturn, are the causes of the "Breath-Vibration" <note>original: "svara-karane," referring to the Svodaya system of divination based on breath and sound</note>. || 27 || <-
-> In the **Side of Life**, one should begin journeys; in the **Side of Death**, one should engage in battle. <-
-> At the proper time, the journey is auspicious; if reversed, it leads to great destruction. || 28 || <-
-> When the Moon and Sun are released and situated in the **Side of Life**, <-
-> Then victory and profit are attained at the time of travel—of this there is no doubt. || 29 || <-

---

### Regarding the Duties of the Two Sections
Now he speaks of what must be done in the two "sides." "In the Side of Life, journeys..." means if the **Moon** is moving through the **Side of Life** and the **Sun** is moving through the **Side of Death**, at that time one should undertake a **Victorious Journey** or a **Conquest of the Directions** <term>Digvijaya: a ritualized military campaign to establish universal sovereignty</term>. 

"If reversed" means a journey undertaken in the opposite conditions brings loss. What is the meaning? As long as the Moon moves through the fourteen constellations to be experienced by Rahu, and the Sun moves through the fourteen constellations of the **Side of Death**, at this time, journeys such as the **Conquest of the Directions** will lead to loss. || 28 ||

Now he explains the result of the journey if the Sun and Moon are positioned in a single "side." "The first two are the Side of Life..." If at the time of the journey the **Sun** and **Moon** are both traveling through the **Side of Life**, then there is victory and mental stability. At dawn, there is victory in battle, well-being, the acquisition of profit, and the attainment of the "Goddess of Victory." One obtains the King's assets such as chariots, elephants, horses, and wealth. || 29 ||

-> <term>Hrim</term> <note>A sacred seed-syllable (Bija Mantra) used to seal the text with divine energy</note> <-

<summary>This page explains how to divide the zodiac into the "Side of Life" and "Side of Death" based on Rahu's position, providing rules for when a king should embark on a conquest (Digvijaya) versus when he should fight. It concludes that success is guaranteed when the Sun and Moon both occupy the Side of Life.</summary>
<keywords>Rahu, Ketu, Jiva-paksha (Side of Life), Mritya-paksha (Side of Death), Digvijaya (Conquest), Journey (Yatra), Astrology, Nakshatra (Lunar Mansions)</keywords>

<header>5 Thus, the Five-fold Rahu 5</header>

<warning>The top of the page contains three lines heavily struck through with blue ink, making several words difficult to decipher.</warning>

<unclear>Now, regarding the results when the Sun and Moon are situated in the Step of Death <note>original: "Mritapada," an inauspicious zone in this astrological system</note> at the time of an event...</unclear>
<unclear>In the Step of Death, if during a King’s military expedition the Moon and Sun are both in the Step of Death,</unclear>
<unclear>then there is loss of wealth, elephants, and riches; in battle, there is fear from the enemy forces.</unclear>
<margin>47</margin>
This leads to a rout, flight from the battlefield, and even death **|| 20 ||**. Next, the text describes the excellence and success for journeys and other deeds when the Moon is in the Side of Life <term>Jiva-paksha: the auspicious half of the zodiac relative to Rahu's position</term> **|| 21 ||**.

Now, the division into five parts <note>original: "Panchanga-bheda"</note> is explained **|| 1 ||**. Regarding the "Time of Rahu": although Rahu is traditionally considered only a severed head <note>referring to the myth where Vishnu beheaded the demon Rahu</note>, here, for the sake of defining characteristics, he is envisioned as a complete body composed of the Hours (*Hora*) and Lunar Mansions (*Nakshatras*). This is the "Five-Limbed" designation. **|| 2 ||**. Regarding the "Limbs beginning with the Mouth": here, in the Five-Limbed Diagram of Rahu, we first address these five limbs and their results. The author of the *Vidhuttuda* <note>original: "Vidhuttuda," a name for Rahu meaning 'The Moon-Tormenter'</note> states:

-> When the Moon and Sun are situated in the Step of Death at that time, <-
-> then loss, fear, defeat, and death for the King occur. **|| 20 ||** When the Moon <-
-> is in the Side of Life, the work undertaken will be of the highest quality. <-
-> In the Step of Death, one should expect death... the strength and weakness... **|| 21 ||** <-
-> This is the Diagram of the Five-fold Rahu. <-

# || Now, the Five-Limbed Rahu ||

-> Now I shall explain the Rahu Diagram through its five-fold divisions. <-
-> The Mouth, the Belly, the Anus, the Tail, and the Skull as the fifth. **|| 1 ||** <-
-> Rahu is the mark of Time, the orb, the destroyer of the houses of the planets. <-
-> The smoky-tailed one is the village of planets; the serpent-goer is called Ketu. **|| 2 ||** <-
-> I shall speak the names of the limbs starting with the Mouth without dispute. <-
-> The "Trembler" and the "Striker of Horses" are the Five-Limbed Moon-Tormenter. **|| 3 ||** <-
-> I shall tell the names of the constellations where Rahu is situated. <-
-> "The Fallen," "The Scissors," and "The Tongue"—thus is the mouth in the eight-limbed [system]. **|| 4 ||** <-
-> ...those that have arrived... <-
-> those thirteen which have been consumed; the Belly, the Casket, the Soul, the Limb of Desire, the Stomach, and the Plow. **|| 5 ||** Thus. <-

**|| Second Explanation ||** This refers to the Rahu of this system. In the five-fold division, the constellations are divided separately into five parts to understand the relationship between the Moon and Sun <note>original: "Abhrantara-kula-vijnanam"</note> **|| 3 ||**. The limbs are stated separately. "In which constellation [Rahu] is situated": the names of the constellations occupied by Rahu are "The Fallen," "The Scissors," and so on **|| 4 ||**.

He then describes the division of the eight-limbed constellations beginning with the mouth: "Those that have arrived, the thirteen lunar mansions consumed." The names are the Belly, the Casket, the Soul, the Limb of Desire, the Stomach, and the Plow. These are the thirteen lunar mansions located ahead of the constellation currently occupied by Rahu. These are the "Tongue-Sides," and their names are "Belly-designation," "Casket-designation," "Soul-designation," "Limb of Desire," "Stomach," and "Plow" **|| 5 ||**.

It transition from general auspicious zones to a specific anatomical mapping where different stars represent Rahu's mouth, belly, and tail.</summary>

These fifteen names, starting from the <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions or sectors of the sky through which the moon passes</note> where Rahu is currently positioned—such as the "anus," the "blazing," and so forth—are as follows: || 6 ||

Thus ends the third section. <gloss>Now, the "four actions" are easy to understand. || 7 ||</gloss> The remaining seven are mentioned here. || 8 || <gloss>Now, he speaks in order to examine the family of stars.</gloss>

Regarding the "constant form": From the Sun's asterism, the curved path's constant form occurs when it stays in a single asterism for thirteen or fourteen days. This is our position. For the Moon, the stay in a single asterism is for <term>twenty-seven</term> <note>original: "sapta-vimsatyai," referring to the 27 divisions of the zodiac</note> parts of a day. > In the asterisms of the belly, which are the fifteen from the face; the "anus," the "blazing"—these are the "shears" starting from the face. || 6 || Thus ends the third. <gloss>From the portion of the anus,</gloss> whatever those numbers are. In the sixth or seventh, they should be avoided; and in the eighth, only there. || 7 || The remaining seven measurements are in the middle positions of the mouth and the tail; in the "bowl," the "dirty," the head, the Moon’s limb, and the circle of enjoyment. || 8 || <gloss>Situated in a constant form,</gloss> if the Sun and Moon are in a constant form. They speak of the five-fold combination of these yogas, one by one, in sequence. || 9 || When the Sun is situated in the curve of Rahu and the Moon is situated in the limb of the Moon, the <term>invader</term> <note>original: "Yayi," the king or army attacking</note> achieves victory there, while the <term>defender</term> <note>original: "Sthayi," the king or army being attacked</note> suffers a break in battle. || 10 || When the Sun is situated in the Moon's limb and the Moon is resting in the "anus," the one who marches forward is victorious in war, while the [opposing] force meets destruction. || 11 || When the Sun is in the "blazing limb" and the Moon is in the "curved limb," if there are two forces, the defender wins the battle. || 12 || When the Sun and Moon are in Rahu’s asterism and the Moon's limb during battle, one should predict victory for the defender in war and utter madness or defeat for the invader. || 13 || When both the Sun and Moon are situated in the curve and limb of Rahu, there is an uprising of both armies; there is doubt, but ultimately the invader wins. || 14

By this, "the current asterism of Rahu" and the "anus asterism" (the fifteenth from Rahu’s current asterism) are easily understood. || 11 || "In the Sun," etc. By "the curved limb," he describes what kind of asterism it is from Rahu’s current position. || 12 || "In Rahu’s asterism," etc. By "the Moon's limb," he means the asterism behind Rahu’s current position. || 13 || "When both become so," etc. || 14 ||
