<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of military astrology (Sangrama Jyotisha) found on the previous page. It details the specific planetary combinations (Yogas) that predict success or failure for the 'Sthayi' (the defender or stationary party) and the 'Yayi' (the attacker or traveling party). The text maps the Sun and Moon onto a metaphorical body of Rahu, the shadow planet, to determine these outcomes.</meta>

The Sun is the Lord of Life. || 15 || Regarding the phrase "When the Sun is in the belly": <term>Vajranga</term> <note>Vajranga: literally "thunderbolt-limb" or "diamond-body," a specific technical position in this astrological system</note> refers to the current <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions or star sectors used in Vedic astrology</note> of Rahu. || 16 || Regarding "Passion-intoxicated": the current Nakshatra of Rahu and the fifth Nakshatra thereafter is like a "crown." || 17 || Regarding "In the Casket": By "Casket," it is easy to understand the tongue of the Lord of Life as the Nakshatra touched by Rahu. || 18 || Regarding "When the Sun is in the belly": By "Belly," the Lord of Life is indicated; it refers to the Sun and Moon being in the position of the Lord of Life. || 19 || Thus ends the five-fold combination of the belly and other parts. The "Punti" combination: "When the Sun is in the Moon's [limb]" means that from the Moon's limb, the fifteenth Nakshatra is the Sun's limb. || 20 || Regarding "The Anus": [This describes] the position... || 21 || "The Sun situated in the Anus"... || 22 ||

---

When the Sun (Savitā) is in the Lord of Life position and the Moon (Nishanatha) is situated in the Anus, the defeat of the **Defender** is declared, and the **Attacker** is always victorious. || 15 ||

When the Sun (Bhānu) is in the belly and the Moon (Shashānka) occupies the Thunderbolt-limb, having struck the Lord of Life, the **Defender** is defeated and the **Attacker** triumphs in battle. || 16 ||

When the Sun (Divānātha) is in the "Passion-intoxicated" position and the Moon (Nishākara) is in the head, the **Defender** achieves victory there, and the **Attacker** gains a complete triumph <note>This likely implies a nuanced result where both sides may claim partial success, or a specific tactical advantage occurs for both under different conditions</note>. || 17 ||

When the Sun (Āditya) is in the Casket and the Moon is situated on the tongue, the **Defender** meets with defeat, and the **Attacker** is somewhat victorious in battle. || 18 ||

When the Sun is in the belly and [the Moon] is situated in the Pundra-lagna <note>Pundra-lagna: a specific auspicious or marked point in the zodiac</note>, even the gods could not achieve victory [for the defender]; what then can be said for a mere human army? || 19 ||

When the Sun is in the "Complete Limb" and the Moon is situated in the Sun's limb, an equal battle shall occur, but the **Newcomer** (Attacker) will be slightly victorious. || 20 ||

When the Sun is in the Anus... and the Moon is in the Skull, the **Attacker** gains control, while the **Defender** remains without victory. || 21 ||

When the Sun is situated in the Anus and the Moon is situated in Rahu's [position], then the battle at the house will be terrible; how wonderful that the **Attacker** shall be the victor! || 22 ||

When the Sun is in the Sun’s Nakshatra and the Moon is in the eyes, it brings death to the **Attacker** and the gain of victory to the one who is **Stationary** (Defender). || 23 ||

When the Sun (Sahasrānshu) is in the Sun’s limb and the Moon is situated on the tongue, it grants victory to the **Attacker** and ordains the defeat of the **Defender**. || 24 ||

---

Thus ends the five-fold section beginning with the Anus. Regarding "Standing in the Sun's Nakshatra": starting from the Nakshatra where Rahu is situated, the 5 Nakshatras ahead are the Sun's Nakshatras; 22 Nakshatras are known as "Sun-limbs," and 7 Nakshatras are known as "Belly." In this combination, if the Sun is in its power and the Moon is in the 18th Nakshatra, the **Attacker** is able to conquer. The **Attacker** wins, or else the **Defender** meets with death. Victory and gain come to the victor; there is triumph in the great battle. From this, one gains world-renown or, in battle, gains such as gold, horses, swords, and weaponry. || 23 || Regarding "In the Sun's limb": In the Sun's limb, the "Tongue" is the current Nakshatra of Rahu; the rest is easy to understand. || 24 ||

It concludes with instructions on calculating these positions and the material rewards of victory, such as gold and weapons.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the *Narapati Jayacharya Vidodaya*, a major medieval Indian treatise on military astrology and omens. It completes the descriptions of the "Vajranga" (Thunderbolt-limb) alignments and begins the "Kalanal" (Fire of Time) of Rahu’s limbs, focusing on predicting the outcome between an attacker and a defender based on lunar mansions.</meta>

Regarding the **Back-cycle** (<term>Prishtha-chakra</term> <note>A technical diagram mapping the 27 lunar mansions (Nakshatras) onto the "back" of the cosmic shadow-planet Rahu</note>), the six parts of the back are considered. Starting from the <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>Nakshatra: One of the 27 sectors of the ecliptic or lunar mansions used in Indian astrology</note> occupied by Rahu and ending with the **Life-limb** (<term>Jivanga</term>), the Moon is situated at 2 and Mercury at 13. When this <term>Yoga</term> <note>Yoga: A specific planetary combination or alignment</note> occurs in the midst of **Battle** (<term>Sangrama</term>), whichever side possesses their own strength will achieve victory over Rahu; that is to say, they shall attain a great victory. || 25 || Regarding "The Sun in Rahu's sixth": By knowing the Nakshatra of Rahu and counting respectively to the serpent-limb or the anus-nakshatra, which is the fifteenth constellation; at the time of this **alignment**, a war occurs where the **Attacker** (<term>Yayi</term> <note>Yayi: The king or army that is on the move or attacking</note>) achieves a slight victory. This is the command. || 26 || Regarding "The Sun and Moon": wherever they are, if they are in the **Thunderbolt-limb** (<term>Vajranga</term> <note>Vajranga: A technical term for a specific limb in the Rahu-diagram, literally 'diamond' or 'thunderbolt' limb</note>), then the Sun and Moon are situated together. In this **alignment**, both armies shall face destruction. || 27 || Thus ends the description of the third alignment within the five-fold **Thunderbolt-limb**. || 3 || Regarding the **Upper-limb** (<term>Urdhvanga</term>):

---

When the Sun (<term>Bhaskara</term>) is in the **Back-cycle** and the Moon is situated in the **Life-limb**, the **Attacker** triumphs there, even if the enemy is of a powerful nature. || 28 || When the Sun is in Rahu's sixth position—the Mouth-limb—and the Moon occupies the Anus, a balanced battle occurs, though the **Attacker** shall be slightly victorious. || 29 || When the Sun and Moon are both situated in the **Thunderbolt-limb**, the destruction of both armies will certainly occur there. || 30 || When the Sun is in the **Upper-limb** and the Moon goes to Rahu's **Tongue-limb**, the **Defender** (<term>Sthayi</term> <note>Sthayi: The stationary party or the one being attacked/defending their territory</note>) wins, and the Attacker suffers a break in formation. || 31 || When the Sun is situated in the **Skull** (<term>Kapala</term>) and the Moon is in the **Casket** (<term>Samputa</term>), the enemy is conquered by the **Attacker** with ease and not otherwise. || 32 || When the Lord of Day (the Sun) is in the Head and the Lord of Night (the Moon) is in the Heart, the **Attacker** is victorious in battle and the **Defender** suffers defeat. || 33 || When the Sun is in **Rahu's Head** and the Moon is in the Eyes, a terrible and fierce conflict arises between the two kings. || 34 ||

---

<margin>G</margin> This section explains the twenty-two constellations beginning from the Nakshatra occupied by Rahu. The **Tongue-limb** is the Nakshatra of Rahu; the remainder, the "Deer-head" (<term>Mriga-bha</term>), indicates fleeing from the **Battle**. || 28 || Regarding "Situated in the Skull": the **Upper-limb** is itself the Skull, while the "Casket" refers to the side of Life. || 29 || "Situated in the Head": the **Upper-limb** is the head, and the Anus is the fifteenth constellation. || 30 || Regarding "**Rahu's Head**": The Sun is in the eyes, which means the eighth constellation from the sixteenth Nakshatra relative to Rahu. || 31 || When the **Upper-limb** occurs... || 32 ||

### When the Sun and Moon are both joined in the Upper-limb,
### Both armies then go quickly to the House of Yama. <note>original: "Yamamandiram" — The House of Yama is the realm of the dead; this signifies total slaughter of both sides.</note> || 32 ||

## || Thus ends the Rahu-Kalanal of the Limbs ||

# Thus ends the Five-limbed Rahu-Kalanal <note>Kalanal: "The Fire of Time" or "Destructive Fire," a technical term for a particularly volatile astrological chart</note> in the "Goddess of Victory" commentary of the *Narapati Jayacharya Vidodaya*. || 1 ||

It concludes with an ominous warning that if both luminaries occupy the upper limb, both armies will face total destruction.</summary>
